Finite-Window Local-to-Clean Transfer and Anti-Phantom Detection for Sharp Navier–Stokes Packages
Runlong Yu

Abstract
We prove a fixed finite-window structural theorem for sharp localized Navier–Stokes packages, formulated as both a local-to-clean detection theorem and an anti-phantom principle. The result addresses whether a defect visible in the baseline quotient geometry can disappear after pressure-tail enrichment, residual transfer, quotienting, and clean-to-local detector comparison. Under synchronized representatives, baseline-to-tail visibility, component comparison, residual-ledger closure, detector comparison, chart visibility, and a clean quotient gap, any baseline-visible defect is either detected by the localized detector or charged to an explicit quotient-residual ledger. Quantitatively, M Λ loc ​ ( 𝒟 − 𝜻 ∗ ) ≥ c Λ , 0 , dist loc , int , 0 ⁡ ( 𝒟 , Γ int ∗ Λ , adm ) − 𝔈 quot ∗ Λ , 0 ​ ( 𝒟 ) . M_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathcal{D}-\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{*})\geq c_{\Lambda,0},\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},0}(\mathcal{D},\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}*{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}})-\mathfrak{E}^{\mathrm{quot}}*{\Lambda,0}(\mathcal{D}). The proof assembles three modules: pressure-tail visibility, componentwise residual-ledger closure, and detector comparison. The anti-phantom interpretation is that a baseline-visible defect cannot be simultaneously detector-silent and residual-cheap. We also record provenance for the imported quotient interface, finite-dimensional pressure-tail models, explicit matrix realizations of the structural inputs, NS-generated coordinate realizability, compactness criteria for clean pressure images, and reduced pressure/tax kernel-free criteria.



Keywords.

Navier–Stokes equations; suitable weak solutions; finite-window packages; pressure decomposition; pressure tails; quotient geometry; residual ledger; detector comparison; local-to-clean transfer; anti-phantom detection; partial regularity.

2020 Mathematics Subject Classification.

35Q30, 35B65, 35B45, 76D05.

1
Problem, anti-phantom principle, and organization

The purpose of this paper is to make precise one fixed finite-window step in a broader audit program for the three-dimensional incompressible Navier–Stokes equations
[
24
,
25
,
26
,
27
,
28
]

\partial_{t}u-\Delta u+u\cdot\nabla u+\nabla p=0,\qquad\nabla\cdot u=0.

The weak-solution background goes back to Leray and Hopf, while the local partial-regularity endpoint used as orientation here comes from Scheffer and Caffarelli–Kohn–Nirenberg, together with later refinements and expositions
[
1
,
2
,
3
,
4
,
5
,
7
,
15
]
. The pressure splitting and pressure-tail bookkeeping below are aligned with the standard local Calderon–Zygmund and harmonic-pressure viewpoint
[
6
,
11
,
15
]
, and the residual/flux terminology is compatible with the coarse-grained local energy-transfer literature
[
8
,
9
,
10
]
.

The program starts from a simple obstruction. Localized data, clean pressure-natural data, pressure-tail coordinates, component residuals, and detector channels do not naturally live in the same representation. A defect may be visible in an older baseline quotient geometry but appear to disappear after localization, pressure splitting, projection, quotienting, or detector comparison.

The central question is therefore the following.

\boxed{\text{Can a baseline-visible finite-window defect be both detector-silent and residual-cheap?}}

The theorem proved here gives a conditional finite-window answer:

\boxed{\text{No, not once all named pressure-tail, residual, chart, detector, and synchronization costs are paid.}}

Relative to the earlier manuscripts in this sequence, the present paper isolates the fixed-window local-to-clean step. The defect-cascade formulation identifies the moving-window invisible obstruction
[
25
]
; the supply–tax ledger records the finite-scale payment alternative
[
26
]
; the singularity-audit transfer manuscript organizes the first local-to-clean residual budget
[
27
]
; and the computational anti-phantom paper proves the clean finite-window quotient gap and enhanced-tail transfer toolkit
[
28
]
. The one-component precursors in this sequence are
[
22
,
23
,
24
]
, with
[
24
]
supplying the closest one-component Schur-visibility analogue of the anti-phantom terminology.

Informally, a finite-window phantom defect is a package
\mathcal{D}
for which

\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},0}(\mathcal{D},\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}})>0

but

M_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathcal{D}-\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{*})=0.

The anti-phantom principle says that such silence cannot be free. The precise theorem gives

M_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathcal{D}-\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{*})\geq c_{\Lambda,0}\,\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},0}(\mathcal{D},\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}})-\mathfrak{E}^{\mathrm{quot}}_{\Lambda,0}(\mathcal{D}),

where
\mathfrak{E}^{\mathrm{quot}}_{\Lambda,0}
is the assembled finite-window quotient error ledger. Hence either the localized detector sees a fixed fraction of the baseline defect, or the quotient-residual ledger pays a comparable cost.

The paper has three layers.

(i)

Abstract structural closure.
The main theorem assembles pressure-tail visibility, residual-ledger closure, detector comparison, chart visibility, component-to-baseline comparison, and a clean quotient gap.

(ii)

Model realization.
A finite-dimensional pressure-tail quotient model shows that the structural assumptions are mutually consistent and non-vacuous in a reduced finite-window setting.

(iii)

NS-generated interface.
Local Navier–Stokes data are shown to generate the basic package coordinates, while compactness and kernel-free inputs are isolated as separate verification problems.

This is not a Navier–Stokes regularity theorem. It is adjacent to, but distinct from, one-component and anisotropic regularity criteria
[
13
,
14
,
16
,
17
,
18
,
20
]
, critical-space/backward-uniqueness methods
[
12
]
, and recent quantitative regularity or concentration approaches
[
19
,
21
]
. It does not prove that arbitrary suitable weak solutions satisfy the structural hypotheses. It proves that, once the listed fixed-window structural inputs are verified on a chosen package class, a baseline-visible defect cannot vanish from the localized detector without appearing in the explicit error ledger.

0.1 Theorem status table

The theorem is a finite-window structural transfer result, not a proof of global Navier–Stokes regularity. The status of the core inputs is as follows.

Item

Status

Pressure-tail visibility

Proved here as a finite-window closure theorem under explicit baseline-visibility, finite-amplitude, same-gauge, projection-tail, and harmonic-tail hypotheses; the synchronized quotient interface is imported through the provenance contract.

Residual ledger closure

Proved here; see the main residual-ledger module and the componentwise closure theorem.

Detector comparison

Proved under structural detector-intertwining input and channelwise comparison hypotheses.

Clean anti-phantom gap

Structural assumption / criterion; verified in reduced finite-dimensional models and left as a concrete package-class input in the main theorem.

NS-generated package realization

Constructed interface: local Navier–Stokes data generate the package coordinates, but this does not verify every structural detector or clean-gap hypothesis.

Scale-uniform propagation

Not proved. All constants and closures are fixed finite-window constants.

Singularity extraction

Future work. No singularity exclusion, infinite-chain theorem, or Clay-problem conclusion is claimed.

The main text states the modules and proves the assembly. The appendices contain the detailed pressure-tail, residual-ledger, detector-comparison, package-realizability, compactness, and reduced kernel-free proofs.

2
Finite-window setup and quotient geometries

We work in a fixed normalized local geometry. Space-time cylinders, cutoff functions, harmonic-tail spaces, pressure-source spaces, and detector channels are fixed once and for all. The constants in this paper may depend on this finite list of choices. This convention is not cosmetic: it is what separates the finite-window theorem from any scale-uniform or infinite-chain claim.

A finite-window package is denoted by
\mathcal{D}
. It collects the coordinates needed by the local-to-clean audit: localized velocity and pressure data, clean pressure-source coordinates, harmonic pressure coordinates, component residual channels, gate/slack variables, and detector coordinates. The admissible or model class is denoted by
\Gamma
, with decorations indicating the geometry in which it is viewed. A representative of the admissible class is denoted by
\zeta
. A finite chain of representatives is denoted by

\boldsymbol{\zeta}=(\zeta_{0},\ldots,\zeta_{K}).

The same-chain convention means that all residual channels are evaluated against one compatible choice of representatives. This prevents a common error in quotient estimates: optimizing pressure, localization, reproduction, and gate/slack errors against different gauges and then pretending that the estimates hold simultaneously.

Baseline distance

The older baseline geometry is the coarsest geometry used in the final detection statement. It is measured by

\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},0}(\mathcal{D},\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}).

This distance is intentionally old-fashioned: it does not directly include every pressure-tail coordinate or every component residual coordinate. The final theorem is stronger when written in this baseline geometry, because it says that the localized detector sees a defect that was already visible in the original quotient language.

Pressure-tail distance

The pressure-tail geometry refines the baseline geometry by adding clean Calderon–Zygmund pressure coordinates and harmonic pressure-tail coordinates. Schematically, it has the form

\|D\|_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},\mathrm{tail}}=\|D\|_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},0}+\|R_{i}R_{j}F^{\mathrm{cl}}_{D,ij}\|_{Y_{\mathrm{prs}}}+\|p_{\mathrm{harm},D}\|_{Y_{\mathrm{harm}}}.

The corresponding sharp tail distance also includes projection-tail and harmonic-tail penalties:

\displaystyle\operatorname{dist}^{\sharp,\mathrm{tail}}_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},\mathrm{tail}}(D,\Gamma):=\inf_{\zeta\in\Gamma}\bigl(

\displaystyle\|D-\zeta\|_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},\mathrm{tail}}+\alpha_{\mathrm{proj}}T_{\mathrm{proj}}(D;\zeta)

\displaystyle+\alpha_{\mathrm{harm}}T_{\mathrm{harm}}(D;\zeta)\bigr).

The pressure-tail module proves that, under baseline visibility and finite-tail approximation, this enhanced distance is controlled by the baseline distance plus explicit errors.

Component distance

The component geometry is the geometry in which the residual ledger closes. It is written as

\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{comp}}^{\sharp,[0,K]}(\mathcal{D},\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{comp}}).

This distance is not merely a pressure distance. It contains the component coordinates needed to control pressure-source residuals, localization leakage, reproduction drift, and gate/slack violations across a finite chain. Its role is intermediate: detector comparison naturally loses a component residual, and the residual-ledger theorem converts that residual into this component distance plus a finite-chain near-minimizer error.

Detectors and charts

The localized detector is denoted by
M_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{loc}}
. It is the detector evaluated on the localized package after subtraction of the selected same-chain representative. The clean or component detector is denoted by
M_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{comp}}
. The local-to-clean chart is

\Theta_{\Lambda}.

The detector-comparison theorem is a stability estimate of the form

M_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathcal{D}-\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{*})\geq M_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{comp}}(\Theta_{\Lambda}(\mathcal{D}-\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{*}))-C_{\mathrm{dc}}\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{comp}}^{[0,K]}(\mathcal{D};\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{*})-\Delta_{\mathrm{dc}}.

This is the bridge from the clean model back to the localized detector.

Imported quotient synchronization

The final version uses the full finite-window quotient geometry developed in the earlier package manuscripts
[
24
,
25
,
26
,
27
,
28
]
and instantiated through the structural contract in
Appendix

A
. We do not introduce a simplified quotient convention in this paper. Instead, we import the quotient geometry through a synchronized representative contract with explicit provenance:
Table

2
records the source and status of each imported object, while
Section

2
fixes the synchronization loss. For every package under consideration, a single admissible representative

\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{*}=\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{*}(\mathcal{D})

is selected and used simultaneously for the baseline distance, pressure-tail excess, component distance, residual ledger, local-to-clean chart, and detector comparison. If the imported quotient geometry gives exact synchronization, the synchronization loss is zero. Otherwise the loss is recorded as

\Delta_{\mathrm{sync}}(\mathcal{D})\geq 0

and is included in the final quotient error ledger. In particular, the proof never minimizes the pressure tail, residual ledger, and detector mismatch over three different gauges and then combines the estimates as if they held on one representative.

Definition 2.1

(Synchronization loss contract)
.

For a chosen synchronized representative
\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{*}(\mathcal{D})
, define

\Delta_{\mathrm{sync}}(\mathcal{D};\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{*}):=\delta_{0}(\mathcal{D};\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{*})+\delta_{\mathrm{tail}}(\mathcal{D};\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{*})+\delta_{\mathrm{comp}}(\mathcal{D};\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{*})+\delta_{\mathrm{chart}}(\mathcal{D};\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{*})+\delta_{\mathrm{det}}(\mathcal{D};\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{*}),

where

\delta_{0}(\mathcal{D};\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{*}):=\Bigl(\left\|\mathcal{D}-\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{*}\right\|_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},0}-\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},0}(\mathcal{D},\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}})\Bigr)_{+},

\delta_{\mathrm{tail}}(\mathcal{D};\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{*}):=\Bigl(\left\|\mathcal{D}-\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{*}\right\|_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},\mathrm{tail}}-\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},\mathrm{tail}}(\mathcal{D},\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}})\Bigr)_{+},

\delta_{\mathrm{comp}}(\mathcal{D};\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{*}):=\Bigl(|\mathcal{D}-\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{*}|_{\mathrm{comp}}^{\sharp,[0,K]}-\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{comp}}^{\sharp,[0,K]}(\mathcal{D},\Gamma^{\mathrm{comp}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}})\Bigr)_{+}.

The terms
\delta_{\mathrm{chart}}
and
\delta_{\mathrm{det}}
are the positive parts of any failure of the chart-visibility and detector-comparison estimates to hold on the same representative. In the exact synchronized case all five defects vanish. In the fixed finite-window theorems below, these representative-selection costs are either already included in the displayed module errors or are inserted into the final quotient ledger through
C_{\mathrm{sync}}\Delta_{\mathrm{sync}}
.

Error ledgers

All errors in the paper are finite-window errors. The final error
\mathfrak{E}_{\Lambda,0}
is assembled from the following types of terms:

\begin{array}[]{ll}\text{projection-tail errors}&\Delta_{\mathrm{proj},N},\ \Delta^{\mathrm{unif}}_{\mathrm{proj},N}(\mathcal{A}_{\Lambda}),\\[1.99997pt]
\text{harmonic-tail errors}&\Delta_{\mathrm{harm},M},\ \Delta^{(3/2)}_{\mathrm{harm},M},\\[1.99997pt]
\text{visibility and split errors}&\Delta_{\mathrm{tail}/0},\ \delta_{0},\\[1.99997pt]
\text{component near-minimizer errors}&\delta_{\mathrm{comp}}^{[0,K]},\\[1.99997pt]
\text{detector-intertwining errors}&\Delta_{\mathrm{dc}},\\[1.99997pt]
\text{chart/component comparison errors}&\Delta_{\mathrm{chart}},\ \Delta_{\mathrm{comp}/0}.\end{array}

The exact combination is not universal; it depends on the selected finite-window model and on which sufficient criteria are used. What is essential is that every term is explicit and appears with a finite-window constant.

3
Structural hypotheses and their status

This section states the hypotheses used in the main theorem in a level of detail appropriate for the body of the paper. The appendices prove the estimates that justify these hypotheses in the finite-window framework and record sufficient criteria for several of them.

Assumption 3.1

(Pressure-tail baseline visibility)
.

The pressure-natural tail geometry is visible from the older baseline geometry. More precisely, for the selected finite-window class there are constants and explicit errors such that

\displaystyle\operatorname{dist}^{\sharp,\mathrm{tail}}_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},\mathrm{tail}}(\mathcal{D},\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}})

\displaystyle\leq C_{\mathrm{tail},0}\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},0}(\mathcal{D},\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}})

(3.1)

\displaystyle+\mathfrak{E}_{\mathrm{tail},0}(\mathcal{D}).

Here
\mathfrak{E}_{\mathrm{tail},0}
consists of projection-tail, harmonic-tail, finite-amplitude, and visibility errors.

Assumption 3.2

(Componentwise residual-ledger closure)
.

For a same-chain representative
\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{*}
, the component residual satisfies

\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{comp}}^{[0,K]}(\mathcal{D};\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{*})\leq C_{\mathrm{comp}}^{[0,K]}(M_{U})\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{comp}}^{\sharp,[0,K]}(\mathcal{D},\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{comp}})+C_{\mathrm{comp}}^{[0,K]}(M_{U})\delta_{\mathrm{comp}}^{[0,K]}.

(3.2)

The residual
\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{comp}}^{[0,K]}
includes pressure-source, localization leakage, reproduction drift, and gate/slack channels.

Assumption 3.3

(Detector comparison)
.

The localized and clean detectors are intertwined up to the component residual:

\displaystyle M_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathcal{D}-\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{*})

\displaystyle\geq M_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{comp}}(\Theta_{\Lambda}(\mathcal{D}-\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{*}))-C_{\mathrm{dc}}\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{comp}}^{[0,K]}(\mathcal{D};\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{*})-\Delta_{\mathrm{dc}}.

(3.3)

Assumption 3.4

(Clean anti-phantom gap)
.

There is a positive clean coefficient
\mu_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{comp}}>0
such that the clean detector controls the clean defect on the selected clean quotient:

M_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{comp}}(\Theta_{\Lambda}(\mathcal{D}-\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{*}))\geq\mu_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{comp}}\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{cl}}(\Theta_{\Lambda}\mathcal{D},\Gamma_{\mathrm{cl},\mathrm{adm}})-\Delta_{\mathrm{cl}}.

(3.4)

This hypothesis rules out a clean nonzero defect that lies in the zero set of the clean detector.

Assumption 3.5

(Chart visibility)
.

The local-to-clean chart sees the baseline defect: for some
\lambda_{G}>0
,

\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{cl}}(\Theta_{\Lambda}\mathcal{D},\Gamma_{\mathrm{cl},\mathrm{adm}})\geq\lambda_{G}\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},0}(\mathcal{D},\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}})-\Delta_{\mathrm{chart}}.

(3.5)

Assumption 3.6

(Component-to-baseline comparison)
.

The sharp component distance is controlled by the older baseline distance and an explicit comparison error:

\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{comp}}^{\sharp,[0,K]}(\mathcal{D},\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{comp}})\leq C_{\mathrm{comp}/0}\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},0}(\mathcal{D},\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}})+\Delta_{\mathrm{comp}/0}.

(3.6)

Assumption 3.7

(Dominance of the positive coefficient)
.

The clean/chart lower bound dominates the detector loss produced by residual closure and component comparison. In schematic form,

c_{\Lambda,0}:=\mu_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{comp}}\lambda_{G}-C_{\mathrm{dc}}C_{\mathrm{comp}}^{[0,K]}(M_{U})C_{\mathrm{comp}/0}>0.

(3.7)

If the pressure-tail comparison is inserted into the component-to-baseline comparison, the corresponding pressure-tail constants are included in
C_{\mathrm{comp}/0}
and the same positivity condition is used.

Remark 3.8

(Why the hypotheses are separated)
.

These hypotheses are not all of the same type.
Section

3
is a bookkeeping theorem proved in the paper.
Section

3
is a detector-intertwining theorem proved under channelwise assumptions.
Section

3
is a pressure-tail comparison theorem proved under baseline visibility and tail approximation. In contrast,
Sections

3
,
3
,
3
and
3
are the structural inputs that must be verified for a concrete Navier–Stokes-generated package class.

4
Pressure-tail visibility module

The first module addresses a mismatch of gauges. The clean model naturally sees pressure through a pressure source, its Calderon–Zygmund image, and a harmonic correction, following the standard local pressure-decomposition viewpoint in Navier–Stokes regularity theory
[
6
,
11
,
15
]
. The older baseline quotient may not contain all these coordinates. Without a visibility theorem, a defect could be large in the pressure-natural tail geometry but invisible in the baseline geometry used in the final theorem.

The appendices prove this module in several steps. Harmonic polynomial approximation controls the harmonic tail. Clean projection-tail approximation controls the finite projection of the clean pressure image. Same-gauge closure ensures that the baseline coordinate, projection tail, and harmonic tail are evaluated on one common representative. Split package estimates treat localized pressure splitting and finite-amplitude terms. Compact clean-source criteria provide uniform projection-tail convergence when the clean pressure image is compact, while effective projection gives a finite-dimensional alternative.

The output used in the main proof is the following.

Theorem 4.1

(Pressure-tail closure in baseline gauge)
.

Assume the finite-window baseline visibility, finite-amplitude, same-gauge, projection-tail, and harmonic-tail hypotheses of Appendix A. Then the pressure-natural tail distance satisfies

\displaystyle\operatorname{dist}^{\sharp,\mathrm{tail}}_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},\mathrm{tail}}(\mathcal{D},\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}})

\displaystyle\leq C_{\mathrm{tail}}\bigl[(1+C_{\mathrm{tail}/0})\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},0}(\mathcal{D},\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}})

(4.1)

\displaystyle+\delta_{0}+\Delta_{\mathrm{tail}/0}\bigr]+\alpha_{\mathrm{proj}}\Delta^{\mathrm{unif}}_{\mathrm{proj},N}(\mathcal{A}_{\Lambda})+\alpha_{\mathrm{harm}}\Delta^{(3/2)}_{\mathrm{harm},M}.

In particular, after collecting the last three terms into
\mathfrak{E}_{\mathrm{tail},0}(\mathcal{D})
,
Section

3
holds.

Proof.

The detailed proof is split between the core material below and the remaining compactness/tax criteria in
Appendix

A
. The logical chain is as follows.
Theorem

4.14
gives pressure-natural harmonic polynomial tail approximation. The clean projection estimates give finite projection-tail control on the selected clean pressure image. The same-gauge comparison and split package estimates show that these tail controls can be imposed on the same admissible representative used by the baseline quotient. Baseline visibility then compares the pressure-natural tail excess to the older baseline excess. Finally, compact clean-pressure-image convergence gives the uniform projection-tail error
\Delta^{\mathrm{unif}}_{\mathrm{proj},N}(\mathcal{A}_{\Lambda})
. Combining these estimates gives (
4.1
); this is exactly the uniform version of
Theorem

A.16
, with the non-uniform form recorded in
Theorem

4.66
.
∎

Remark 4.2

(Problem solved by this module)
.

The module rules out a purely notational failure: adding pressure-tail coordinates should not create an uncontrolled direction that the baseline distance cannot see. It does not prove that all possible Navier–Stokes pressure-source families are compact. It proves that once compactness or effective approximation is available on the selected finite-window class, the pressure tails enter the final theorem only through explicit errors.

Core proof details for the pressure-tail module

The following material is kept in the main text because it is the first place where the paper’s central geometric issue appears: enhanced pressure-tail coordinates must be controlled by a baseline quotient without changing representatives. Later compactness criteria and pressure/tax quotient criteria are deferred to
Appendix

A
.

Package Geometry and Pressure Tails

4.1
Normalized pressure geometry and package distances

Convention 4.3

(Normalized pressure geometry)
.

Throughout the paper,

I=(-1,0),\qquad Q_{1}=B_{1}\times I,\qquad B_{1/2}\subset B_{2/3}\subset B_{3/4}\subset B_{1}\subset\mathbb{R}^{3}.

When a cutoff is needed,
\eta\in C_{c}^{\infty}(B_{1})
is fixed with

0\leq\eta\leq 1,\qquad\eta\equiv 1\quad\text{on }B_{3/4}.

Set

X_{\mathrm{src}}:=L^{3/2}\bigl(I;L^{3/2}(B_{1})\bigr)^{3\times 3},\qquad Y_{\mathrm{prs}}:=L^{3/2}\bigl(I;L^{3/2}(B_{1/2})\bigr).

The default harmonic observation space is the pressure-natural space

Y_{\mathrm{harm}}:=Y^{(3/2)}_{\mathrm{harm}}:=L^{3/2}\bigl(I;L^{3/2}(B_{3/4})\bigr).

The auxiliary Hilbert harmonic space is

Y^{(2)}_{\mathrm{harm}}:=L^{2}\bigl(I;L^{2}(B_{3/4})\bigr).

Definition 4.4

(Tail-resolved intrinsic norm)
.

Let
D
be a finite-window package carrying a baseline intrinsic norm

\|D\|_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},0}
, a clean pressure source
F^{\mathrm{cl}}_{D}\in X_{\mathrm{src}}
for which

R_{i}R_{j}(F^{\mathrm{cl}}_{D,ij})\in Y_{\mathrm{prs}}
, and a harmonic pressure coordinate

p_{\mathrm{harm},D}\in Y_{\mathrm{harm}}
. Define

\|D\|_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},\mathrm{tail}}:=\|D\|_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},0}+\left\|R_{i}R_{j}(F^{\mathrm{cl}}_{D,ij})\right\|_{Y_{\mathrm{prs}}}+\left\|p_{\mathrm{harm},D}\right\|_{Y_{\mathrm{harm}}}.

(4.2)

Unless a different norm is explicitly named,
\|\cdot\|_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int}}
means

\|\cdot\|_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},\mathrm{tail}}
.

Definition 4.5

(Intrinsic and enhanced-tail distances)
.

Let
\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda}
be a finite-window intrinsic gauge space. The tail-resolved quotient
distance is

\dist_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},\mathrm{tail}}(D,\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda}):=\inf_{\zeta\in\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda}}\|D-\zeta\|_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},\mathrm{tail}}.

The older baseline distance is

\dist_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},0}(D,\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda}):=\inf_{\zeta\in\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda}}\|D-\zeta\|_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},0}.

Given nonnegative tail functionals
T_{\mathrm{proj}}(D;\zeta)
and
T_{\mathrm{harm}}(D;\zeta)
and
weights
\alpha_{\mathrm{proj}},\alpha_{\mathrm{harm}}>0
, define

\dist^{\sharp,\mathrm{tail}}_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},\mathrm{tail}}(D,\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda}):=\inf_{\zeta\in\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda}}\Bigl(\|D-\zeta\|_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},\mathrm{tail}}+\alpha_{\mathrm{proj}}T_{\mathrm{proj}}(D;\zeta)+\alpha_{\mathrm{harm}}T_{\mathrm{harm}}(D;\zeta)\Bigr).

(4.3)

Proposition 4.6

(Common-representative comparison)
.

Suppose that for a package
D
there is a representative
\zeta_{*}(D)\in\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda}
such that

\|D-\zeta_{*}\|_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},\mathrm{tail}}\leq\dist_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},\mathrm{tail}}(D,\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda})+\delta_{\mathrm{int}},

(4.4)

and such that the same representative satisfies

T_{\mathrm{proj}}(D;\zeta_{*})\leq C^{\mathrm{app}}_{\mathrm{proj}}\dist_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},\mathrm{tail}}(D,\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda})+\Delta_{\mathrm{proj},N},

(4.5)

T_{\mathrm{harm}}(D;\zeta_{*})\leq C^{\mathrm{app}}_{\mathrm{harm}}\dist_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},\mathrm{tail}}(D,\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda})+\Delta_{\mathrm{harm},M}.

Then

\dist^{\sharp,\mathrm{tail}}_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},\mathrm{tail}}(D,\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda})\leq C_{\mathrm{tail}/\mathrm{int}}\dist_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},\mathrm{tail}}(D,\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda})+\Delta_{\mathrm{tail}/\mathrm{int}},

(4.6)

where

C_{\mathrm{tail}/\mathrm{int}}:=1+\alpha_{\mathrm{proj}}C^{\mathrm{app}}_{\mathrm{proj}}+\alpha_{\mathrm{harm}}C^{\mathrm{app}}_{\mathrm{harm}},\qquad\Delta_{\mathrm{tail}/\mathrm{int}}:=\delta_{\mathrm{int}}+\alpha_{\mathrm{proj}}\Delta_{\mathrm{proj},N}+\alpha_{\mathrm{harm}}\Delta_{\mathrm{harm},M}.

Proof.

Use
\zeta_{*}
as a competitor in the infimum defining

\dist^{\sharp,\mathrm{tail}}_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},\mathrm{tail}}
. Then substitute
(
4.4
) and the two tail bounds in (
4.5
) and
collect the coefficients of
\dist_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},\mathrm{tail}}(D,\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda})
.
∎

Remark 4.7

(Role of the common representative)
.

The point of Proposition
4.1
is not the algebra, which is immediate.
The point is that the intrinsic core, the clean projection tail, and the harmonic tail must
be controlled on one gauge representative. Controlling the three quantities after three
separate optimizations would not imply (
4.6
).

4.2
Harmonic polynomial approximation

4.2.1
The Hilbert harmonic model

Definition 4.8

(Harmonic polynomial spaces)
.

For
R>0
, let
\mathcal{H}_{M}(B_{R})
be the space of restrictions to
B_{R}
of harmonic
polynomials on
\mathbb{R}^{3}
of degree at most
M
. Let

\Pi^{R}_{\mathrm{harm},M}:L^{2}(B_{R})\to\mathcal{H}_{M}(B_{R})

be the
L^{2}(B_{R})
-orthogonal projection. For time-dependent functions,

\Pi^{R}_{\mathrm{harm},M}
acts on the spatial variable for almost every time.

Lemma 4.9

(Finite-dimensional harmonic gauge space)
.

For every
M\geq 0
and
R>0
,
\mathcal{H}_{M}(B_{R})
is finite dimensional. In three spatial
dimensions,

\dim\mathcal{H}_{M}(B_{R})=\sum_{m=0}^{M}(2m+1)=(M+1)^{2}.

Proof.

Every harmonic polynomial decomposes uniquely into homogeneous harmonic polynomials.
The homogeneous harmonic polynomials of degree
m
in
\mathbb{R}^{3}
have dimension
2m+1
.
Summing over
0\leq m\leq M
gives the dimension formula. Restriction to
B_{R}
is
injective on polynomials, so the same dimension holds for
\mathcal{H}_{M}(B_{R})
.
∎

Theorem 4.10

(Hilbert harmonic polynomial tail estimate)
.

Let
0<r<R
, let
M\geq 0
, and let

h\in L^{2}\bigl(I;L^{2}(B_{R})\bigr)

be harmonic in
B_{R}
for almost every
t\in I
. Then

\left\|(I-\Pi^{R}_{\mathrm{harm},M})h\right\|_{L^{2}(I;L^{2}(B_{r}))}\leq\left(\frac{r}{R}\right)^{M+5/2}\left\|h\right\|_{L^{2}(I;L^{2}(B_{R}))}.

(4.7)

Consequently, for
r=1/2
and
R=3/4
,

\left\|(I-\Pi^{3/4}_{\mathrm{harm},M})h\right\|_{L^{2}(I;L^{2}(B_{1/2}))}\leq\left(\frac{2}{3}\right)^{M+1}\left\|h\right\|_{L^{2}(I;L^{2}(B_{3/4}))}.

Proof.

It is enough to prove the spatial estimate for almost every fixed time. Let
h
be
harmonic in
B_{R}
. Its harmonic expansion at the origin is

h(x)=\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}H_{m}(x),

where
H_{m}
is a homogeneous harmonic polynomial of degree
m
. Terms of different
degree are orthogonal in
L^{2}(B_{\rho})
for every
0<\rho<R
, because their restrictions
to spheres are spherical harmonics of different degrees. Homogeneity gives

\left\|H_{m}\right\|_{L^{2}(B_{\rho})}^{2}=\left(\frac{\rho}{R}\right)^{2m+3}\left\|H_{m}\right\|_{L^{2}(B_{R})}^{2}.

Since
\Pi^{R}_{\mathrm{harm},M}h=\sum_{m=0}^{M}H_{m}
, the tail satisfies

\displaystyle\left\|(I-\Pi^{R}_{\mathrm{harm},M})h\right\|_{L^{2}(B_{r})}^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{m>M}\left\|H_{m}\right\|_{L^{2}(B_{r})}^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{m>M}\left(\frac{r}{R}\right)^{2m+3}\left\|H_{m}\right\|_{L^{2}(B_{R})}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{r}{R}\right)^{2M+5}\sum_{m>M}\left\|H_{m}\right\|_{L^{2}(B_{R})}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{r}{R}\right)^{2M+5}\left\|h\right\|_{L^{2}(B_{R})}^{2}.

Taking square roots gives the spatial estimate. Integrating in time gives
(
4.7
). In the normalized geometry
r/R=2/3
, and the stronger
factor
(2/3)^{M+5/2}
is bounded by
(2/3)^{M+1}
.
∎

4.2.2
The pressure-natural harmonic model

The local pressure class naturally gives
p\in L^{3/2}
, not
p\in L^{2}
. Therefore the
pressure-natural harmonic geometry requires an
L^{3/2}
harmonic tail estimate.

Definition 4.11

(Pressure-natural harmonic projection on harmonic data)
.

Let
0<\rho<R
. If

h\in L^{3/2}\bigl(I;L^{3/2}(B_{R})\bigr)
is harmonic in
B_{R}
for almost every time,
define
\Pi^{\rho}_{\mathrm{harm},M}h(t,\cdot)
to be the
L^{2}(B_{\rho})
-orthogonal projection of

h(t,\cdot)
onto
\mathcal{H}_{M}(B_{\rho})
, restricted to
B_{\rho}
.

Remark 4.12
.

The operator
\Pi^{\rho}_{\mathrm{harm},M}
is used here only on harmonic data. We do not claim
that it is a bounded projection on all of
L^{3/2}(B_{R})
. Harmonic interior estimates give
the required
L^{2}(B_{\rho})
control on the harmonic subspace.

Lemma 4.13

(Interior
L^{3/2}
-to-
L^{2}
estimate)
.

For every
0<\rho<R
, there exists
C_{\rho,R}<\infty
such that every harmonic function

h\in L^{3/2}(B_{R})
satisfies

\left\|h\right\|_{L^{2}(B_{\rho})}\leq C_{\rho,R}\left\|h\right\|_{L^{3/2}(B_{R})}.

Proof.

Let
d=(R-\rho)/4
. For every
x\in B_{\rho}
, the ball
B_{2d}(x)
is contained in
B_{R}
.
Since
|h|^{3/2}
is subharmonic for harmonic
h
, the mean-value estimate gives

|h(x)|^{3/2}\leq Cd^{-3}\int_{B_{d}(x)}|h(y)|^{3/2}\,dy\leq Cd^{-3}\left\|h\right\|_{L^{3/2}(B_{R})}^{3/2}.

Thus
\left\|h\right\|_{L^{\infty}(B_{\rho})}\leq C_{\rho,R}\left\|h\right\|_{L^{3/2}(B_{R})}
. Multiplying by

|B_{\rho}|^{1/2}
gives the
L^{2}(B_{\rho})
estimate.
∎

Theorem 4.14

(
L^{3/2}
harmonic polynomial tail estimate)
.

Let
0<r<\rho<R
. Let

h\in L^{3/2}\bigl(I;L^{3/2}(B_{R})\bigr)

be harmonic in
B_{R}
for almost every
t\in I
. Then there exists
C_{r,\rho,R}<\infty

such that

\left\|(I-\Pi^{\rho}_{\mathrm{harm},M})h\right\|_{L^{3/2}(I;L^{3/2}(B_{r}))}\leq C_{r,\rho,R}\left(\frac{r}{\rho}\right)^{M+5/2}\left\|h\right\|_{L^{3/2}(I;L^{3/2}(B_{R}))}.

(4.8)

In particular, for any fixed
\theta
with
r/\rho<\theta<1
, the right-hand side is bounded by

C_{r,\rho,R,\theta}\theta^{M}\left\|h\right\|_{L^{3/2}(I;L^{3/2}(B_{R}))}.

Proof.

Fix a time
t
for which
h(t,\cdot)
is harmonic in
B_{R}
. By
Lemma
4.2.2
,
h(t,\cdot)\in L^{2}(B_{\rho})
. The Hilbert tail estimate on

B_{r}\subset B_{\rho}
gives

\left\|(I-\Pi^{\rho}_{\mathrm{harm},M})h(t)\right\|_{L^{2}(B_{r})}\leq\left(\frac{r}{\rho}\right)^{M+5/2}\left\|h(t)\right\|_{L^{2}(B_{\rho})}.

By finite-measure embedding on
B_{r}
,

\left\|(I-\Pi^{\rho}_{\mathrm{harm},M})h(t)\right\|_{L^{3/2}(B_{r})}\leq|B_{r}|^{1/6}\left\|(I-\Pi^{\rho}_{\mathrm{harm},M})h(t)\right\|_{L^{2}(B_{r})}.

Using Lemma
4.2.2
once more gives

\left\|(I-\Pi^{\rho}_{\mathrm{harm},M})h(t)\right\|_{L^{3/2}(B_{r})}\leq C_{r,\rho,R}\left(\frac{r}{\rho}\right)^{M+5/2}\left\|h(t)\right\|_{L^{3/2}(B_{R})}.

Taking the
L^{3/2}
norm in time proves (
4.8
). The
\theta^{M}

form follows by absorbing the fixed factor
(r/\rho)^{5/2}
and the comparison between

(r/\rho)^{M}
and
\theta^{M}
into the constant.
∎

Corollary 4.15

(Normalized pressure-natural harmonic tail)
.

Let

h\in L^{3/2}\bigl(I;L^{3/2}(B_{3/4})\bigr)

be harmonic in
B_{3/4}
for almost every time. Then

\left\|(I-\Pi^{2/3}_{\mathrm{harm},M})h\right\|_{L^{3/2}(I;L^{3/2}(B_{1/2}))}\leq C_{\mathrm{harm},3/2}\left(\frac{3}{4}\right)^{M}\left\|h\right\|_{L^{3/2}(I;L^{3/2}(B_{3/4}))}.

(4.9)

Proof.

Apply Theorem
4.14
with
r=1/2
,
\rho=2/3
, and
R=3/4
. Then

r/\rho=3/4
, and the fixed factor
(3/4)^{5/2}
is absorbed into
C_{\mathrm{harm},3/2}
.
∎

Definition 4.16

(Harmonic tail errors)
.

In the pressure-natural harmonic geometry, set

\Delta^{(3/2)}_{\mathrm{harm},M}(h):=C_{\mathrm{harm},3/2}\left(\frac{3}{4}\right)^{M}\left\|h\right\|_{Y_{\mathrm{harm}}}.

(4.10)

In the Hilbert harmonic geometry, the corresponding normalized error is

\Delta^{(2)}_{\mathrm{harm},M}(h):=\left(\frac{2}{3}\right)^{M+1}\left\|h\right\|_{Y^{(2)}_{\mathrm{harm}}}.

4.3
Clean projection-tail approximation

Definition 4.17

(Clean pressure projection datum)
.

A clean pressure projection datum is a sequence of finite-rank bounded linear maps

P^{\mathrm{cl}}_{\mathrm{prs},N}:Y_{\mathrm{prs}}\to Y_{\mathrm{prs}}.

It is called strongly convergent if
P^{\mathrm{cl}}_{\mathrm{prs},N}g\to g
in
Y_{\mathrm{prs}}
for every

g\in Y_{\mathrm{prs}}
. It is uniformly bounded if

C_{P}:=\sup_{N}\left\|P^{\mathrm{cl}}_{\mathrm{prs},N}\right\|_{Y_{\mathrm{prs}}\to Y_{\mathrm{prs}}}<\infty.

Definition 4.18

(Clean projection tail)
.

Let
F^{\mathrm{cl}}\in X_{\mathrm{src}}
be a clean pressure source for which

g_{F}:=R_{i}R_{j}(F^{\mathrm{cl}}_{ij})

is defined as an element of
Y_{\mathrm{prs}}
. The clean projection tail is

\Delta_{\mathrm{proj},N}(F^{\mathrm{cl}}):=\left\|(I-P^{\mathrm{cl}}_{\mathrm{prs},N})g_{F}\right\|_{Y_{\mathrm{prs}}}.

Proposition 4.19

(Fixed-source projection convergence)
.

Assume
P^{\mathrm{cl}}_{\mathrm{prs},N}\to I
strongly on
Y_{\mathrm{prs}}
. If
F^{\mathrm{cl}}\in X_{\mathrm{src}}
and

R_{i}R_{j}(F^{\mathrm{cl}}_{ij})\in Y_{\mathrm{prs}}
, then

\Delta_{\mathrm{proj},N}(F^{\mathrm{cl}})\to 0\qquad\text{as }N\to\infty.

Proof.

By definition,

\Delta_{\mathrm{proj},N}(F^{\mathrm{cl}})=\left\|(I-P^{\mathrm{cl}}_{\mathrm{prs},N})R_{i}R_{j}(F^{\mathrm{cl}}_{ij})\right\|_{Y_{\mathrm{prs}}}.

Strong convergence of
P^{\mathrm{cl}}_{\mathrm{prs},N}
to the identity, applied to the fixed element

R_{i}R_{j}(F^{\mathrm{cl}}_{ij})\in Y_{\mathrm{prs}}
, gives the result.
∎

Proposition 4.20

(Compact-family projection-tail convergence)
.

Assume
P^{\mathrm{cl}}_{\mathrm{prs},N}\to I
strongly on
Y_{\mathrm{prs}}
and

C_{P}:=\sup_{N}\|P^{\mathrm{cl}}_{\mathrm{prs},N}\|_{Y_{\mathrm{prs}}\to Y_{\mathrm{prs}}}<\infty
. If

\mathcal{G}\subset Y_{\mathrm{prs}}
is compact, then

\sup_{g\in\mathcal{G}}\left\|(I-P^{\mathrm{cl}}_{\mathrm{prs},N})g\right\|_{Y_{\mathrm{prs}}}\to 0.

Equivalently, if

\mathcal{G}=\{R_{i}R_{j}(F_{ij}):F\in\mathcal{F}\}

is compact in
Y_{\mathrm{prs}}
, then

\sup_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\left\|(I-P^{\mathrm{cl}}_{\mathrm{prs},N})R_{i}R_{j}(F_{ij})\right\|_{Y_{\mathrm{prs}}}\to 0.

Proof.

Fix
\varepsilon>0
. Since
\mathcal{G}
is compact, choose finitely many points

g_{1},\ldots,g_{J}\in\mathcal{G}
such that for every
g\in\mathcal{G}
there is
j
with

\|g-g_{j}\|_{Y_{\mathrm{prs}}}\leq\varepsilon
. For each fixed center, strong convergence gives

\|(I-P^{\mathrm{cl}}_{\mathrm{prs},N})g_{j}\|_{Y_{\mathrm{prs}}}\to 0
. Since there are finitely many centers, for

N
large enough,

\max_{1\leq j\leq J}\left\|(I-P^{\mathrm{cl}}_{\mathrm{prs},N})g_{j}\right\|_{Y_{\mathrm{prs}}}\leq\varepsilon.

For arbitrary
g\in\mathcal{G}
, choose
g_{j}
as above. Then

\left\|(I-P^{\mathrm{cl}}_{\mathrm{prs},N})g\right\|_{Y_{\mathrm{prs}}}\leq\left\|(I-P^{\mathrm{cl}}_{\mathrm{prs},N})(g-g_{j})\right\|_{Y_{\mathrm{prs}}}+\left\|(I-P^{\mathrm{cl}}_{\mathrm{prs},N})g_{j}\right\|_{Y_{\mathrm{prs}}}\leq(1+C_{P})\varepsilon+\varepsilon.

Taking the supremum over
g\in\mathcal{G}
and then letting
\varepsilon\downarrow 0
proves the
claim.
∎

Remark 4.21
.

Compactness is essential in Proposition
4.3
. Strong convergence of
finite-rank projections does not imply uniform convergence on arbitrary bounded subsets of
an infinite-dimensional Banach space.

4.4
Same-gauge pressure-tail closure

Definition 4.22

(Tail-compatible finite-window class)
.

Let
X_{\Lambda}
be a finite-dimensional normed package space with norm

\|\cdot\|_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},\mathrm{tail}}
, and let
\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda}\subset X_{\Lambda}
be a gauge subspace.
A class
\mathcal{C}_{\Lambda}\subset X_{\Lambda}
is called tail-compatible, with constants

C^{\mathrm{tail}}_{\mathrm{proj}},C^{\mathrm{tail}}_{\mathrm{harm}}<\infty
and errors

\Delta_{\mathrm{proj},N},\Delta_{\mathrm{harm},M}\geq 0
, if for every
D\in\mathcal{C}_{\Lambda}
and every

\zeta\in\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda}
,

T_{\mathrm{proj}}(D;\zeta)\leq C^{\mathrm{tail}}_{\mathrm{proj}}\|D-\zeta\|_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},\mathrm{tail}}+\Delta_{\mathrm{proj},N},

T_{\mathrm{harm}}(D;\zeta)\leq C^{\mathrm{tail}}_{\mathrm{harm}}\|D-\zeta\|_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},\mathrm{tail}}+\Delta_{\mathrm{harm},M}.

Lemma 4.23

(Existence of a common best representative)
.

Assume
X_{\Lambda}
is finite dimensional and
\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda}\subset X_{\Lambda}
is a closed
subspace. Then every
D\in X_{\Lambda}
admits
\zeta_{*}(D)\in\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda}
such that

\|D-\zeta_{*}\|_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},\mathrm{tail}}=\dist_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},\mathrm{tail}}(D,\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda}).

Proof.

Choose a minimizing sequence
(\zeta_{n})\subset\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda}
. Since

\|D-\zeta_{n}\|_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},\mathrm{tail}}
is bounded along the sequence, the triangle inequality
implies that
(\zeta_{n})
is bounded in the finite-dimensional normed space
X_{\Lambda}
.
Because
\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda}
is closed and finite dimensional, a subsequence converges to some

\zeta_{*}\in\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda}
. Continuity of
\zeta\mapsto\|D-\zeta\|_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},\mathrm{tail}}
gives the
minimum.
∎

Proposition 4.24

(Tail-compatible bounds give same-gauge compatibility)
.

Let
\mathcal{C}_{\Lambda}
be a tail-compatible finite-window class. Then every

D\in\mathcal{C}_{\Lambda}
satisfies the hypotheses of Proposition
4.1
with

\delta_{\mathrm{int}}=0
,

C^{\mathrm{app}}_{\mathrm{proj}}=C^{\mathrm{tail}}_{\mathrm{proj}},\qquad C^{\mathrm{app}}_{\mathrm{harm}}=C^{\mathrm{tail}}_{\mathrm{harm}}.

Consequently,

\displaystyle\dist^{\sharp,\mathrm{tail}}_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},\mathrm{tail}}(D,\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda})

\displaystyle\leq\bigl(1+\alpha_{\mathrm{proj}}C^{\mathrm{tail}}_{\mathrm{proj}}+\alpha_{\mathrm{harm}}C^{\mathrm{tail}}_{\mathrm{harm}}\bigr)\dist_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},\mathrm{tail}}(D,\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda})

\displaystyle\quad+\alpha_{\mathrm{proj}}\Delta_{\mathrm{proj},N}+\alpha_{\mathrm{harm}}\Delta_{\mathrm{harm},M}.

(4.11)

Proof.

Choose the best representative
\zeta_{*}(D)
from Lemma
4.4
. The core
near-minimizer bound holds with
\delta_{\mathrm{int}}=0
. Applying the two tail-compatible
bounds in Definition
4.4
to this same representative gives the projection
and harmonic tail estimates required by Proposition
4.1
. Substitution
into (
4.6
) gives (
4.24
).
∎

Definition 4.25

(Coordinate tail model)
.

A coordinate tail model on
X_{\Lambda}
consists of normed spaces
Z_{\mathrm{proj}}
and

Z_{\mathrm{harm}}
and bounded linear maps

A_{\mathrm{proj}}:X_{\Lambda}\to Z_{\mathrm{proj}},\qquad A_{\mathrm{harm}}:X_{\Lambda}\to Z_{\mathrm{harm}}.

The corresponding model tail functionals are

T_{\mathrm{proj}}(D;\zeta):=\left\|A_{\mathrm{proj}}(D-\zeta)\right\|_{Z_{\mathrm{proj}}}+\Delta_{\mathrm{proj},N},

T_{\mathrm{harm}}(D;\zeta):=\left\|A_{\mathrm{harm}}(D-\zeta)\right\|_{Z_{\mathrm{harm}}}+\Delta_{\mathrm{harm},M}.

Lemma 4.26

(Coordinate-map criterion)
.

Every coordinate tail model defines a tail-compatible finite-window class with

C^{\mathrm{tail}}_{\mathrm{proj}}=\left\|A_{\mathrm{proj}}\right\|_{X_{\Lambda}\to Z_{\mathrm{proj}}},\qquad C^{\mathrm{tail}}_{\mathrm{harm}}=\left\|A_{\mathrm{harm}}\right\|_{X_{\Lambda}\to Z_{\mathrm{harm}}}.

Proof.

For every
D\in X_{\Lambda}
and
\zeta\in\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda}
,

\left\|A_{\mathrm{proj}}(D-\zeta)\right\|_{Z_{\mathrm{proj}}}\leq\left\|A_{\mathrm{proj}}\right\|_{X_{\Lambda}\to Z_{\mathrm{proj}}}\|D-\zeta\|_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},\mathrm{tail}}.

Adding
\Delta_{\mathrm{proj},N}
gives the projection-tail bound. The harmonic estimate is
identical with
A_{\mathrm{harm}}
in place of
A_{\mathrm{proj}}
.
∎

Theorem 4.27

(Abstract finite-window pressure-tail closure)
.

Let
\mathcal{C}_{\Lambda}
be a tail-compatible finite-window class in the tail-resolved geometry.
Then every
D\in\mathcal{C}_{\Lambda}
satisfies

\dist^{\sharp,\mathrm{tail}}_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},\mathrm{tail}}(D,\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda})\leq C_{\mathrm{tail}}\dist_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},\mathrm{tail}}(D,\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda})+\alpha_{\mathrm{proj}}\Delta_{\mathrm{proj},N}+\alpha_{\mathrm{harm}}\Delta_{\mathrm{harm},M},

(4.12)

where

C_{\mathrm{tail}}:=1+\alpha_{\mathrm{proj}}C^{\mathrm{tail}}_{\mathrm{proj}}+\alpha_{\mathrm{harm}}C^{\mathrm{tail}}_{\mathrm{harm}}.

Moreover, the harmonic error may be chosen as (
4.10
) in the
pressure-natural harmonic geometry whenever the shifted harmonic coordinate is harmonic,
and as
\Delta^{(2)}_{\mathrm{harm},M}
in the Hilbert harmonic geometry whenever the shifted
harmonic coordinate belongs to
Y^{(2)}_{\mathrm{harm}}
. The clean projection error converges by
Proposition
4.3
for fixed clean sources and by
Proposition
4.3
for compact pressure-source images with uniformly
bounded projections.

Proof.

The estimate (
4.12
) is exactly
Proposition
4.4
. The final assertions are substitutions of the
harmonic approximation estimates from Section
4.2
and the projection
convergence results from Section
4.3
into the errors

\Delta_{\mathrm{harm},M}
and
\Delta_{\mathrm{proj},N}
.
∎

4.5
Localized pressure splitting and split packages

This section constructs a concrete fixed-scale package model from local pressure data. The
construction is local and finite-window. It does not assert scale-uniformity, pressure/tax
coercivity, or canonicality of the gauge class.

Definition 4.28

(Pressure-admissible local data)
.

A local pair
(u,p)
on
Q_{1}
is called pressure-admissible if

u\in L^{3}(Q_{1})^{3},\qquad p\in L^{3/2}(Q_{1}),

and, for almost every
t\in I
,

-\Delta p(t,\cdot)=\partial_{i}\partial_{j}(u_{i}u_{j})(t,\cdot)

in the sense of distributions on
B_{1}
.

Convention 4.29

(Pressure normalization)
.

Since the pressure equation determines
p
only up to a time-dependent constant, we choose
the representative satisfying

\int_{B_{3/4}}p(t,x)\,dx=0\qquad\text{for almost every }t\in I,

after subtracting a suitable function of time if necessary. This is only a finite-window
normalization convention.

Definition 4.30

(Localized active and harmonic pressure)
.

For pressure-admissible data
(u,p)
, set

F^{\mathrm{act}}_{ij}:=\eta u_{i}u_{j}.

Extend
F^{\mathrm{act}}
by zero outside
B_{1}
and define

p_{\mathrm{act}}:=R_{i}R_{j}(F^{\mathrm{act}}_{ij}).

On
B_{3/4}\times I
, define the harmonic pressure remainder

p_{\mathrm{harm}}:=p-p_{\mathrm{act}}.

Lemma 4.31

(Localized active pressure bound)
.

For every pressure-admissible local pair
(u,p)
,

\left\|p_{\mathrm{act}}\right\|_{Y_{\mathrm{prs}}}\leq C_{\mathrm{CZ}}\left\|u\right\|_{L^{3}(Q_{1})}^{2}.

Proof.

For almost every
t
, the Calderon–Zygmund inequality for second Riesz transforms on

\mathbb{R}^{3}
, applied after zero extension from
B_{1}
, gives

\left\|R_{i}R_{j}(F^{\mathrm{act}}_{ij})(t)\right\|_{L^{3/2}(B_{1/2})}\leq C_{\mathrm{CZ}}\left\|F^{\mathrm{act}}(t)\right\|_{L^{3/2}(B_{1})^{3\times 3}}.

Taking the
L^{3/2}
norm in time and using
0\leq\eta\leq 1
yields

\left\|p_{\mathrm{act}}\right\|_{Y_{\mathrm{prs}}}\leq C_{\mathrm{CZ}}\left\|\eta u_{i}u_{j}\right\|_{L^{3/2}(I;L^{3/2}(B_{1}))^{3\times 3}}\leq C_{\mathrm{CZ}}\left\|u\right\|_{L^{3}(Q_{1})}^{2},

where harmless finite-component constants are absorbed into
C_{\mathrm{CZ}}
.
∎

Lemma 4.32

(Harmonicity of the pressure remainder)
.

If
(u,p)
is pressure-admissible, then for almost every
t\in I
,

-\Delta p_{\mathrm{harm}}(t,\cdot)=0

in the sense of distributions on
B_{3/4}
.

Proof.

The global distributional identity for the localized Riesz potential gives

-\Delta p_{\mathrm{act}}=\partial_{i}\partial_{j}(\eta u_{i}u_{j})

on
\mathbb{R}^{3}
. Let
\varphi\in C_{c}^{\infty}(B_{3/4})
. Since
\eta\equiv 1
on a neighborhood
of
\supp\varphi
,

\langle\partial_{i}\partial_{j}(\eta u_{i}u_{j}),\varphi\rangle=\langle\partial_{i}\partial_{j}(u_{i}u_{j}),\varphi\rangle.

Thus
p_{\mathrm{act}}
satisfies the same pressure Poisson equation as
p
on
B_{3/4}
, and their
difference is harmonic there.
∎

Proposition 4.33

(Pressure-natural admissibility)
.

Let
(u,p)
be pressure-admissible on
Q_{1}
. Then

p_{\mathrm{harm}}=p-p_{\mathrm{act}}\in Y_{\mathrm{harm}}=L^{3/2}(I;L^{3/2}(B_{3/4})).

Consequently,

\left\|(I-\Pi^{2/3}_{\mathrm{harm},M})p_{\mathrm{harm}}\right\|_{L^{3/2}(I;L^{3/2}(B_{1/2}))}\leq C_{\mathrm{harm},3/2}\left(\frac{3}{4}\right)^{M}\left\|p_{\mathrm{harm}}\right\|_{Y_{\mathrm{harm}}}.

(4.13)

Proof.

By Lemma
4.5
,
p_{\mathrm{act}}\in L^{3/2}(I;L^{3/2}(B_{3/4}))
. Since

p\in L^{3/2}(Q_{1})
, its restriction to
B_{3/4}\times I
lies in the same space. Hence

p_{\mathrm{harm}}=p-p_{\mathrm{act}}\in Y_{\mathrm{harm}}
. By Lemma
4.5
,
p_{\mathrm{harm}}
is
harmonic in
B_{3/4}
for almost every time. Therefore Corollary
4.2.2

applies and gives (
4.13
).
∎

Definition 4.34

(Residual localized split package)
.

A residual localized split package is a finite-window object

D=(u_{D},p_{\mathrm{act},D},p_{\mathrm{harm},D},F^{\mathrm{cl}}_{D},E^{\mathrm{cl}}_{F,D},\Pi_{D},\Phi_{D},T_{D},s_{D})

with

u_{D}\in L^{3}(Q_{1})^{3},\qquad E^{\mathrm{cl}}_{F,D}\in X_{\mathrm{src}},\qquad p_{\mathrm{harm},D}\in Y_{\mathrm{harm}},

such that
p_{\mathrm{harm},D}(t,\cdot)
is harmonic in
B_{3/4}
for almost every time and

F^{\mathrm{cl}}_{D,ij}=\eta u_{D,i}u_{D,j}+E^{\mathrm{cl}}_{F,D,ij}.

(4.14)

The coordinates
\Pi_{D},\Phi_{D},T_{D},s_{D}
denote auxiliary finite-window flux, energy or
trace, selected-trace, and slack data. They are retained as package coordinates but are not
used in the pressure-tail estimates below.

Definition 4.35

(Canonical localized split package)
.

If
(u,p)
is pressure-admissible, its canonical localized split package is the residual
split package
D_{\mathrm{NS}}(u,p)
with

u_{D}=u,\qquad E^{\mathrm{cl}}_{F,D}=0,\qquad F^{\mathrm{cl}}_{D,ij}=\eta u_{i}u_{j},\qquad p_{\mathrm{harm},D}=p-p_{\mathrm{act}}.

Proposition 4.36

(Pressure-splitting control of tail coordinates)
.

For every residual localized split package
D
,

\left\|R_{i}R_{j}(F^{\mathrm{cl}}_{D,ij})\right\|_{Y_{\mathrm{prs}}}\leq C_{\mathrm{CZ}}\Bigl(\left\|u_{D}\right\|_{L^{3}(Q_{1})}^{2}+\left\|E^{\mathrm{cl}}_{F,D}\right\|_{X_{\mathrm{src}}}\Bigr).

(4.15)

Consequently,

\|D\|_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},\mathrm{tail}}\leq\|D\|_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},0}+C_{\mathrm{CZ}}\Bigl(\left\|u_{D}\right\|_{L^{3}(Q_{1})}^{2}+\left\|E^{\mathrm{cl}}_{F,D}\right\|_{X_{\mathrm{src}}}\Bigr)+\left\|p_{\mathrm{harm},D}\right\|_{Y_{\mathrm{harm}}}.

Proof.

Extend
F^{\mathrm{cl}}_{D}
by zero outside
B_{1}
. The Calderon–Zygmund estimate for second
Riesz transforms gives

\left\|R_{i}R_{j}(F^{\mathrm{cl}}_{D,ij})\right\|_{Y_{\mathrm{prs}}}\leq C_{\mathrm{CZ}}\left\|F^{\mathrm{cl}}_{D}\right\|_{X_{\mathrm{src}}}.

Using (
4.14
) and
0\leq\eta\leq 1
,

\left\|F^{\mathrm{cl}}_{D}\right\|_{X_{\mathrm{src}}}\leq\left\|\eta u_{D,i}u_{D,j}\right\|_{X_{\mathrm{src}}}+\left\|E^{\mathrm{cl}}_{F,D}\right\|_{X_{\mathrm{src}}}\leq\left\|u_{D}\right\|_{L^{3}(Q_{1})}^{2}+\left\|E^{\mathrm{cl}}_{F,D}\right\|_{X_{\mathrm{src}}},

after absorbing finite-component constants into
C_{\mathrm{CZ}}
. The second estimate follows
from the definition of the tail-resolved norm.
∎

4.6
Conservative gauge and finite-amplitude split control

Definition 4.37

(Conservative admissible split gauge)
.

Fix finite-dimensional subspaces

G_{E}\subset X_{\mathrm{src}},\qquad G_{h}\subset Y_{\mathrm{harm}},

where elements of
G_{h}
are harmonic in
B_{3/4}
for almost every time, and a finite-dimensional
auxiliary gauge space
G_{\mathrm{aux}}
for the remaining package coordinates. Define

\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}:=\{0\}\times G_{E}\times G_{h}\times G_{\mathrm{aux}}.

For
\zeta=(\zeta_{u},\zeta_{E},\zeta_{h},\ldots)\in\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}
, the conservative convention is

\zeta_{u}=0.

Thus

u_{D-\zeta}:=u_{D},\qquad E^{\mathrm{cl}}_{F,D-\zeta}:=E^{\mathrm{cl}}_{F,D}-\zeta_{E},\qquad p_{\mathrm{harm},D-\zeta}:=p_{\mathrm{harm},D}-\zeta_{h}.

The shifted clean source is

F^{\mathrm{cl}}_{D-\zeta,ij}:=\eta u_{D,i}u_{D,j}+E^{\mathrm{cl}}_{F,D-\zeta,ij}.

Definition 4.38

(Split-support norm and distance)
.

For a localized split package, define

\|D\|_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},\mathrm{split}}:=\|D\|_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},\mathrm{tail}}+\left\|u_{D}\right\|_{L^{3}(Q_{1})}+\left\|E^{\mathrm{cl}}_{F,D}\right\|_{X_{\mathrm{src}}}+\left\|p_{\mathrm{harm},D}\right\|_{Y_{\mathrm{harm}}}.

(4.16)

The associated quotient distance over the conservative admissible gauge class is

\dist_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},\mathrm{split}}(D,\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}):=\inf_{\zeta\in\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}}\|D-\zeta\|_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},\mathrm{split}}.

Lemma 4.39

(Coordinate domination)
.

For every admissible gauge
\zeta\in\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}
,

\left\|u_{D-\zeta}\right\|_{L^{3}(Q_{1})}+\left\|E^{\mathrm{cl}}_{F,D-\zeta}\right\|_{X_{\mathrm{src}}}+\left\|p_{\mathrm{harm},D-\zeta}\right\|_{Y_{\mathrm{harm}}}\leq\|D-\zeta\|_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},\mathrm{split}}.

Proof.

This is immediate from the definition (
4.16
) applied to the shifted
package
D-\zeta
, after discarding the nonnegative tail-resolved term.
∎

Lemma 4.40

(Existence of an admissible quotient minimizer)
.

Assume
\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}
is a finite-dimensional closed subspace of the split package space
equipped with
\|\cdot\|_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},\mathrm{split}}
. Then every localized split package
D
admits

\zeta_{*}(D)\in\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}
such that

\|D-\zeta_{*}\|_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},\mathrm{split}}=\dist_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},\mathrm{split}}(D,\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}).

Proof.

The proof is the same as Lemma
4.4
, with

\|\cdot\|_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},\mathrm{split}}
and
\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}
in place of the tail norm and
\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda}
.
A minimizing sequence is bounded by the triangle inequality, finite-dimensional compactness
gives a convergent subsequence in
\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}
, and continuity gives the minimum.
∎

Definition 4.41

(Pressure-splitting functional)
.

For a shifted package
D-\zeta
, define

P_{\mathrm{split}}(D;\zeta):=C_{\mathrm{CZ}}\Bigl(\left\|u_{D-\zeta}\right\|_{L^{3}(Q_{1})}^{2}+\left\|E^{\mathrm{cl}}_{F,D-\zeta}\right\|_{X_{\mathrm{src}}}\Bigr)+\left\|p_{\mathrm{harm},D-\zeta}\right\|_{Y_{\mathrm{harm}}}.

(4.17)

Definition 4.42

(Finite-amplitude localized split class)
.

For
M_{U}<\infty
and
\delta_{\mathrm{split}}\geq 0
, let

\mathcal{A}^{\mathrm{NS},\mathrm{split}}_{\Lambda}(M_{U},\delta_{\mathrm{split}})
be the class of residual localized split
packages
D
equipped with the conservative gauge class
\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}
for which there exists
a same-gauge representative
\zeta_{*}(D)\in\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}
such that

\|D-\zeta_{*}\|_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},\mathrm{split}}\leq\dist_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},\mathrm{split}}(D,\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}})+\delta_{\mathrm{split}},

and

\left\|u_{D-\zeta_{*}}\right\|_{L^{3}(Q_{1})}\leq M_{U}.

Under the conservative convention
\zeta_{u}=0
, this finite-amplitude condition is simply

\|u_{D}\|_{L^{3}(Q_{1})}\leq M_{U}.

Theorem 4.43

(Finite-amplitude split-control criterion)
.

If
D\in\mathcal{A}^{\mathrm{NS},\mathrm{split}}_{\Lambda}(M_{U},\delta_{\mathrm{split}})
, then the representative

\zeta_{*}(D)
in Definition
4.6
satisfies

P_{\mathrm{split}}(D;\zeta_{*})\leq C_{\mathrm{FA}}(M_{U})\dist_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},\mathrm{split}}(D,\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}})+C_{\mathrm{FA}}(M_{U})\delta_{\mathrm{split}},

(4.18)

where one may take

C_{\mathrm{FA}}(M_{U}):=\max\{C_{\mathrm{CZ}}M_{U},C_{\mathrm{CZ}},1\}.

Proof.

The finite-amplitude bound gives

\left\|u_{D-\zeta_{*}}\right\|_{L^{3}(Q_{1})}^{2}\leq M_{U}\left\|u_{D-\zeta_{*}}\right\|_{L^{3}(Q_{1})}.

Therefore, by the definition of
P_{\mathrm{split}}
,

\displaystyle P_{\mathrm{split}}(D;\zeta_{*})

\displaystyle\leq C_{\mathrm{CZ}}M_{U}\left\|u_{D-\zeta_{*}}\right\|_{L^{3}(Q_{1})}+C_{\mathrm{CZ}}\left\|E^{\mathrm{cl}}_{F,D-\zeta_{*}}\right\|_{X_{\mathrm{src}}}+\left\|p_{\mathrm{harm},D-\zeta_{*}}\right\|_{Y_{\mathrm{harm}}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\mathrm{FA}}(M_{U})\Bigl(\left\|u_{D-\zeta_{*}}\right\|_{L^{3}(Q_{1})}+\left\|E^{\mathrm{cl}}_{F,D-\zeta_{*}}\right\|_{X_{\mathrm{src}}}+\left\|p_{\mathrm{harm},D-\zeta_{*}}\right\|_{Y_{\mathrm{harm}}}\Bigr).

By Lemma
4.6
, the parenthesized term is bounded by

\|D-\zeta_{*}\|_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},\mathrm{split}}
. The near-minimizer property of
\zeta_{*}
then gives
(
4.18
).
∎

Corollary 4.44

(Tail-resolved comparison from split control)
.

Let
D\in\mathcal{A}^{\mathrm{NS},\mathrm{split}}_{\Lambda}(M_{U},\delta_{\mathrm{split}})
, and assume that the same
representative
\zeta_{*}
satisfies the pressure-splitting quotient bound

\dist_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},\mathrm{tail}}(D,\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}})\leq\|D-\zeta_{*}\|_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},\mathrm{split}}+P_{\mathrm{split}}(D;\zeta_{*}).

(4.19)

Then

\dist_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},\mathrm{tail}}(D,\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}})\leq\bigl(1+C_{\mathrm{FA}}(M_{U})\bigr)\dist_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},\mathrm{split}}(D,\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}})+\bigl(1+C_{\mathrm{FA}}(M_{U})\bigr)\delta_{\mathrm{split}}.

(4.20)

Proof.

Use (
4.19
), the near-minimizer bound

\|D-\zeta_{*}\|_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},\mathrm{split}}\leq\dist_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},\mathrm{split}}(D,\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}})+\delta_{\mathrm{split}},

and the split-control estimate (
4.18
). Adding the two
terms gives (
4.20
).
∎

Proposition 4.45

(The pressure-splitting quotient bound)
.

For residual localized split packages with the conservative gauge convention,
(
4.19
) holds.

Proof.

Use
\zeta_{*}
as a competitor in
\dist_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},\mathrm{tail}}(D,\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}})
. By the definition
of the tail-resolved norm and by Proposition
4.5
applied to the
shifted package
D-\zeta_{*}
,

\displaystyle\dist_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},\mathrm{tail}}(D,\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}})

\displaystyle\leq\|D-\zeta_{*}\|_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},\mathrm{tail}}

\displaystyle\leq\|D-\zeta_{*}\|_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},0}+P_{\mathrm{split}}(D;\zeta_{*}).

Since
\|D-\zeta_{*}\|_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},0}\leq\|D-\zeta_{*}\|_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},\mathrm{split}}
, the claimed bound
follows.
∎

4.7
Split and abstract baseline pressure-tail closure

Theorem 4.46

(Main split-geometry pressure-tail closure)
.

Let
D\in\mathcal{A}^{\mathrm{NS},\mathrm{split}}_{\Lambda}(M_{U},\delta_{\mathrm{split}})
be a residual localized split
package in the conservative gauge geometry. Suppose that the package class is tail-compatible
in the sense of Definition
4.4
, with constants

C^{\mathrm{tail}}_{\mathrm{proj}}
and
C^{\mathrm{tail}}_{\mathrm{harm}}
. Then

\displaystyle\dist^{\sharp,\mathrm{tail}}_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},\mathrm{tail}}(D,\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}})

\displaystyle\leq C_{\mathrm{tail}}\bigl(1+C_{\mathrm{FA}}(M_{U})\bigr)\dist_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},\mathrm{split}}(D,\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}})

\displaystyle\quad+C_{\mathrm{tail}}\bigl(1+C_{\mathrm{FA}}(M_{U})\bigr)\delta_{\mathrm{split}}+\alpha_{\mathrm{proj}}\Delta_{\mathrm{proj},N}+\alpha_{\mathrm{harm}}\Delta_{\mathrm{harm},M},

(4.21)

where

C_{\mathrm{tail}}:=1+\alpha_{\mathrm{proj}}C^{\mathrm{tail}}_{\mathrm{proj}}+\alpha_{\mathrm{harm}}C^{\mathrm{tail}}_{\mathrm{harm}}.

In the pressure-natural harmonic geometry, one may take

\Delta_{\mathrm{harm},M}=C_{\mathrm{harm},3/2}\left(\frac{3}{4}\right)^{M}\left\|p_{\mathrm{harm},D-\zeta_{*}}\right\|_{Y_{\mathrm{harm}}}

whenever the shifted harmonic coordinate is the harmonic datum being approximated. The
projection error
\Delta_{\mathrm{proj},N}
converges to zero for fixed clean sources and uniformly
on compact clean pressure-source images under the hypotheses of
Propositions
4.3
and
4.3
.

Proof.

Apply the abstract tail-resolved closure theorem Theorem
4.27
with

\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}
in place of
\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda}
:

\dist^{\sharp,\mathrm{tail}}_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},\mathrm{tail}}(D,\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}})\leq C_{\mathrm{tail}}\dist_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},\mathrm{tail}}(D,\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}})+\alpha_{\mathrm{proj}}\Delta_{\mathrm{proj},N}+\alpha_{\mathrm{harm}}\Delta_{\mathrm{harm},M}.

Then substitute the split-to-tail comparison (
4.20
). The final statements
about the two errors follow from Corollary
4.2.2
and
Propositions
4.3
and
4.3
.
∎

Definition 4.47

(Baseline quotient distance)
.

The older baseline quotient distance over the conservative admissible gauge class is

\dist_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},0}(D,\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}):=\inf_{\zeta\in\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}}\|D-\zeta\|_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},0}.

Definition 4.48

(Split excess over the baseline)
.

For an admissible shifted package
D-\zeta
, define

\displaystyle E_{\mathrm{split}/0}(D;\zeta)

\displaystyle:=\|D-\zeta\|_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},\mathrm{split}}-\|D-\zeta\|_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},0}

\displaystyle=\left\|R_{i}R_{j}(F^{\mathrm{cl}}_{D-\zeta,ij})\right\|_{Y_{\mathrm{prs}}}+\left\|u_{D-\zeta}\right\|_{L^{3}(Q_{1})}+\left\|E^{\mathrm{cl}}_{F,D-\zeta}\right\|_{X_{\mathrm{src}}}+2\left\|p_{\mathrm{harm},D-\zeta}\right\|_{Y_{\mathrm{harm}}}.

The harmonic coordinate appears twice only because the split norm was defined as the
tail-resolved norm plus an additional split-support harmonic coordinate.

Assumption 4.49

(Same-gauge baseline-to-split excess control)
.

There are constants
C_{\mathrm{split}/0}<\infty
,
\Delta_{\mathrm{split}/0}\geq 0
, and a representative

\zeta_{0}(D)\in\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}
such that

\|D-\zeta_{0}\|_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},0}\leq\dist_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},0}(D,\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}})+\delta_{0},

and, on the same representative,

E_{\mathrm{split}/0}(D;\zeta_{0})\leq C_{\mathrm{split}/0}\dist_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},0}(D,\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}})+\Delta_{\mathrm{split}/0}.

Theorem 4.50

(Conditional baseline form of the split closure)
.

Assume Assumption
4.7
and the hypotheses of Theorem
4.46
.
Then

\displaystyle\dist^{\sharp,\mathrm{tail}}_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},\mathrm{tail}}(D,\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}})

\displaystyle\leq C_{\mathrm{tail}}\bigl(1+C_{\mathrm{FA}}(M_{U})\bigr)\Bigl[(1+C_{\mathrm{split}/0})\dist_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},0}(D,\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}})+\delta_{0}+\Delta_{\mathrm{split}/0}\Bigr]

\displaystyle\quad+C_{\mathrm{tail}}\bigl(1+C_{\mathrm{FA}}(M_{U})\bigr)\delta_{\mathrm{split}}

\displaystyle\quad+\alpha_{\mathrm{proj}}\Delta_{\mathrm{proj},N}+\alpha_{\mathrm{harm}}\Delta_{\mathrm{harm},M}.

(4.22)

Proof.

Use
\zeta_{0}
as a competitor in the split-support distance:

\displaystyle\dist_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},\mathrm{split}}(D,\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}})

\displaystyle\leq\|D-\zeta_{0}\|_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},\mathrm{split}}

\displaystyle=\|D-\zeta_{0}\|_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},0}+E_{\mathrm{split}/0}(D;\zeta_{0})

\displaystyle\leq(1+C_{\mathrm{split}/0})\dist_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},0}(D,\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}})+\delta_{0}+\Delta_{\mathrm{split}/0}.

Substitute this bound into (
4.46
).
∎

4.8
Baseline visibility and excess control

The pressure-tail closure theorem above works first in the tail-resolved and
split-support geometries. We now identify a sufficient finite-window mechanism
for returning to the older baseline geometry. The mechanism is deliberately
same-gauge: the representative that nearly minimizes the baseline distance must
also be the representative on which the visible pressure-tail and split-support
coordinates are controlled.

Convention 4.51

(Fixed source-to-pressure bound)
.

For every shifted residual localized split package
D-\zeta
, the fixed-window
pressure model uses the Calderon–Zygmund estimate

\left\|R_{i}R_{j}(F^{\mathrm{cl}}_{D-\zeta,ij})\right\|_{Y_{\mathrm{prs}}}\leq C_{\mathrm{CZ}}\left(\left\|u_{D-\zeta}\right\|_{L^{3}(Q_{1})}^{2}+\left\|E^{\mathrm{cl}}_{F,D-\zeta}\right\|_{X_{\mathrm{src}}}\right).

This is the shifted form of Proposition
4.5

inside the localized split package model. It is a fixed-window estimate; no
scale-uniform bound is claimed.

4.8.1
Baseline and tail excess functionals

4.8.2
Older baseline quotient distance

Definition 4.52

(Older baseline distance)
.

The older baseline intrinsic norm is denoted by

\|\cdot\|_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},0}.

The corresponding admissible quotient distance is

\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},0}(D,\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}):=\inf_{\zeta\in\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}}\|D-\zeta\|_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},0}.

Remark 4.53

(Baseline limitation)
.

The baseline norm does not automatically contain the active pressure source,
the harmonic pressure coordinate, the velocity amplitude, or the
pressure-splitting error. Any comparison with the tail-resolved or
split-support geometry therefore requires explicit visibility assumptions.

4.8.3
Pressure-natural tail excess

Definition 4.54

(Tail excess over the baseline norm)
.

For a shifted package
D-\zeta
, define

\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{tail}/0}^{(3/2)}(D;\zeta):=\|R_{i}R_{j}(F^{\mathrm{cl}}_{D-\zeta,ij})\|_{Y_{\mathrm{prs}}}+\|p_{\mathrm{harm},D-\zeta}\|_{Y_{\mathrm{harm}}}.

4.8.4
Split excess

Definition 4.55

(Split excess over the baseline norm)
.

The split-support excess is

\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{split}/0}^{(3/2)}(D;\zeta):=\|D-\zeta\|_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},\mathrm{split}}-\|D-\zeta\|_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},0}.

With the current split-support convention, this expands as

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{split}/0}^{(3/2)}(D;\zeta)

\displaystyle=\|R_{i}R_{j}(F^{\mathrm{cl}}_{D-\zeta,ij})\|_{Y_{\mathrm{prs}}}+\|u_{D-\zeta}\|_{L^{3}(Q_{1})}

\displaystyle+\|E^{\mathrm{cl}}_{F,D-\zeta}\|_{X_{\mathrm{src}}}+2\|p_{\mathrm{harm},D-\zeta}\|_{Y_{\mathrm{harm}}}.

Remark 4.56

(Double harmonic coordinate)
.

The harmonic coordinate appears twice only because the split-support norm was
defined as the tail-resolved norm plus additional split coordinates. This is
a bookkeeping convention, not a new PDE obstruction.

4.8.5
No-free-comparison principle

Lemma 4.57

(No baseline comparison without visibility)
.

Suppose there exists a shifted package direction
H
such that the ray

\{\lambda H:\lambda\geq 0\}
belongs to the admissible class under
consideration and

\|H\|_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},0}=0,\qquad\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{tail}/0}^{(3/2)}(H;0)>0.

Then no estimate of the form

\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{tail}/0}^{(3/2)}(D;\zeta_{0})\leq C_{\mathrm{tail}/0}\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},0}(D,\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}})+\Delta_{\mathrm{tail}/0}

can hold uniformly with
\Delta_{\mathrm{tail}/0}=0
. The analogous statement holds
for the split excess if the baseline norm is blind to velocity,
pressure-splitting error, or harmonic split coordinates.

Proof.

Assume that such a zero-error estimate holds uniformly. For
\lambda>0
,
set
D_{\lambda}=\lambda H
and use the representative
\zeta_{0}=0
.
Since
0\in\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}
, the quotient distance is bounded by the baseline norm of
this representative:

\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},0}(D_{\lambda},\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}})\leq\|\lambda H\|_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},0}=\lambda\|H\|_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},0}=0.

The tail excess is positively homogeneous in the finite-window coordinate
model, hence

\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{tail}/0}^{(3/2)}(D_{\lambda};0)=\lambda\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{tail}/0}^{(3/2)}(H;0)>0.

The alleged estimate with
\Delta_{\mathrm{tail}/0}=0
gives

\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{tail}/0}^{(3/2)}(D_{\lambda};0)\leq C_{\mathrm{tail}/0}\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},0}(D_{\lambda},\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}})=0,

which is a contradiction. The split-excess statement is the same argument
with
H
chosen in an invisible velocity, pressure-splitting error, or
harmonic split-coordinate direction for which

\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{split}/0}^{(3/2)}(H;0)>0
.
∎

Remark 4.58

(Role of the lemma)
.

This lemma is a sanity check. It prevents the argument from implying that
the older baseline geometry controls pressure-tail data without an explicit
visibility mechanism.

4.8.6
Baseline visibility and finite amplitude

Assumption 4.59

(Same-gauge baseline near-minimizer)
.

For the package
D
, there exists

\zeta_{0}(D)\in\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}
such that

\|D-\zeta_{0}\|_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},0}\leq\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},0}(D,\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}})+\delta_{0}.

The same representative
\zeta_{0}
is used to estimate the baseline
distance and all tail or split excess terms.

Assumption 4.60

(Baseline coordinate visibility)
.

There exist constants
C_{\mathrm{vis}}<\infty
and

\Delta_{\mathrm{vis}}\geq 0
such that, on the same representative

\zeta_{0}(D)
,

\displaystyle\|u_{D-\zeta_{0}}\|_{L^{3}(Q_{1})}+\|E^{\mathrm{cl}}_{F,D-\zeta_{0}}\|_{X_{\mathrm{src}}}+\|p_{\mathrm{harm},D-\zeta_{0}}\|_{Y_{\mathrm{harm}}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\mathrm{vis}}\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},0}(D,\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}})+\Delta_{\mathrm{vis}}.

Assumption 4.61

(Finite amplitude)
.

There is a constant
M_{U}<\infty
such that

\|u_{D-\zeta_{0}}\|_{L^{3}(Q_{1})}\leq M_{U}.

Under the conservative gauge convention
\zeta_{u}=0
, this is simply

\|u_{D}\|_{L^{3}(Q_{1})}\leq M_{U}.

Remark 4.62

(Structural status)
.

Sections

4.8.6
and
4.8.6
are structural
finite-window assumptions. They are not consequences of Navier–Stokes
regularity, and they are not claimed to hold for all suitable weak solutions.

4.8.7
Baseline-to-tail excess bound

Theorem 4.63

(Finite-amplitude baseline-to-tail bound)
.

Assume the same-gauge near-minimizer, baseline visibility, and
finite-amplitude hypotheses from
Section

4.8.6
.
Assume also the pressure-natural harmonic geometry and the fixed
source-to-pressure bound in
Section

4.8
. Then

\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{tail}/0}^{(3/2)}(D;\zeta_{0})\leq C_{\mathrm{tail}/0}(M_{U},C_{\mathrm{vis}})\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},0}(D,\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}})+\Delta_{\mathrm{tail}/0},

where one may take

C_{\mathrm{tail}/0}(M_{U},C_{\mathrm{vis}})=C_{\mathrm{vis}}\max\{C_{\mathrm{CZ}}M_{U},C_{\mathrm{CZ}},1\},

and

\Delta_{\mathrm{tail}/0}=\max\{C_{\mathrm{CZ}}M_{U},C_{\mathrm{CZ}},1\}\Delta_{\mathrm{vis}}.

Proof.

Set

K_{U}:=\max\{C_{\mathrm{CZ}}M_{U},C_{\mathrm{CZ}},1\}.

For the selected representative
\zeta_{0}
, the split source has the form

F^{\mathrm{cl}}_{D-\zeta_{0},ij}=\eta u_{D-\zeta_{0},i}u_{D-\zeta_{0},j}+E^{\mathrm{cl}}_{F,D-\zeta_{0},ij}.

By
Section

4.8
,

\|R_{i}R_{j}(F^{\mathrm{cl}}_{D-\zeta_{0},ij})\|_{Y_{\mathrm{prs}}}\leq C_{\mathrm{CZ}}\left(\|u_{D-\zeta_{0}}\|_{L^{3}(Q_{1})}^{2}+\|E^{\mathrm{cl}}_{F,D-\zeta_{0}}\|_{X_{\mathrm{src}}}\right).

The finite-amplitude assumption gives

\|u_{D-\zeta_{0}}\|_{L^{3}(Q_{1})}^{2}\leq M_{U}\|u_{D-\zeta_{0}}\|_{L^{3}(Q_{1})}.

Therefore

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{tail}/0}^{(3/2)}(D;\zeta_{0})

\displaystyle=\|R_{i}R_{j}(F^{\mathrm{cl}}_{D-\zeta_{0},ij})\|_{Y_{\mathrm{prs}}}+\|p_{\mathrm{harm},D-\zeta_{0}}\|_{Y_{\mathrm{harm}}}

\displaystyle\leq K_{U}\left(\|u_{D-\zeta_{0}}\|_{L^{3}(Q_{1})}+\|E^{\mathrm{cl}}_{F,D-\zeta_{0}}\|_{X_{\mathrm{src}}}+\|p_{\mathrm{harm},D-\zeta_{0}}\|_{Y_{\mathrm{harm}}}\right).

Applying the baseline coordinate visibility assumption gives

\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{tail}/0}^{(3/2)}(D;\zeta_{0})\leq K_{U}C_{\mathrm{vis}}\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},0}(D,\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}})+K_{U}\Delta_{\mathrm{vis}}.

This is the stated estimate.
∎

Remark 4.64

(Status)
.

Theorem

4.63
proves a finite-window implication from
visibility and finite amplitude. It does not prove those hypotheses from the
Navier–Stokes equations.

4.8.8
Baseline-to-split excess bound

Theorem 4.65

(Finite-amplitude baseline-to-split bound)
.

Under the assumptions of
Theorem

4.63
,

\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{split}/0}^{(3/2)}(D;\zeta_{0})\leq C_{\mathrm{split}/0}(M_{U},C_{\mathrm{vis}})\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},0}(D,\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}})+\Delta_{\mathrm{split}/0},

where one may take, up to harmless finite-component constants,

C_{\mathrm{split}/0}(M_{U},C_{\mathrm{vis}})=C_{\mathrm{vis}}\left(1+\max\{C_{\mathrm{CZ}}M_{U},C_{\mathrm{CZ}},1\}\right),

and

\Delta_{\mathrm{split}/0}=\left(1+\max\{C_{\mathrm{CZ}}M_{U},C_{\mathrm{CZ}},1\}\right)\Delta_{\mathrm{vis}}.

Proof.

With the split-support convention used here,

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{split}/0}^{(3/2)}(D;\zeta_{0})

\displaystyle=\|R_{i}R_{j}(F^{\mathrm{cl}}_{D-\zeta_{0},ij})\|_{Y_{\mathrm{prs}}}+\|u_{D-\zeta_{0}}\|_{L^{3}(Q_{1})}

\displaystyle+\|E^{\mathrm{cl}}_{F,D-\zeta_{0}}\|_{X_{\mathrm{src}}}+2\|p_{\mathrm{harm},D-\zeta_{0}}\|_{Y_{\mathrm{harm}}}.

This is the sum of the tail excess and one additional copy of the visible
split-coordinate block:

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{split}/0}^{(3/2)}(D;\zeta_{0})

\displaystyle=\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{tail}/0}^{(3/2)}(D;\zeta_{0})

\displaystyle+\|u_{D-\zeta_{0}}\|_{L^{3}(Q_{1})}+\|E^{\mathrm{cl}}_{F,D-\zeta_{0}}\|_{X_{\mathrm{src}}}+\|p_{\mathrm{harm},D-\zeta_{0}}\|_{Y_{\mathrm{harm}}}.

Let
K_{U}=\max\{C_{\mathrm{CZ}}M_{U},C_{\mathrm{CZ}},1\}
. By

Theorem

4.63
, the first term is bounded by

K_{U}C_{\mathrm{vis}}\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},0}(D,\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}})+K_{U}\Delta_{\mathrm{vis}}.

By baseline coordinate visibility, the second visible block is bounded by

C_{\mathrm{vis}}\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},0}(D,\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}})+\Delta_{\mathrm{vis}}.

Adding the two estimates gives

\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{split}/0}^{(3/2)}(D;\zeta_{0})\leq(1+K_{U})C_{\mathrm{vis}}\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},0}(D,\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}})+(1+K_{U})\Delta_{\mathrm{vis}},

which is the claimed bound.
∎

4.9
Baseline closure after visibility

The preceding section proves the excess bounds that were left as abstract
same-gauge assumptions in the split closure theorem. We now spell out the
resulting baseline forms without referring to any external pressure-tail
module.

Theorem 4.66

(Visibility-driven baseline pressure-tail closure)
.

Assume the same-gauge baseline near-minimizer, baseline coordinate visibility,
and finite-amplitude hypotheses of Section
4.8
.
Assume also that the package class is tail-compatible in the sense of
Definition
4.4
. Then

\displaystyle\dist^{\sharp,\mathrm{tail}}_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},\mathrm{tail}}(D,\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}})

\displaystyle\leq C_{\mathrm{tail}}\bigl[(1+C_{\mathrm{tail}/0})\dist_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},0}(D,\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}})+\delta_{0}+\Delta_{\mathrm{tail}/0}\bigr]

\displaystyle+\alpha_{\mathrm{proj}}\Delta_{\mathrm{proj},N}+\alpha_{\mathrm{harm}}\Delta^{(3/2)}_{\mathrm{harm},M},

where

C_{\mathrm{tail}}=1+\alpha_{\mathrm{proj}}C^{\mathrm{tail}}_{\mathrm{proj}}+\alpha_{\mathrm{harm}}C^{\mathrm{tail}}_{\mathrm{harm}},

and
C_{\mathrm{tail}/0}
and
\Delta_{\mathrm{tail}/0}
may be chosen as in
Theorem
4.63
.

Proof.

Let
\zeta_{0}
be the same-gauge baseline representative. By definition of
the tail excess,

\left\|D-\zeta_{0}\right\|_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},\mathrm{tail}}=\left\|D-\zeta_{0}\right\|_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},0}+\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{tail}/0}^{(3/2)}(D;\zeta_{0}).

The near-minimizer property and Theorem
4.63

give

\dist_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},\mathrm{tail}}(D,\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}})\leq(1+C_{\mathrm{tail}/0})\dist_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},0}(D,\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}})+\delta_{0}+\Delta_{\mathrm{tail}/0}.

Applying the abstract pressure-tail closure theorem, Theorem
4.27
, and substituting this bound gives
the displayed estimate.
∎

Theorem 4.67

(Visibility-driven baseline split-control form)
.

Assume the hypotheses of Theorem
4.65
and the
finite-amplitude split-control hypothesis of Definition
4.6
.
Then the split-control functional satisfies

\displaystyle P_{\mathrm{split}}(D;\zeta_{*})

\displaystyle\leq C_{\mathrm{FA}}(M_{U})\bigl[(1+C_{\mathrm{split}/0})\dist_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},0}(D,\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}})+\delta_{0}+\Delta_{\mathrm{split}/0}\bigr]

\displaystyle+C_{\mathrm{FA}}(M_{U})\delta_{\mathrm{split}}.

Proof.

The finite-amplitude split-control theorem gives

P_{\mathrm{split}}(D;\zeta_{*})\leq C_{\mathrm{FA}}(M_{U})\dist_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},\mathrm{split}}(D,\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}})+C_{\mathrm{FA}}(M_{U})\delta_{\mathrm{split}}.

Using the same-gauge baseline representative
\zeta_{0}
as a competitor in the
split distance and applying Theorem
4.65
,

\displaystyle\dist_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},\mathrm{split}}(D,\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}})

\displaystyle\leq\left\|D-\zeta_{0}\right\|_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},\mathrm{split}}

\displaystyle=\left\|D-\zeta_{0}\right\|_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},0}+\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{split}/0}^{(3/2)}(D;\zeta_{0})

\displaystyle\leq(1+C_{\mathrm{split}/0})\dist_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},0}(D,\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}})+\delta_{0}+\Delta_{\mathrm{split}/0}.

Substitution proves the claim.
∎

Remark 4.68
.

The baseline forms above are still conditional. Their contribution is to make
explicit which visible coordinates are needed to control the return from the
pressure-natural tail geometry to the older baseline geometry.

5
Componentwise residual-ledger module

The second module closes the main residual channels. Its bookkeeping is the fixed-window local-to-clean version of the supply–tax and defect-audit ledgers developed in
[
26
,
27
]
. This is the part of the paper where the local Navier–Stokes bookkeeping is most detailed, and it is the reason the complete proof belongs in an appendix rather than in the middle of the main narrative.

The residual ledger has four branches.

\boxed{\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{comp}}^{[0,K]}=\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{prs}}^{[0,K]}+\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{loc}}^{[0,K]}+\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{rep}}^{[0,K]}+\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{gs}}^{[0,K]}.}

The pressure-source branch controls separated-support commutators and active covariance mismatch. The localization branch controls momentum leakage, localized energy leakage, flux leakage, and annular pressure leakage. The reproduction branch controls active source reproduction, model source reproduction, pressure reproduction, and harmonic pressure reproduction along the finite chain. The gate/slack branch controls positive-part gate violations and slack identity mismatch.

The key point is not merely that each branch has an estimate. The point is that all branches are estimated in one same-chain sharp component geometry. Thus they can be added without changing representatives.

Theorem 5.1

(Componentwise residual-ledger closure)
.

Assume the finite-window package geometry, same-chain representative convention, amplitude bound
M_{U}
, and near-minimizer condition of Appendix B. Then
