\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{comp}}^{[0,K]}(\mathcal{D};\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{*})\leq C_{\mathrm{comp}}^{[0,K]}(M_{U})\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{comp}}^{\sharp,[0,K]}(\mathcal{D},\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{comp}})+C_{\mathrm{comp}}^{[0,K]}(M_{U})\delta_{\mathrm{comp}}^{[0,K]}.

(5.1)

Proof.

The detailed branch estimates are proved in
Appendix

B
. The proof first establishes pressure-source absorption, localization leakage absorption, reproduction drift absorption, and gate/slack absorption separately. In each branch the residual is bounded by the corresponding component coordinate evaluated against the same-chain representative, plus the relevant branch near-minimizer error. The unified component norm is then defined to contain all branch norms with fixed finite-window weights. Adding the four estimates and absorbing the branch constants into
C_{\mathrm{comp}}^{[0,K]}(M_{U})
gives (
5.1
). This is
Theorem

B.136
.
∎

Corollary 5.2

(Weighted residual closure)
.

Under the hypotheses of
Theorem

5.1
, any finite positive weighting of the component residual channels satisfies the corresponding weighted closure estimate, with the constant multiplied by the maximum finite-window weight ratio.

Proof.

This is the weighted version of the same summation argument. Since the number of channels is finite, all positive weights are equivalent up to finite constants. The complete statement is given in Appendix B.
∎

Remark 5.3

(Problem solved by this module)
.

The module turns a list of residuals into one usable theorem. Without it, detector comparison would lose several unrelated error terms and the final theorem would not have a clean form. With the ledger closed, detector comparison loses only
\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{comp}}^{[0,K]}
, which is immediately converted into a sharp component distance plus a finite-chain near-minimizer error.

6
Detector comparison and local-to-clean transfer

The third module is the top interface. It compares the detector evaluated on the localized package with the detector evaluated after the local-to-clean chart. The clean quotient gap inserted at this stage is the structural version of the finite-window computational anti-phantom gap in
[
28
]
. This is where clean-side information enters the localized theorem.

Theorem 6.1

(Detector comparison)
.

Assume the detector-intertwining hypotheses of Appendix C. Then

\displaystyle M_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathcal{D}-\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{*})

\displaystyle\geq M_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{comp}}(\Theta_{\Lambda}(\mathcal{D}-\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{*}))-C_{\mathrm{dc}}\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{comp}}^{[0,K]}(\mathcal{D};\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{*})-\Delta_{\mathrm{dc}}.

(6.1)

Proof.

This is
Theorem

C.14
. The proof decomposes the detector discrepancy into channelwise detector-intertwining errors. Each channelwise discrepancy is bounded by the corresponding residual component plus a finite-window detector error. Summing the finitely many channels gives (
6.1
).
∎

Inserting residual-ledger closure into detector comparison gives the immediately usable form.

Corollary 6.2

(Detector comparison after ledger closure)
.

Under the hypotheses of
Theorems

5.1
and
6.1
,

\displaystyle M_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathcal{D}-\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{*})

\displaystyle\geq M_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{comp}}(\Theta_{\Lambda}(\mathcal{D}-\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{*}))

(6.2)

\displaystyle-C_{\mathrm{dc}}C_{\mathrm{comp}}^{[0,K]}(M_{U})\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{comp}}^{\sharp,[0,K]}(\mathcal{D},\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{comp}})

\displaystyle-C_{\mathrm{dc}}C_{\mathrm{comp}}^{[0,K]}(M_{U})\delta_{\mathrm{comp}}^{[0,K]}-\Delta_{\mathrm{dc}}.

Proof.

Substitute (
5.1
) into (
6.1
). This is the weighted detector comparison recorded as
Section

C.7
in Appendix C.
∎

The final local-to-clean theorem additionally uses the clean gap, chart visibility, and component-to-baseline comparison.

Theorem 6.3

(Conditional local-to-clean transfer)
.

Assume
Sections

3
,
3
and
3
together with
Theorems

5.1
and
6.1
. Then

\displaystyle M_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathcal{D}-\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{*})

\displaystyle\geq\Bigl(\mu_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{comp}}\lambda_{G}-C_{\mathrm{dc}}C_{\mathrm{comp}}^{[0,K]}(M_{U})C_{\mathrm{comp}/0}\Bigr)\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},0}(\mathcal{D},\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}})

(6.3)

\displaystyle-\mathfrak{E}_{\mathrm{pre}}(\mathcal{D}),

where

\displaystyle\mathfrak{E}_{\mathrm{pre}}(\mathcal{D}):=

\displaystyle\Delta_{\mathrm{cl}}+\mu_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{comp}}\Delta_{\mathrm{chart}}+C_{\mathrm{dc}}C_{\mathrm{comp}}^{[0,K]}(M_{U})\Delta_{\mathrm{comp}/0}

\displaystyle+C_{\mathrm{dc}}C_{\mathrm{comp}}^{[0,K]}(M_{U})\delta_{\mathrm{comp}}^{[0,K]}+\Delta_{\mathrm{dc}}.

Proof.

Start from (
6.2
). Apply the clean gap (
3.4
) to the clean detector term. Apply chart visibility (
3.5
) to convert the clean distance into the baseline distance. Apply component-to-baseline comparison (
3.6
) to the negative component-distance term. Collect the coefficient of the baseline distance and place all remaining terms into
\mathfrak{E}_{\mathrm{pre}}
. This is the main transfer step proved in
Theorem

C.23
.
∎

Remark 6.4

(Where pressure tails enter the transfer)
.

In the abstract transfer inequality (
6.3
), the component-to-baseline comparison is stated as a direct input. In applications where component comparison passes through pressure-tail geometry,
Theorem

4.1
is inserted before
Section

3
. The result is the same type of inequality, with
\mathfrak{E}_{\mathrm{pre}}
enlarged by projection-tail, harmonic-tail, and visibility errors.

7
Main finite-window detection theorem

We now state and prove the main theorem in the form used in the introduction.

Theorem 7.1

(Finite-window local-to-clean detection)
.

Fix a finite-window sharp localized Navier–Stokes package
\mathcal{D}
, a same-chain representative
\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{*}
, a local-to-clean chart
\Theta_{\Lambda}
, and a detector channel
\Lambda
. Assume the pressure-tail visibility, componentwise residual-ledger closure, detector comparison, clean gap, chart visibility, component-to-baseline comparison, and positive coefficient hypotheses stated in
Section

3
. Then there are a positive finite-window coefficient
c_{\Lambda,0}>0
and an explicit finite-window error functional
\mathfrak{E}_{\Lambda,0}(\mathcal{D})
such that

M_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathcal{D}-\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{*})\geq c_{\Lambda,0}\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},0}(\mathcal{D},\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}})-\mathfrak{E}_{\Lambda,0}(\mathcal{D}).

(7.1)

Consequently, if

\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},0}(\mathcal{D},\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}})>\frac{\mathfrak{E}_{\Lambda,0}(\mathcal{D})}{c_{\Lambda,0}},

(7.2)

then

M_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathcal{D}-\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{*})>0.

Proof.

The proof is an assembly of the three modules.

First,
Theorem

4.1
gives pressure-tail closure in baseline gauge. Whenever the component-to-baseline comparison uses pressure-tail coordinates, this theorem converts those coordinates into the baseline distance and adds only the explicit tail error

\mathfrak{E}_{\mathrm{tail},0}(\mathcal{D})=C_{\mathrm{tail}}(\delta_{0}+\Delta_{\mathrm{tail}/0})+\alpha_{\mathrm{proj}}\Delta^{\mathrm{unif}}_{\mathrm{proj},N}(\mathcal{A}_{\Lambda})+\alpha_{\mathrm{harm}}\Delta^{(3/2)}_{\mathrm{harm},M},

up to the finite-window constants already included in
C_{\mathrm{comp}/0}
.

Second,
Theorem

5.1
gives

\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{comp}}^{[0,K]}(\mathcal{D};\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{*})\leq C_{\mathrm{comp}}^{[0,K]}(M_{U})\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{comp}}^{\sharp,[0,K]}(\mathcal{D},\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{comp}})+C_{\mathrm{comp}}^{[0,K]}(M_{U})\delta_{\mathrm{comp}}^{[0,K]}.

This converts the residual loss in detector comparison into a component distance and a finite-chain near-minimizer error.

Third,
Theorem

6.1
gives

\displaystyle M_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathcal{D}-\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{*})

\displaystyle\geq M_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{comp}}(\Theta_{\Lambda}(\mathcal{D}-\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{*}))-C_{\mathrm{dc}}\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{comp}}^{[0,K]}(\mathcal{D};\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{*})-\Delta_{\mathrm{dc}}.

Substituting the residual closure yields (
6.2
).

Fourth, the clean gap and chart visibility give

\displaystyle M_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{comp}}(\Theta_{\Lambda}(\mathcal{D}-\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{*}))

\displaystyle\geq\mu_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{comp}}\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{cl}}(\Theta_{\Lambda}\mathcal{D},\Gamma_{\mathrm{cl},\mathrm{adm}})-\Delta_{\mathrm{cl}}

\displaystyle\geq\mu_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{comp}}\lambda_{G}\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},0}(\mathcal{D},\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}})-\Delta_{\mathrm{cl}}-\mu_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{comp}}\Delta_{\mathrm{chart}}.

Fifth, component-to-baseline comparison gives

\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{comp}}^{\sharp,[0,K]}(\mathcal{D},\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{comp}})\leq C_{\mathrm{comp}/0}\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},0}(\mathcal{D},\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}})+\Delta_{\mathrm{comp}/0},

with
C_{\mathrm{comp}/0}
and
\Delta_{\mathrm{comp}/0}
enlarged if the comparison passes through the pressure-tail module.

Collecting the positive and negative coefficients of the baseline distance gives

c_{\Lambda,0}=\mu_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{comp}}\lambda_{G}-C_{\mathrm{dc}}C_{\mathrm{comp}}^{[0,K]}(M_{U})C_{\mathrm{comp}/0}.

By
Section

3
, this number is positive. All remaining terms are placed into

\displaystyle\mathfrak{E}_{\Lambda,0}(\mathcal{D}):=

\displaystyle\Delta_{\mathrm{cl}}+\mu_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{comp}}\Delta_{\mathrm{chart}}+C_{\mathrm{dc}}C_{\mathrm{comp}}^{[0,K]}(M_{U})\Delta_{\mathrm{comp}/0}

\displaystyle+C_{\mathrm{dc}}C_{\mathrm{comp}}^{[0,K]}(M_{U})\delta_{\mathrm{comp}}^{[0,K]}+\Delta_{\mathrm{dc}}+\mathfrak{E}_{\mathrm{tail},0}(\mathcal{D}),

with harmless finite-window constant changes allowed. This proves (
7.1
). The positivity conclusion follows immediately from (
7.2
).
∎

Corollary 7.2

(Vanishing-error finite-window detection)
.

Suppose the hypotheses of
Theorem

7.1
hold along a sequence of finite-window packages
\mathcal{D}_{n}
, and suppose

\mathfrak{E}_{\Lambda,0}(\mathcal{D}_{n})\to 0,\qquad\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},0}(\mathcal{D}_{n},\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}})\geq d_{0}>0.

Then for all sufficiently large
n
,

M_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathcal{D}_{n}-\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{*,n})>0.

Proof.

By
Theorem

7.1
,

M_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathcal{D}_{n}-\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{*,n})\geq c_{\Lambda,0}d_{0}-\mathfrak{E}_{\Lambda,0}(\mathcal{D}_{n}).

The right-hand side is positive for all sufficiently large
n
.
∎

Corollary 7.3

(Contrapositive form)
.

Under the hypotheses of
Theorem

7.1
, if

M_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathcal{D}-\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{*})=0,

then

\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},0}(\mathcal{D},\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}})\leq\frac{\mathfrak{E}_{\Lambda,0}(\mathcal{D})}{c_{\Lambda,0}}.

Thus a localized detector-zero package must be baseline-close to the admissible class up to the explicit finite-window error threshold.

Proof.

This is the contrapositive of (
7.1
).
∎

8
Anti-phantom alternatives and quotient-residual interpretation

The lower bound in
Theorem

7.1
is most useful when read as an anti-phantom alternative. To make this interpretation explicit, enlarge the finite-window error ledger, if necessary, by the synchronization loss:

\mathfrak{E}^{\mathrm{quot}}_{\Lambda,0}(\mathcal{D}):=\mathfrak{E}_{\Lambda,0}(\mathcal{D})+C_{\mathrm{sync}}\Delta_{\mathrm{sync}}(\mathcal{D}).

If the imported quotient geometry provides exact synchronization, then
\Delta_{\mathrm{sync}}(\mathcal{D})=0
. Since this enlargement is nonnegative,
Theorem

7.1
remains valid with
\mathfrak{E}_{\Lambda,0}^{\mathrm{quot}}
in place of
\mathfrak{E}_{\Lambda,0}
.

Corollary 8.1

(Finite-window anti-phantom alternative)
.

Assume the hypotheses of
Theorem

7.1
and suppose
c_{\Lambda,0}>0
. Then every admissible finite-window package
\mathcal{D}
satisfies at least one of the following alternatives:

M_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathcal{D}-\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{*})\geq\frac{c_{\Lambda,0}}{2}\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},0}(\mathcal{D},\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}),

or

\mathfrak{E}^{\mathrm{quot}}_{\Lambda,0}(\mathcal{D})\geq\frac{c_{\Lambda,0}}{2}\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},0}(\mathcal{D},\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}).

Proof.

If the first alternative holds there is nothing to prove. Otherwise,

M_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathcal{D}-\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{*})<\frac{c_{\Lambda,0}}{2}\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},0}(\mathcal{D},\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}).

The main lower bound gives

M_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathcal{D}-\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{*})\geq c_{\Lambda,0}\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},0}(\mathcal{D},\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}})-\mathfrak{E}^{\mathrm{quot}}_{\Lambda,0}(\mathcal{D}).

Combining the two inequalities yields the second alternative, with a non-strict inequality after closure.
∎

Corollary 8.2

(Detector silence forces residual concentration)
.

Under the hypotheses of
Theorem

7.1
, if

M_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathcal{D}-\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{*})=0,

then

\mathfrak{E}^{\mathrm{quot}}_{\Lambda,0}(\mathcal{D})\geq c_{\Lambda,0}\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},0}(\mathcal{D},\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}).

Proof.

Insert
M_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathcal{D}-\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{*})=0
into the main lower bound and rearrange.
∎

Remark 8.3

(Interpretation)
.

A finite-window baseline defect cannot be both detector-invisible and quotient-residual-cheap. Thus detector silence is meaningful only relative to the explicit error ledger. If the ledger is small compared with the baseline defect, the localized detector must be positive.

9
A finite-dimensional pressure-tail quotient model

This section records a reduced model whose role is not to solve the Navier–Stokes equations, but to show that the quotient-geometric hypotheses used in the main theorem are mutually consistent and non-vacuous.

Let

X_{N}=B_{N}\oplus T_{N}^{\mathrm{CZ}}\oplus T_{N}^{\mathrm{harm}}\oplus S_{N}\oplus R_{N}.

Here
B_{N}
is the baseline coordinate space,
T_{N}^{\mathrm{CZ}}
is a finite Calderon–Zygmund pressure-tail space,
T_{N}^{\mathrm{harm}}
is a finite harmonic-tail space,
S_{N}
is a source-coordinate space, and
R_{N}
is a residual-channel space. A typical model package is

D=(b,t^{\mathrm{CZ}},t^{\mathrm{harm}},s,r)\in X_{N}.

One may take
T_{N}^{\mathrm{harm}}
to be spanned by harmonic polynomial modes and
T_{N}^{\mathrm{CZ}}
to be spanned by finitely many Calderon–Zygmund images
R_{i}R_{j}\phi_{\ell,ij}
of source modes. Let

\Gamma_{N}=\{D\in X_{N}:L_{j}D=0,\\
1\leq j\leq J\}

be a finite-dimensional admissible quotient class. Define

\operatorname{dist}_{0}(D,\Gamma_{N}):=\dist(b,\Pi_{B}\Gamma_{N}),

and

\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{tail}}(D,\Gamma_{N}):=\dist((b,t^{\mathrm{CZ}},t^{\mathrm{harm}}),\Pi_{B,T}\Gamma_{N}).

Assumption 9.1

(Finite-dimensional tail visibility)
.

There are constants
C_{N,\mathrm{tail}}<\infty
and
\Delta_{N,\mathrm{tail}}\geq 0
such that

\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{tail}}(D,\Gamma_{N})\leq C_{N,\mathrm{tail}}\operatorname{dist}_{0}(D,\Gamma_{N})+\Delta_{N,\mathrm{tail}}

for every
D
in the reduced model class
\mathcal{A}_{N}
.

Assumption 9.2

(Reduced model detector data)
.

The reduced class
\mathcal{A}_{N}\subset X_{N}
is stable under positive quotient normalization. The baseline unit section

S_{N,0}:=\{D\in\mathcal{A}_{N}:\operatorname{dist}_{0}(D,\Gamma_{N})=1\}

is compact. The reduced detector
M_{N}:\mathcal{A}_{N}\to[0,\infty)
is continuous and positively homogeneous, and is kernel-free on the reduced quotient:

M_{N}(D)=0\quad\Longrightarrow\quad D\in\Gamma_{N}.

Assumption 9.3

(Residual-loss comparison in the model)
.

There are
C_{N}^{\mathrm{res}}<\infty
,
\Delta_{N}\geq 0
, and a nonnegative residual functional
\mathsf{Err}_{N}
such that

M_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{loc}}(D)\geq M_{N}(D)-C_{N}^{\mathrm{res}}\mathsf{Err}_{N}(D)-\Delta_{N}

for every
D\in\mathcal{A}_{N}
.

Theorem 9.4

(Finite-dimensional pressure-tail quotient model)
.

Under
Sections

9
and
9
,

\mu_{N}:=\inf_{D\in S_{N,0}}M_{N}(D)>0.

Consequently, for every
D\in\mathcal{A}_{N}
,

M_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{loc}}(D)\geq\mu_{N}\operatorname{dist}_{0}(D,\Gamma_{N})-C_{N}^{\mathrm{res}}\mathsf{Err}_{N}(D)-\Delta_{N}.

Proof.

The compactness and continuity assumptions give a minimizer
D_{*}\in S_{N,0}
for the infimum. If
\mu_{N}=0
, then
M_{N}(D_{*})=0
, and kernel-freeness implies
D_{*}\in\Gamma_{N}
, contradicting
\operatorname{dist}_{0}(D_{*},\Gamma_{N})=1
. Thus
\mu_{N}>0
. If
r=\operatorname{dist}_{0}(D,\Gamma_{N})>0
, normalize
\widehat{D}=D/r\in S_{N,0}
. Homogeneity gives
M_{N}(D)=rM_{N}(\widehat{D})\geq r\mu_{N}
. Insert this lower bound into the residual-loss comparison. The case
r=0
is immediate from nonnegativity.
∎

Corollary 9.5

(Finite-dimensional anti-phantom alternative)
.

Under the hypotheses of
Theorem

9.4
, every
D\in\mathcal{A}_{N}
satisfies at least one of the alternatives

M_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{loc}}(D)\geq\frac{\mu_{N}}{2}\operatorname{dist}_{0}(D,\Gamma_{N}),

or

C_{N}^{\mathrm{res}}\mathsf{Err}_{N}(D)+\Delta_{N}\geq\frac{\mu_{N}}{2}\operatorname{dist}_{0}(D,\Gamma_{N}).

Proof.

Apply
Theorem

9.4
and argue exactly as in
Section

8
.
∎

An explicit matrix realization

The previous theorem gives a compact quotient criterion. The following elementary realization makes the non-vacuity statement completely explicit: all structural features can occur simultaneously in a reduced pressure-tail model with nonzero Calderon–Zygmund and harmonic tail coordinates.

Proposition 9.6

(Explicit finite-dimensional pressure-tail matrix model)
.

Let
B_{N}=\mathbb{R}^{m}
,
T_{N}^{\mathrm{CZ}}=\mathbb{R}^{p}
,
T_{N}^{\mathrm{harm}}=\mathbb{R}^{q}
,
S_{N}=\mathbb{R}^{s}
, and
R_{N}=\mathbb{R}^{r}
, all with Euclidean norms. Fix linear maps

L_{\mathrm{CZ}}:B_{N}\to T_{N}^{\mathrm{CZ}},\qquad L_{\mathrm{harm}}:B_{N}\to T_{N}^{\mathrm{harm}},\qquad L_{S}:B_{N}\to S_{N},\qquad L_{R}:B_{N}\to R_{N}.

Let

\Gamma_{N}=\{0\}\subset X_{N}

and let the reduced model class be the linear graph

\mathcal{A}_{N}^{\mathrm{mat}}:=\{(b,L_{\mathrm{CZ}}b,L_{\mathrm{harm}}b,L_{S}b,L_{R}b):b\in B_{N}\}.

Define

M_{N}(D):=\|b\|_{B_{N}},\qquad\mathsf{Err}_{N}(D):=\|L_{R}b\|_{R_{N}},\qquad M_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{loc}}(D):=M_{N}(D).

Then
\mathcal{A}_{N}^{\mathrm{mat}}
satisfies finite-dimensional tail visibility, compact baseline unit-section compactness, detector kernel-freeness, and residual-loss comparison. More precisely,

\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{tail}}(D,\Gamma_{N})\leq\bigl(1+\|L_{\mathrm{CZ}}\|^{2}+\|L_{\mathrm{harm}}\|^{2}\bigr)^{1/2}\operatorname{dist}_{0}(D,\Gamma_{N}),

M_{N}(D)=\operatorname{dist}_{0}(D,\Gamma_{N}),\qquad M_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{loc}}(D)\geq M_{N}(D)-\mathsf{Err}_{N}(D),

and the anti-phantom alternative holds with
\mu_{N}=1
,
C_{N}^{\mathrm{res}}=1
, and
\Delta_{N}=0
.

Proof.

For
D=(b,L_{\mathrm{CZ}}b,L_{\mathrm{harm}}b,L_{S}b,L_{R}b)
, the baseline distance to
\Gamma_{N}=\{0\}
is
\operatorname{dist}_{0}(D,\Gamma_{N})=\|b\|_{B_{N}}
. The tail distance is the Euclidean norm of the baseline and tail coordinates:

\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{tail}}(D,\Gamma_{N})=\bigl(\|b\|^{2}+\|L_{\mathrm{CZ}}b\|^{2}+\|L_{\mathrm{harm}}b\|^{2}\bigr)^{1/2}.

The displayed tail-visibility estimate follows from the operator-norm bounds for
L_{\mathrm{CZ}}
and
L_{\mathrm{harm}}
. The unit baseline section is the Euclidean unit sphere in
B_{N}
transported by the graph map, hence compact. Since
M_{N}(D)=\|b\|
, the zero set of
M_{N}
on the graph is exactly
b=0
, which is
D\in\Gamma_{N}
. Thus the detector is kernel-free and the compact quotient gap is
\mu_{N}=1
. Finally, by definition
M_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{loc}}=M_{N}
, so

M_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{loc}}(D)=M_{N}(D)\geq M_{N}(D)-\mathsf{Err}_{N}(D).

This is the residual-loss comparison with
C_{N}^{\mathrm{res}}=1
and
\Delta_{N}=0
, and
Section

9
gives the stated alternative.
∎

Remark 9.7

(Why the matrix model is included)
.

The model is intentionally elementary. Its role is to remove the vacuity concern: the baseline, pressure-tail, residual, and detector components can be chosen so that the quotient gap, tail visibility, residual comparison, and anti-phantom alternative all hold simultaneously. It is not a discretization of the Navier–Stokes equations and carries no scale-uniform information.

Detector-specific inputs in an explicit matrix model

The previous graph model verifies non-vacuity of the baseline, pressure-tail, and residual pieces. The next realization verifies the detector-specific inputs appearing in the main theorem: clean gap, chart visibility, component-to-baseline comparison, residual-ledger closure, detector comparison, and kernel-freeness.

Theorem 9.8

(Explicit detector-input matrix realization)
.

Let
B=\mathbb{R}^{m}
,
T=\mathbb{R}^{p}
,
C=\mathbb{R}^{q}
,
R=\mathbb{R}^{r}
, and
Y=\mathbb{R}^{\ell}
, all with Euclidean norms. Let

A_{T}:B\to T,\qquad A_{C}:B\to C,\qquad A_{R}:B\to R,\qquad G:B\to Y,\qquad H:Y\to\mathbb{R}^{d},\qquad K:R\to\mathbb{R}^{d}

be linear maps. Assume that
G
is injective and that
H
is injective on
G(B)
. Define

\lambda_{G}:=\inf_{\|b\|=1}\|Gb\|>0,\qquad\mu_{H}:=\inf_{y\in G(B),\ \|y\|=1}\|Hy\|>0.

Let the reduced model class be the graph

\mathcal{A}^{\det}_{N}:=\{(b,A_{T}b,A_{C}b,A_{R}b):b\in B\}\subset B\oplus T\oplus C\oplus R,

with gauge class
\Gamma_{N}=\{0\}
. Define

\operatorname{dist}_{0}(D,\Gamma_{N}):=\|b\|,\qquad\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{comp}}(D,\Gamma_{N}):=\|(b,A_{T}b,A_{C}b,A_{R}b)\|,\qquad\mathsf{Err}_{N}(D):=\|A_{R}b\|,

\Theta_{N}(D):=Gb,\qquad M_{N}^{\mathrm{comp}}(\Theta_{N}D):=\|HGb\|,\qquad M_{N}^{\mathrm{loc}}(D):=\|HGb+KA_{R}b\|.

Then the following finite-window structural inputs hold on
\mathcal{A}_{N}^{\det}
:

(a)

Clean gap:

M_{N}^{\mathrm{comp}}(\Theta_{N}D)\geq\mu_{H}\dist_{\mathrm{cl}}(\Theta_{N}D,0),\qquad\dist_{\mathrm{cl}}(\Theta_{N}D,0):=\|Gb\|.

(b)

Chart visibility:

\dist_{\mathrm{cl}}(\Theta_{N}D,0)\geq\lambda_{G}\operatorname{dist}_{0}(D,\Gamma_{N}).

(c)

Component-to-baseline comparison:

\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{comp}}(D,\Gamma_{N})\leq C_{\mathrm{comp}/0}^{N}\operatorname{dist}_{0}(D,\Gamma_{N}),\qquad C_{\mathrm{comp}/0}^{N}:=\bigl(1+\|A_{T}\|^{2}+\|A_{C}\|^{2}+\|A_{R}\|^{2}\bigr)^{1/2}.

(d)

Residual-ledger closure:

\mathsf{Err}_{N}(D)\leq\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{comp}}(D,\Gamma_{N}).

(e)

Detector comparison:

M_{N}^{\mathrm{loc}}(D)\geq M_{N}^{\mathrm{comp}}(\Theta_{N}D)-\|K\|\mathsf{Err}_{N}(D).

Consequently, if

c_{N}:=\mu_{H}\lambda_{G}-\|K\|C_{\mathrm{comp}/0}^{N}>0,

then

M_{N}^{\mathrm{loc}}(D)\geq c_{N}\operatorname{dist}_{0}(D,\Gamma_{N})

with zero additive error. In particular, detector silence forces
D\in\Gamma_{N}
on this graph.

Proof.

For
y=Gb\in G(B)
, the definition of
\mu_{H}
gives
\|Hy\|\geq\mu_{H}\|y\|
, proving the clean gap. The chart visibility estimate is the definition of
\lambda_{G}
. The component comparison follows from

\|(b,A_{T}b,A_{C}b,A_{R}b)\|^{2}\leq\bigl(1+\|A_{T}\|^{2}+\|A_{C}\|^{2}+\|A_{R}\|^{2}\bigr)\|b\|^{2}.

The residual closure is immediate because
\|A_{R}b\|
is a coordinate of the component norm. The detector comparison is the reverse triangle inequality:

\|HGb+KA_{R}b\|\geq\|HGb\|-\|K\|\|A_{R}b\|.

Combining these estimates gives

M_{N}^{\mathrm{loc}}(D)\geq\mu_{H}\lambda_{G}\|b\|-\|K\|\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{comp}}(D,\Gamma_{N})\geq(\mu_{H}\lambda_{G}-\|K\|C_{\mathrm{comp}/0}^{N})\|b\|.

The last expression is
c_{N}\operatorname{dist}_{0}(D,\Gamma_{N})
. If
M_{N}^{\mathrm{loc}}(D)=0
and
c_{N}>0
, the lower bound forces
b=0
, hence
D=0\in\Gamma_{N}
on the graph.
∎

Corollary 9.9

(Concrete scalar kernel-free matrix example)
.

Take
B=T=C=R=Y=\mathbb{R}
and

A_{T}b=b,\qquad A_{C}b=b,\qquad A_{R}b=\rho b,\qquad Gb=b,\qquad Hy=y,\qquad Kr=-\kappa r,

where
\rho>0
and
0\leq\kappa<1/\sqrt{3+\rho^{2}}
. Then

\lambda_{G}=1,\qquad\mu_{H}=1,\qquad C_{\mathrm{comp}/0}^{N}=\sqrt{3+\rho^{2}},\qquad c_{N}=1-\kappa\sqrt{3+\rho^{2}}>0.

The model has nonzero tail coordinate
t=b
and nonzero residual coordinate
r=\rho b
whenever
b\neq 0
. Its detector zero set is exactly the admissible class
\Gamma_{N}=\{0\}
.

Proof.

All maps are scalar, so the injectivity assumptions are immediate. The component graph norm is

\|(b,b,b,\rho b)\|=\sqrt{3+\rho^{2}}\,|b|.

The preceding theorem gives the stated constants and the lower bound
M_{N}^{\mathrm{loc}}(D)\geq c_{N}|b|
. Since
c_{N}>0
, the detector can vanish only when
b=0
, which is precisely the gauge class on the graph.
∎

Remark 9.10

(What the explicit matrix model verifies)
.

Theorems

9.8
and
9
verify the detector-specific inputs that remain structural in the PDE-facing theory: clean quotient gap, chart visibility, detector comparison, residual-ledger closure, component-to-baseline comparison, and kernel-freeness. This verification is finite-dimensional and fixed-window. It proves non-vacuity of the hypothesis package; it is not a statement about arbitrary Navier–Stokes-generated packages.

10
NS-generated coordinate layer and smooth finite-window subclasses

The main theorem is stated for finite-window packages. This section records how local Navier–Stokes data enter the coordinate layer of the package framework and where the genuinely structural assumptions remain. The NS-realizability emphasis follows the defect-cascade and ledger-realizable package viewpoint of
[
25
,
26
,
27
]
.

Let
(u,p)
be local pressure-admissible Navier–Stokes data on a normalized cylinder
Q_{1}=B_{1}\times(-1,0)
, with

u\in L^{3}(Q_{1})^{3},\qquad p\in L^{3/2}(Q_{1}),

and

-\Delta p=\partial_{i}\partial_{j}(u_{i}u_{j})

in distributions on the fixed local window, modulo time-dependent pressure constants. The package-realizability theorem in
Theorem

C.34
shows that such data generate the active source, active Calderon–Zygmund pressure, harmonic pressure coordinate, leakage coordinates, gate/slack coordinates, and finite-chain reproduction coordinates whenever the corresponding finite-window maps are fixed.

Definition 10.1

(NS-generated verification class)
.

For parameters
M,K,N,\eta
, let
\mathfrak{N}_{M,K,N,\eta}^{\mathrm{NS}}
be the class of packages
\mathcal{D}(u,p;z_{0},r_{0},K)
generated by local Navier–Stokes data and satisfying the following finite-window module assumptions:

(i)

the coordinate-domain realizability conclusions of
Theorem

C.34
;

(ii)

compatibility with the imported quotient geometry and the synchronized representative convention;

(iii)

finite amplitude or a declared quadratic component geometry;

(iv)

pressure-tail approximation, either by compact clean pressure image or by an effective projection bound;

(v)

clean quotient gap, chart visibility, component-to-baseline comparison, residual-ledger closure, and detector comparison on the selected package class.

Theorem 10.2

(NS-generated verification-class theorem)
.

If
\mathcal{D}(u,p;z_{0},r_{0},K)\in\mathfrak{N}_{M,K,N,\eta}^{\mathrm{NS}}
, then the hypotheses of the finite-window detection theorem
Theorem

7.1
are satisfied on this package. Therefore

M_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathcal{D}(u,p;z_{0},r_{0},K)-\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{*})\geq c_{\Lambda,0}\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},0}(\mathcal{D}(u,p;z_{0},r_{0},K),\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}})-\mathfrak{E}^{\mathrm{quot}}_{\Lambda,0}(\mathcal{D}(u,p;z_{0},r_{0},K)).

If
c_{\Lambda,0}>0
, the anti-phantom alternative
Section

8
applies.

Proof.

Membership in
\mathfrak{N}_{M,K,N,\eta}^{\mathrm{NS}}
is defined precisely to include the finite-window module hypotheses required by
Theorem

7.1
. Applying that theorem gives the displayed inequality, and
Section

8
gives the alternative.
∎

Definition 10.3

(Smooth finite-window coordinate/compactness subclass)
.

Let
\mathfrak{N}_{M,K,N,\eta}^{\mathrm{smooth}}
be the subclass of NS-generated packages obtained from smooth local Navier–Stokes data with uniform finite-window
C^{m}
bounds, or uniform
H^{s}
bounds with
s
large enough for the compact embeddings used on the fixed window. We also require the chosen finite-window reproduction maps and projections to be bounded on the declared coordinate spaces.

Theorem 10.4

(Smooth packages satisfy the coordinate and compactness layer)
.

Every package in
\mathfrak{N}_{M,K,N,\eta}^{\mathrm{smooth}}
satisfies the coordinate-domain conclusions of
Theorem

C.34
. Moreover, the selected clean source family is precompact in
X_{\mathrm{src}}
, hence its clean pressure image is precompact in
Y_{\mathrm{prs}}
. Therefore the compact clean projection-tail criterion applies.

Proof.

Coordinate realizability is
Theorem

C.34
. Uniform smooth bounds on a fixed bounded window imply precompactness of the selected clean sources in
L^{3/2}
, hence in
X_{\mathrm{src}}
, by Arzela–Ascoli in the
C^{m}
formulation or by Rellich–Kondrachov in the Sobolev formulation. The source-to-pressure map is bounded, so the clean pressure image is precompact in
Y_{\mathrm{prs}}
. The projection-tail conclusion follows from the compact image criterion in
Theorems

C.37
and
C.38
.
∎

Corollary 10.5

(Nonempty smooth coordinate/compactness layer)
.

The smooth coordinate/compactness layer is nonempty, and it contains nonzero local Navier–Stokes data. In particular, for any constant vector
a\in\mathbb{R}^{3}
, the pair

u(x,t)\equiv a,\qquad p(x,t)\equiv 0

on the fixed finite window is a smooth pressure-admissible Navier–Stokes pair. If
a\neq 0
, the generated package has nonzero velocity and nonzero active source coordinates, and the singleton package family satisfies the coordinate-domain and compactness conclusions of
Theorem

10.4
.

Proof.

The constant field is divergence-free, satisfies
\partial_{t}u=0
,
\Delta u=0
, and
(u\cdot\nabla)u=0
. With
p=0
, the Navier–Stokes equations hold on the fixed cylinder. The pressure-admissibility identity also holds, since
\partial_{i}\partial_{j}(a_{i}a_{j})=0
. The finite-window coordinates are therefore realized by
Theorem

C.34
. A singleton family is compact in every declared coordinate space, so the clean-source and pressure-image compactness inputs are satisfied. For
a\neq 0
, the velocity coordinate and the localized active source
\eta a_{i}a_{j}
are nonzero.
∎

Remark 10.6

(Nonemptiness versus full verification)
.

Section

10
proves nonemptiness of the coordinate and compactness layer only. It does not assert the detector-specific kernel-free, chart-visibility, component-budget, or detector-comparison inputs. Those remain the finite-window structural conditions isolated by the main theorem.

Remark 10.7

(Remaining detector-specific inputs)
.

The smooth coordinate layer does not prove clean kernel-freeness, chart kernel-freeness, detector-channel comparison, component-budget visibility, or residual-channel closure. Those remain finite-window detector/model inputs unless they are separately verified for the chosen reduced class.

11
PDE-facing interpretation and limitations

The theorem separates two layers of work. The first layer is the finite-window audit layer. It is closed in this paper: pressure-tail geometry, residual-ledger closure, detector comparison, package-realizability statements, compactness criteria, reduced kernel-free criteria, and an explicit matrix verification of the detector-specific inputs are proved in the text and appendices. The second layer is the PDE-facing layer. It asks whether the same structural hypotheses can be verified for broad concrete package classes generated by Navier–Stokes solutions.

What has been closed

The finite-window bookkeeping no longer has a missing residual channel. Pressure-source residuals, localization leakage, reproduction drift, and gate/slack mismatch all enter the unified residual ledger. The detector comparison no longer loses a collection of unrelated terms; it loses only the closed component residual and a detector-intertwining error. Pressure-tail coordinates no longer float outside the baseline geometry; they are controlled by baseline visibility plus explicit projection and harmonic tail errors.

In this precise sense, the paper completes a finite-window conditional detection mechanism.

What remains structural

The remaining inputs are not bookkeeping estimates. They are mathematical statements about the selected Navier–Stokes-generated class.

The clean anti-phantom gap asks whether the clean detector has a nontrivial kernel on the relevant quotient. Pressure/tax kernel-free criteria in Appendix C give reduced finite-dimensional and compact quotient ways to verify such a statement, but they do not prove it automatically for all possible packages.

Chart visibility asks whether the local-to-clean chart preserves the baseline defect. This is a geometric property of the chart and the selected quotient. If the chart has a large kernel in the baseline direction, no detector comparison theorem can recover the lost defect.

Component-to-baseline comparison asks whether the sharp component distance can be controlled by the older baseline distance. This is where one must be careful about hidden coordinates: component norms are stronger than baseline norms unless the extra coordinates are visible, compact, or otherwise controlled.

Compactness or effective projection of clean pressure sources asks whether finite pressure projections capture the clean pressure image uniformly. Appendix A proves that compactness is sufficient and also explains why boundedness alone is not enough.

Why no Clay-level conclusion follows

A global regularity or singularity-exclusion theorem would require several additional steps not performed here. One would need to show that suitable weak solutions generate packages satisfying the structural hypotheses at all relevant scales, that the constants remain controlled under scale iteration, that the finite-window detection threshold can be propagated or made scale-uniform, and that positive detection contradicts a possible singular cascade. None of these steps is hidden in the present theorem. They are deliberately left outside the finite-window result.

Research path suggested by the theorem

The theorem identifies the next PDE-facing tasks in a clean order.

(i)

Prove clean anti-phantom gaps for reduced or physically generated clean package classes.

(ii)

Prove chart visibility for concrete local-to-clean maps arising from Navier–Stokes localization and pressure splitting.

(iii)

Prove component-to-baseline comparisons without adding invisible coordinates.

(iv)

Replace compact pressure-image assumptions by effective projection or equation-derived compactness criteria.

(v)

Study whether the finite-window constants can be made scale-uniform on a controlled class.

Only after these tasks are addressed would it be appropriate to discuss singularity exclusion or global regularity consequences.

12
Conclusion

The paper proves a fixed finite-window structural theorem for sharp localized Navier–Stokes packages. The theorem can be read in two equivalent ways. As a local-to-clean transfer theorem, it says that clean-side detection transfers to localized detection after pressure-tail, residual-ledger, detector-comparison, chart, and component losses are paid. As an anti-phantom theorem, it says that a baseline-visible finite-window defect cannot be both detector-silent and quotient-residual-cheap.

The result is conditional but complete at the finite-window level. The imported quotient geometry is now recorded through a reference-grade interface, the smooth Navier–Stokes layer supplies genuine coordinate/compactness examples, and the explicit matrix model verifies the detector-specific inputs in a concrete reduced quotient geometry. The remaining challenge is no longer to locate a missing bookkeeping term in the finite-window ledger. The remaining challenge is to verify the same structural inputs for broader Navier–Stokes-generated package classes, and then to determine whether the constants and residual ledgers can be made stable under recursive scale iteration.

A.1
Reference-grade imported quotient geometry module

This subsection records the exact interface through which the earlier finite-window quotient geometry is used in the present paper. The word “imported” is not used as an unspecified black box. It means that the main theorem uses only the following finite list of quotient objects, representatives, distances, and compatibility estimates. Each item is either proved in the present manuscript, realized by the finite-dimensional model below, or explicitly retained as a finite-window structural input for a selected package class.

Definition A.1

(Imported quotient interface)
.

For a fixed finite window
[0,K]
, the imported quotient interface consists of the following data.

(IQG1)

A Banach product package space

\mathcal{X}^{[0,K]}=\prod_{k=0}^{K}\mathcal{X}_{k},

containing the velocity, active pressure, harmonic pressure, source, residual, gate/slack, reproduction, chart, and detector coordinates used below.

(IQG2)

A closed admissible gauge subspace
\mathcal{Z}^{[0,K]}\subset\mathcal{X}^{[0,K]}
and the quotient relation
\mathcal{D}\sim\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
if
\mathcal{D}-\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Z}^{[0,K]}
.

(IQG3)

Local integrated, component, and clean admissible classes

\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}},\qquad\Gamma^{\mathrm{comp}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}},\qquad\Gamma^{\mathrm{cl}}_{\Lambda}.

(IQG4)

The baseline, tail, and component quotient distances

\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},0},\qquad\operatorname{dist}^{\sharp,\mathrm{tail}}_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},\mathrm{tail}},\qquad\operatorname{dist}^{\sharp,[0,K]}_{\mathrm{comp}}.

(IQG5)

A synchronized representative selection
\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{*}(\mathcal{D})\in\mathcal{Z}^{[0,K]}
, with a nonnegative synchronization loss
\Delta_{\mathrm{sync}}(\mathcal{D})
. The same shifted package
\mathcal{D}-\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{*}
is used for baseline near-minimization, pressure-tail excess, component distance, residual ledger, local-to-clean chart, and detector comparison.

(IQG6)

A local-to-clean chart
\Theta_{\Lambda}
and local/clean detector functionals
M^{\mathrm{loc}}_{\Lambda}
and
M^{\mathrm{comp}}_{\Lambda}
, evaluated on the synchronized representative.

Detector comparison

Same-representative local-to-clean detector inequality

Proved from detector-intertwining inputs in
Theorem

C.14
; those detector-specific inputs are verified in the explicit matrix model
Theorem

9.8
.

Remark A.2

(Provenance versus PDE verification)
.

The provenance ledger is a bookkeeping and verification device. It does not claim that every suitable weak solution satisfies the structural quotient inputs. It says exactly which fixed-window objects are imported, which module estimates are proved here, and which inputs must be separately verified on a chosen package class. The explicit matrix model in
Theorem

9.8
verifies the detector-specific structural inputs in a completely concrete reduced quotient setting.

A.2
Uniform clean-source compactness and projection-tail convergence

The baseline pressure-tail closure estimate in

Theorem

4.66
still contains the clean projection
tail. This section records the finite-window functional-analytic mechanism
that turns compactness of the selected clean pressure-source image into
uniform projection-tail convergence.

A.2.1
Clean source and pressure image

In this section

Y_{\mathrm{prs}}=L^{3/2}\bigl((-1,0);L^{3/2}(B_{1/2})\bigr)

and

X_{\mathrm{src}}=L^{3/2}\bigl((-1,0);L^{3/2}(B_{1})\bigr)^{3\times 3}.

For a clean source
F\in X_{\mathrm{src}}
, define

\mathcal{R}(F):=R_{i}R_{j}(F_{ij}),

where
F
is extended by zero outside
B_{1}
before applying the Riesz
transforms and the result is restricted to
B_{1/2}
. We use the fixed
Calderon–Zygmund bound

\|\mathcal{R}(F)\|_{Y_{\mathrm{prs}}}\leq C_{\mathrm{CZ}}\|F\|_{X_{\mathrm{src}}}.

Let

P^{\mathrm{cl}}_{\mathrm{prs},N}:Y_{\mathrm{prs}}\to Y_{\mathrm{prs}}

be finite-rank clean pressure projections such that

P^{\mathrm{cl}}_{\mathrm{prs},N}g\to g\quad\text{in }Y_{\mathrm{prs}}\quad\text{for every }g\in Y_{\mathrm{prs}},

and assume the uniform boundedness condition

C_{P}:=\sup_{N}\|P^{\mathrm{cl}}_{\mathrm{prs},N}\|_{Y_{\mathrm{prs}}\to Y_{\mathrm{prs}}}<\infty.

Definition A.3

(Selected clean-source family)
.

Let
\mathcal{A}_{\Lambda}
be the admissible package class under
consideration, and let
\zeta_{0}(D)
be the same-gauge baseline
near-minimizer from
Section

4.8.6
. Define

\mathcal{F}_{\Lambda,0}:=\left\{F^{\mathrm{cl}}_{D-\zeta_{0}(D)}:D\in\mathcal{A}_{\Lambda}\right\}\subset X_{\mathrm{src}}

and its pressure image

\mathcal{G}_{\Lambda,0}:=\mathcal{R}(\mathcal{F}_{\Lambda,0})=\left\{R_{i}R_{j}(F^{\mathrm{cl}}_{D-\zeta_{0}(D),ij}):D\in\mathcal{A}_{\Lambda}\right\}\subset Y_{\mathrm{prs}}.

The uniform clean projection-tail error is

\Delta^{\mathrm{unif}}_{\mathrm{proj},N}(\mathcal{A}_{\Lambda}):=\sup_{D\in\mathcal{A}_{\Lambda}}\left\|(I-P^{\mathrm{cl}}_{\mathrm{prs},N})R_{i}R_{j}(F^{\mathrm{cl}}_{D-\zeta_{0}(D),ij})\right\|_{Y_{\mathrm{prs}}}.

A.2.2
Boundedness is not enough

Lemma A.4

(No uniform projection-tail decay on arbitrary bounded sets)
.

Let
Y
be an infinite-dimensional Banach space and let

P_{N}:Y\to Y
be finite-rank operators. Even if
P_{N}g\to g
strongly for
each fixed
g\in Y
, one cannot conclude that

\sup_{\|g\|_{Y}\leq 1}\|(I-P_{N})g\|_{Y}\to 0.

Proof.

If the displayed convergence held, then
P_{N}\to I
in operator norm. Each

P_{N}
is finite rank and therefore compact. The operator-norm limit of
compact operators is compact, so the identity
I:Y\to Y
would be compact.
That would make the closed unit ball of
Y
relatively compact. By the
standard Riesz lemma consequence, this is impossible when
Y
is
infinite-dimensional. Hence strong convergence on individual vectors cannot
be upgraded to uniform convergence on arbitrary bounded infinite-dimensional
sets.
∎

Remark A.5

(Consequence)
.

A uniform clean projection-tail theorem requires compactness,
finite-dimensionality, smoothing, translation compactness, or another genuine
approximation mechanism. Finite amplitude and boundedness alone are not
sufficient.

A.2.3
Compact pressure-image hypothesis

Assumption A.6

(Compact clean pressure image)
.

The selected pressure image

\mathcal{G}_{\Lambda,0}\subset Y_{\mathrm{prs}}

has compact closure in
Y_{\mathrm{prs}}
. Equivalently, it is enough to assume that
the selected source family
\mathcal{F}_{\Lambda,0}\subset X_{\mathrm{src}}
has compact
closure, since
\mathcal{R}:X_{\mathrm{src}}\to Y_{\mathrm{prs}}
is bounded and hence continuous.

Remark A.7

(Structural status)
.

Section

A.2.3
is a finite-window compactness
hypothesis. It is not automatic from suitable weak solutions or from a
bounded
L^{3}
velocity norm.

A.2.4
Uniform projection-tail convergence

Theorem A.8

(Uniform convergence on compact pressure images)
.

Assume that
P^{\mathrm{cl}}_{\mathrm{prs},N}\to I
strongly on
Y_{\mathrm{prs}}
, that

\sup_{N}\|P^{\mathrm{cl}}_{\mathrm{prs},N}\|_{Y_{\mathrm{prs}}\to Y_{\mathrm{prs}}}=C_{P}<\infty,

and that
\mathcal{G}_{\Lambda,0}
has compact closure in
Y_{\mathrm{prs}}
. Then

\Delta^{\mathrm{unif}}_{\mathrm{proj},N}(\mathcal{A}_{\Lambda})=\sup_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{\Lambda,0}}\|(I-P^{\mathrm{cl}}_{\mathrm{prs},N})g\|_{Y_{\mathrm{prs}}}\to 0.

Proof.

Let
K=\overline{\mathcal{G}_{\Lambda,0}}
, which is compact by assumption.
Fix
\varepsilon>0
. Choose

\rho=\frac{\varepsilon}{3(1+C_{P})}.

By compactness, there exist
g_{1},\ldots,g_{J}\in K
such that

K\subset\bigcup_{j=1}^{J}B_{Y_{\mathrm{prs}}}(g_{j},\rho).

For each fixed center
g_{j}
, strong convergence gives

\|(I-P^{\mathrm{cl}}_{\mathrm{prs},N})g_{j}\|_{Y_{\mathrm{prs}}}\to 0.

Since there are finitely many centers, choose
N_{0}
such that, for all

N\geq N_{0}
and all
1\leq j\leq J
,

\|(I-P^{\mathrm{cl}}_{\mathrm{prs},N})g_{j}\|_{Y_{\mathrm{prs}}}\leq\varepsilon/3.

Now let
g\in K
, and choose
j
with
\|g-g_{j}\|_{Y_{\mathrm{prs}}}<\rho
. Then

\displaystyle\|(I-P^{\mathrm{cl}}_{\mathrm{prs},N})g\|_{Y_{\mathrm{prs}}}

\displaystyle\leq\|(I-P^{\mathrm{cl}}_{\mathrm{prs},N})(g-g_{j})\|_{Y_{\mathrm{prs}}}+\|(I-P^{\mathrm{cl}}_{\mathrm{prs},N})g_{j}\|_{Y_{\mathrm{prs}}}

\displaystyle\leq(1+C_{P})\rho+\varepsilon/3<2\varepsilon/3.

Taking the supremum over
g\in\mathcal{G}_{\Lambda,0}\subset K
proves the
claim.
∎

Theorem A.9

(Source-level compactness criterion)
.

If the selected clean-source family

\mathcal{F}_{\Lambda,0}\subset X_{\mathrm{src}}

has compact closure in
X_{\mathrm{src}}
, then

\mathcal{G}_{\Lambda,0}=\mathcal{R}(\mathcal{F}_{\Lambda,0})
has compact
closure in
Y_{\mathrm{prs}}
. Consequently

\Delta^{\mathrm{unif}}_{\mathrm{proj},N}(\mathcal{A}_{\Lambda})\to 0.

Proof.

The Calderon–Zygmund estimate makes

\mathcal{R}:X_{\mathrm{src}}\to Y_{\mathrm{prs}}
a bounded linear map, hence a continuous map.
The continuous image of a compact set is compact. Applying

Theorem

A.8
to the compact closure of

\mathcal{R}(\mathcal{F}_{\Lambda,0})
gives the uniform projection-tail
convergence.
∎

A.2.5
Sufficient compactness criteria

Theorem A.10

(Finite-dimensional clean-source model)
.

Assume there is a finite-dimensional subspace

\mathcal{S}_{K}\subset X_{\mathrm{src}}

such that

F^{\mathrm{cl}}_{D-\zeta_{0}(D)}\in\mathcal{S}_{K}\qquad\text{for all }D\in\mathcal{A}_{\Lambda},

and assume the corresponding coefficient set is bounded. Then

\mathcal{F}_{\Lambda,0}
has compact closure in
X_{\mathrm{src}}
, and hence

\Delta^{\mathrm{unif}}_{\mathrm{proj},N}(\mathcal{A}_{\Lambda})\to 0.

Proof.

In a finite-dimensional normed space, bounded sets have compact closure.
Thus the selected source family has compact closure in
\mathcal{S}_{K}
, and
therefore also in
X_{\mathrm{src}}
. The conclusion follows from

Theorem

A.9
.
∎

Remark A.11

(Model status)
.

Theorem

A.10
is a model compactness theorem.
It is useful for finite-window reduced models, numerical quotient models, or
explicitly truncated pressure-source packages. It is not a claim that all
localized Navier–Stokes sources are finite-dimensional.

Theorem A.12

(Strong compactness of velocity and residual source)
.

Let

\mathcal{U}_{\Lambda,0}:=\{u_{D-\zeta_{0}(D)}:D\in\mathcal{A}_{\Lambda}\}\subset L^{3}(Q_{1})^{3}

and

\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{src}}_{\Lambda,0}:=\{E^{\mathrm{cl}}_{F,D-\zeta_{0}(D)}:D\in\mathcal{A}_{\Lambda}\}\subset X_{\mathrm{src}}.

If
\mathcal{U}_{\Lambda,0}
has compact closure in
L^{3}(Q_{1})^{3}
and

\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{src}}_{\Lambda,0}
has compact closure in
X_{\mathrm{src}}
, then

\mathcal{F}_{\Lambda,0}
has compact closure in
X_{\mathrm{src}}
. Consequently

\Delta^{\mathrm{unif}}_{\mathrm{proj},N}(\mathcal{A}_{\Lambda})\to 0.

Proof.

It is enough to prove continuity of the source map

(u,E)\mapsto\eta u_{i}u_{j}+E_{ij}

from
L^{3}(Q_{1})^{3}\times X_{\mathrm{src}}
to
X_{\mathrm{src}}
. If
u_{n}\to u
in

L^{3}(Q_{1})^{3}
, then the sequence
u_{n}
is bounded in
L^{3}
, and for
each
i,j
,

\displaystyle\|\eta(u_{n,i}u_{n,j}-u_{i}u_{j})\|_{L^{3/2}(Q_{1})}

\displaystyle\leq\|\eta\|_{L^{\infty}}\|u_{n,i}\|_{L^{3}(Q_{1})}\|u_{n,j}-u_{j}\|_{L^{3}(Q_{1})}

\displaystyle+\|\eta\|_{L^{\infty}}\|u_{n,i}-u_{i}\|_{L^{3}(Q_{1})}\|u_{j}\|_{L^{3}(Q_{1})}.

The right-hand side tends to zero. If also
E_{n}\to E
in
X_{\mathrm{src}}
, then

\eta u_{n,i}u_{n,j}+E_{n,ij}\to\eta u_{i}u_{j}+E_{ij}\quad\text{in }L^{3/2}(Q_{1}).

The product of the two compact closures is compact, and the continuous image
of this compact product has compact closure in
X_{\mathrm{src}}
. Therefore

Theorem

A.9
applies.
∎

Remark A.13

(Finite amplitude is not compactness)
.

The finite-amplitude bound
\|u\|_{L^{3}(Q_{1})}\leq M_{U}
gives boundedness,
not compactness.
Theorem

A.12
requires
strong compactness, or another compactness mechanism replacing it.

Theorem A.14

(Regularity compactness criterion)
.

Assume the selected source family
\mathcal{F}_{\Lambda,0}
is bounded in a
space compactly embedded into
X_{\mathrm{src}}
. For example, assume that for some

s>0
,

\mathcal{F}_{\Lambda,0}\subset W^{s,3/2}(Q_{1})^{3\times 3}

with a uniform bound. Then
\mathcal{F}_{\Lambda,0}
has compact closure in

X_{\mathrm{src}}
, and hence

\Delta^{\mathrm{unif}}_{\mathrm{proj},N}(\mathcal{A}_{\Lambda})\to 0.

The same conclusion holds under any Kolmogorov–Riesz compactness hypothesis
that gives precompactness in
L^{3/2}(Q_{1})^{3\times 3}
.

Proof.

Since
Q_{1}
is bounded, the Rellich–Kondrachov compactness theorem gives
compact embedding of
W^{s,3/2}(Q_{1})
into
L^{3/2}(Q_{1})
for

s>0
. Thus a uniformly bounded family in the displayed Sobolev space has
compact closure in
X_{\mathrm{src}}
. The projection-tail conclusion follows from

Theorem

A.9
. The Kolmogorov–Riesz variant
is exactly the corresponding compactness criterion in
L^{3/2}
, followed
by the same source-level argument.
∎

Remark A.15

(Status)
.

Theorem

A.14
is a compactness criterion, not
a Navier–Stokes regularity theorem. This paper does not prove the
required regularity or translation compactness from the equations.

A.2.6
Baseline closure with uniform projection tail

Theorem A.16

(Baseline closure with uniform projection tail)
.

Assume the baseline coordinate visibility and finite-amplitude hypotheses
above, the pressure-natural harmonic approximation
theorem, compactness of the selected clean pressure image

\mathcal{G}_{\Lambda,0}
, and strong convergence with uniform boundedness
of
P^{\mathrm{cl}}_{\mathrm{prs},N}
on
Y_{\mathrm{prs}}
. Then, for every

D\in\mathcal{A}_{\Lambda}
,

\displaystyle\operatorname{dist}^{\sharp,\mathrm{tail}}_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},\mathrm{tail}}(D,\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}})

\displaystyle\leq C_{\mathrm{tail}}\bigl[(1+C_{\mathrm{tail}/0})\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},0}(D,\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}})+\delta_{0}+\Delta_{\mathrm{tail}/0}\bigr]

\displaystyle+\alpha_{\mathrm{proj}}\Delta^{\mathrm{unif}}_{\mathrm{proj},N}(\mathcal{A}_{\Lambda})+\alpha_{\mathrm{harm}}\Delta_{\mathrm{harm},M}^{(3/2)}.

Moreover,

\Delta^{\mathrm{unif}}_{\mathrm{proj},N}(\mathcal{A}_{\Lambda})\to 0\quad\text{as }N\to\infty,

and

\Delta_{\mathrm{harm},M}^{(3/2)}\leq C_{\mathrm{harm},3/2}\left(\frac{3}{4}\right)^{M}\|p_{\mathrm{harm},D}\|_{Y_{\mathrm{harm}}}.

Proof.

The baseline pressure-tail closure theorem

Theorem

4.66
gives the same estimate with the
package-wise projection error
\Delta_{\mathrm{proj},N}
. For

D\in\mathcal{A}_{\Lambda}
, the package-wise clean projection tail is
bounded by the supremum defining

\Delta^{\mathrm{unif}}_{\mathrm{proj},N}(\mathcal{A}_{\Lambda})
. This gives
the displayed closure estimate. The convergence of the uniform projection
tail is
Theorem

A.8
. The harmonic tail bound follows from
Section

4.2.2
.
∎

Remark A.17

(Remaining non-explicit inputs)
.

After this theorem, the remaining non-explicit quantities in the finite-window
baseline pressure-tail closure are the baseline visibility constants, the
finite-amplitude constants, and the chosen compactness class. The older
baseline distance alone is not claimed to supply these quantities
automatically.

Corollary A.18

(Finite-window approximation accuracy)
.

Assume the hypotheses of

Theorem

A.16
. If, in addition, the
selected harmonic remainders satisfy a uniform bound

\sup_{D\in\mathcal{A}_{\Lambda}}\|p_{\mathrm{harm},D}\|_{Y_{\mathrm{harm}}}\leq H_{\Lambda}<\infty,

then for every
\varepsilon>0
there exist
N
and
M
such that,
uniformly for
D\in\mathcal{A}_{\Lambda}
,

\alpha_{\mathrm{proj}}\Delta^{\mathrm{unif}}_{\mathrm{proj},N}(\mathcal{A}_{\Lambda})+\alpha_{\mathrm{harm}}\Delta_{\mathrm{harm},M}^{(3/2)}\leq\varepsilon.

Consequently

\displaystyle\operatorname{dist}^{\sharp,\mathrm{tail}}_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},\mathrm{tail}}(D,\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}})

\displaystyle\leq C_{\mathrm{tail}}\bigl[(1+C_{\mathrm{tail}/0})\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},0}(D,\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}})+\delta_{0}+\Delta_{\mathrm{tail}/0}\bigr]+\varepsilon.

Proof.

By
Theorem

A.8
, choose
N
so large
that

\alpha_{\mathrm{proj}}\Delta^{\mathrm{unif}}_{\mathrm{proj},N}(\mathcal{A}_{\Lambda})\leq\varepsilon/2.

The uniform harmonic bound gives

\alpha_{\mathrm{harm}}\Delta_{\mathrm{harm},M}^{(3/2)}\leq\alpha_{\mathrm{harm}}C_{\mathrm{harm},3/2}\left(\frac{3}{4}\right)^{M}H_{\Lambda}.

Choose
M
so large that the right-hand side is at most
\varepsilon/2
.
Substitute these choices into

Theorem

A.16
.
∎

A.3
Conditional local-to-clean assembly

This section assembles the finite-window modules proved above with external clean and local-to-clean transfer inputs. The result is a conditional
baseline local-to-clean detection theorem in the older baseline geometry. No
pressure/tax coercivity, scale-uniform transfer, or Navier–Stokes regularity
is proved here.

A.3.1
Imported assembly inputs

Assumption A.19

(Clean anti-phantom gap)
.

There is a clean detector
M^{\mathrm{comp}}_{\Lambda}
, a clean quotient
distance

\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{cl}}(d,\Gamma^{\mathrm{cl}}_{\Lambda}),

and a constant
\mu^{\mathrm{comp}}_{\Lambda}>0
such that, for every clean
package
d
,

M^{\mathrm{comp}}_{\Lambda}(d)\geq\mu^{\mathrm{comp}}_{\Lambda}\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{cl}}(d,\Gamma^{\mathrm{cl}}_{\Lambda}).

This is imported from the clean finite-window anti-phantom framework and is
not reproved here.

Assumption A.20

(Chart visibility into the clean quotient)
.

There is a local-to-clean map
\Theta_{\Lambda}
and constants

\lambda_{G}>0
,
\delta_{G}\geq 0
such that

\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{cl}}(\Theta_{\Lambda}D,\Gamma^{\mathrm{cl}}_{\Lambda})\geq\lambda_{G}\operatorname{dist}^{\sharp,\mathrm{tail}}_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},\mathrm{tail}}(D,\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}})-\delta_{G}.

The enhanced-tail geometry dominates the older baseline geometry:

\operatorname{dist}^{\sharp,\mathrm{tail}}_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},\mathrm{tail}}(D,\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}})\geq\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},0}(D,\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}).

Consequently,

\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{cl}}(\Theta_{\Lambda}D,\Gamma^{\mathrm{cl}}_{\Lambda})\geq\lambda_{G}\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},0}(D,\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}})-\delta_{G}.

Assumption A.21

(Detector domination)
.

The localized detector
M^{\mathrm{loc}}_{\Lambda}
dominates the clean detector up
to the finite-window residual-budget error:

M^{\mathrm{loc}}_{\Lambda}(D)\geq M^{\mathrm{comp}}_{\Lambda}(\Theta_{\Lambda}D)-\mathrm{Err}_{\Lambda}(D).

This is the imported residual-budget detector interface.

Assumption A.22

(Residual-budget control)
.

There are constants
\eta_{\Lambda}\geq 0
and

\Delta_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{res}}\geq 0
such that

\mathrm{Err}_{\Lambda}(D)\leq\eta_{\Lambda}\operatorname{dist}^{\sharp,\mathrm{tail}}_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},\mathrm{tail}}(D,\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}})+\Delta_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{res}}.

This is a residual-budget assumption, not pressure/tax coercivity.

Definition A.23

(Baseline assembly constants)
.

Define

C_{B}:=C_{\mathrm{tail}}(1+C_{\mathrm{tail}/0})

and, for a package
D
,

B_{N,M}(D):=C_{\mathrm{tail}}(\delta_{0}+\Delta_{\mathrm{tail}/0})+\alpha_{\mathrm{proj}}\Delta^{\mathrm{unif}}_{\mathrm{proj},N}(\mathcal{A}_{\Lambda})+\alpha_{\mathrm{harm}}\Delta_{\mathrm{harm},M}^{(3/2)}(D).

Here

\Delta_{\mathrm{harm},M}^{(3/2)}(D)=C_{\mathrm{harm},3/2}\left(\frac{3}{4}\right)^{M}\|p_{\mathrm{harm},D}\|_{Y_{\mathrm{harm}}}.

The baseline pressure-tail closure theorem gives

\operatorname{dist}^{\sharp,\mathrm{tail}}_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},\mathrm{tail}}(D,\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}})\leq C_{B}\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},0}(D,\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}})+B_{N,M}(D).

A.3.2
Assembly ledger

Module

Input

Output

Error

Clean gap

\mu^{\mathrm{comp}}_{\Lambda}

clean lower bound

0

Chart

\lambda_{G},\delta_{G}

baseline visibility

\mu^{\mathrm{comp}}_{\Lambda}\delta_{G}

Residual

\eta_{\Lambda},\Delta_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{res}}

detector transfer

\eta_{\Lambda}\operatorname{dist}^{\sharp}+\Delta_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{res}}

Baseline closure

C_{B},B_{N,M}

tail by baseline

\eta_{\Lambda}B_{N,M}

Approximation

N,M

explicit tails

\alpha_{\mathrm{proj}}\Delta_{\mathrm{proj}}+\alpha_{\mathrm{harm}}\Delta_{\mathrm{harm}}

A.3.3
Main assembly theorem

Theorem A.24

(Conditional assembly)
.

Assume the clean anti-phantom gap

Section

A.3.1
, chart visibility

Section

A.3.1
, detector domination

Section

A.3.1
, residual-budget control

Section

A.3.1
, and baseline pressure-tail closure with
uniform projection-tail error. Then

M^{\mathrm{loc}}_{\Lambda}(D)\geq c_{\Lambda,0}\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},0}(D,\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}})-\mathfrak{E}_{\Lambda,0}(D;N,M),

where

c_{\Lambda,0}:=\mu^{\mathrm{comp}}_{\Lambda}\lambda_{G}-\eta_{\Lambda}C_{B}

and

\mathfrak{E}_{\Lambda,0}(D;N,M):=\mu^{\mathrm{comp}}_{\Lambda}\delta_{G}+\eta_{\Lambda}B_{N,M}(D)+\Delta_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{res}}.

Equivalently,

\displaystyle\mathfrak{E}_{\Lambda,0}(D;N,M)

\displaystyle=\mu^{\mathrm{comp}}_{\Lambda}\delta_{G}+\eta_{\Lambda}\bigl[C_{\mathrm{tail}}(\delta_{0}+\Delta_{\mathrm{tail}/0})+\alpha_{\mathrm{proj}}\Delta^{\mathrm{unif}}_{\mathrm{proj},N}(\mathcal{A}_{\Lambda})

\displaystyle+\alpha_{\mathrm{harm}}\Delta_{\mathrm{harm},M}^{(3/2)}(D)\bigr]+\Delta_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{res}}.

If
c_{\Lambda,0}>0
, then the local detector has a positive finite-window
lower bound in the older baseline geometry, up to the explicit assembly
error.

Proof.

By detector domination,

M^{\mathrm{loc}}_{\Lambda}(D)\geq M^{\mathrm{comp}}_{\Lambda}(\Theta_{\Lambda}D)-\mathrm{Err}_{\Lambda}(D).

The clean anti-phantom gap gives

M^{\mathrm{comp}}_{\Lambda}(\Theta_{\Lambda}D)\geq\mu^{\mathrm{comp}}_{\Lambda}\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{cl}}(\Theta_{\Lambda}D,\Gamma^{\mathrm{cl}}_{\Lambda}).

Using chart visibility and the monotonicity route into the baseline geometry,

M^{\mathrm{comp}}_{\Lambda}(\Theta_{\Lambda}D)\geq\mu^{\mathrm{comp}}_{\Lambda}\lambda_{G}\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},0}(D,\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}})-\mu^{\mathrm{comp}}_{\Lambda}\delta_{G}.

On the other hand, residual-budget control and

Section

A.3.1
imply

\displaystyle\mathrm{Err}_{\Lambda}(D)

\displaystyle\leq\eta_{\Lambda}\operatorname{dist}^{\sharp,\mathrm{tail}}_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},\mathrm{tail}}(D,\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}})+\Delta_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{res}}

\displaystyle\leq\eta_{\Lambda}C_{B}\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},0}(D,\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}})+\eta_{\Lambda}B_{N,M}(D)+\Delta_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{res}}.

Substituting this upper bound for the residual error into the detector
domination inequality and collecting the baseline-distance coefficient gives
the claimed estimate.
∎

Remark A.25

(Status of the assembly theorem)
.

Theorem

A.24
is a conditional finite-window
assembly theorem. It does not prove pressure/tax coercivity. It does not
prove that the residual-budget assumptions hold for all suitable weak
solutions. It does not prove scale-uniformity or Navier–Stokes regularity.
It only states that, if the clean gap, chart visibility, residual-budget
transfer, baseline visibility, and compact projection-tail hypotheses hold on
the same finite-window class, then the localized detector controls the older
baseline quotient distance up to explicit errors.

Corollary A.26

(Finite-window positive baseline transfer)
.

If

\mu^{\mathrm{comp}}_{\Lambda}\lambda_{G}>\eta_{\Lambda}C_{B},

then
c_{\Lambda,0}>0
. In that case, every package satisfying

\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},0}(D,\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}})>\frac{\mathfrak{E}_{\Lambda,0}(D;N,M)}{c_{\Lambda,0}}

also satisfies

M^{\mathrm{loc}}_{\Lambda}(D)>0.

Proof.

The strict inequality is exactly the statement
c_{\Lambda,0}>0
. Dividing
the lower bound in
Theorem

A.24
by this positive
constant shows that the right-hand side is positive whenever the baseline
distance is larger than the displayed threshold.
∎

Remark A.27

(Detection, not regularity)
.

Section

A.3.3
is a finite-window detection statement:
a non-gauge baseline defect above the explicit finite-window error threshold
must be visible to the localized detector. It is not a regularity theorem.

Corollary A.28

(Finite-window assembly accuracy)
.

Assume the hypotheses of
Theorem

A.24
. Assume
also that the selected clean pressure image is compact and that the selected
harmonic remainders satisfy

\sup_{D\in\mathcal{A}_{\Lambda}}\|p_{\mathrm{harm},D}\|_{Y_{\mathrm{harm}}}\leq H_{\Lambda}<\infty.

Then, for every
\varepsilon>0
, one can choose
N
and
M
such that

\alpha_{\mathrm{proj}}\Delta^{\mathrm{unif}}_{\mathrm{proj},N}(\mathcal{A}_{\Lambda})+\alpha_{\mathrm{harm}}\Delta_{\mathrm{harm},M}^{(3/2)}(D)\leq\varepsilon

for every
D\in\mathcal{A}_{\Lambda}
. Consequently

\displaystyle M^{\mathrm{loc}}_{\Lambda}(D)

\displaystyle\geq c_{\Lambda,0}\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},0}(D,\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}})

\displaystyle-\left\{\mu^{\mathrm{comp}}_{\Lambda}\delta_{G}+\eta_{\Lambda}\bigl[C_{\mathrm{tail}}(\delta_{0}+\Delta_{\mathrm{tail}/0})+\varepsilon\bigr]+\Delta_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{res}}\right\}.

Proof.

The compact pressure image and

Theorem

A.8
give

\Delta^{\mathrm{unif}}_{\mathrm{proj},N}(\mathcal{A}_{\Lambda})\to 0.

The uniform harmonic bound gives uniform convergence of

C_{\mathrm{harm},3/2}\left(\frac{3}{4}\right)^{M}\|p_{\mathrm{harm},D}\|_{Y_{\mathrm{harm}}}

to zero on
\mathcal{A}_{\Lambda}
. Choose
N
and
M
so that the two
weighted approximation errors have sum at most
\varepsilon
, then insert
this bound into
\mathfrak{E}_{\Lambda,0}(D;N,M)
in

Theorem

A.24
.
∎

A.4
Compact quotient pressure/tax detection

This section records a finite-window pressure/tax coercivity criterion. The
previous section assembled the clean gap, chart visibility, residual-budget
transfer, baseline visibility, and projection-tail uniformity into the
conditional lower bound

M^{\mathrm{loc}}_{\Lambda}(D)\geq c_{\Lambda,0}\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},0}(D,\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}})-\mathfrak{E}_{\Lambda,0}(D;N,M).

The present section asks a different finite-window question: when does a
normalized pressure/tax detector itself see non-gauge baseline defects? No
scale-uniform estimate, singularity exclusion, or Navier–Stokes regularity
claim is made.

A.4.1
Motivation and endpoint

We use the older baseline quotient distance

\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},0}(D,\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}})=\inf_{\zeta\in\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}}\|D-\zeta\|_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},0},

with the conservative admissible gauge convention
\zeta_{u}=0
. The
baseline visibility and finite-amplitude results above control the
pressure-tail and split excess on a same-gauge representative, while

Theorem

A.8
gives uniform clean
projection-tail convergence under compactness of the selected clean pressure
image. The endpoint for this section is

Theorem

A.24
; it is not reproved here.

A.4.2
Normalized pressure/tax detector

Definition A.29

(Normalized pressure/tax detector)
.

Fix nonnegative finite-window weights

\beta_{\mathrm{rep}},\quad\beta_{\mathrm{prs}},\quad\beta_{\mathrm{flux}},\quad\beta_{\mathrm{gate}},\quad\beta_{\mathrm{slack}}.

When a channel is used in a kernel-free coercivity theorem below, its weight
is assumed positive; zero-weight channels are retained only as bookkeeping
coordinates unless an explicit detector-zero-set assumption is imposed.
The normalized pressure/tax detector is the nonnegative functional

\displaystyle\mathfrak{M}^{\mathrm{tax}}_{\Lambda}(D)

\displaystyle:=\|O^{0}_{\Lambda}D\|_{\mathcal{O}}+\beta_{\mathrm{rep}}\operatorname{Rep}_{\Lambda}(D)+\beta_{\mathrm{prs}}\operatorname{Tax}^{\mathrm{prs}}_{\Lambda}(D)

\displaystyle+\beta_{\mathrm{flux}}\operatorname{Tax}^{\mathrm{flux}}_{\Lambda}(D)+\beta_{\mathrm{gate}}\operatorname{Tax}^{\mathrm{gate}}_{\Lambda}(D)+\beta_{\mathrm{slack}}\operatorname{Tax}^{\mathrm{slack}}_{\Lambda}(D).

Here
\|O^{0}_{\Lambda}D\|_{\mathcal{O}}
is the older-baseline observable
part of the localized package,
\operatorname{Rep}_{\Lambda}
measures
finite-window reproduction drift,
\operatorname{Tax}^{\mathrm{prs}}_{\Lambda}

measures pressure-source or pressure-tail cost,

\operatorname{Tax}^{\mathrm{flux}}_{\Lambda}
measures nonlinear flux or
cutoff leakage cost, and

\operatorname{Tax}^{\mathrm{gate}}_{\Lambda}
and

\operatorname{Tax}^{\mathrm{slack}}_{\Lambda}
measure gate, slack, or
admissibility-threshold failures.

Remark A.30

(Accounting status)
.

\mathfrak{M}^{\mathrm{tax}}_{\Lambda}
is a normalized finite-window
accounting object. Its components are nonnegative. This paper does not
claim that any individual component is automatically coercive for
Navier–Stokes solutions.

Definition A.31

(Normalized tax class)
.

Let
\mathcal{A}^{\mathrm{tax}}_{\Lambda}
be a finite-window admissible class
of packages satisfying the pressure-natural harmonic geometry, the
conservative admissible gauge convention, the baseline visibility
hypotheses, the finite-amplitude bound, a compact clean pressure image or
another projection-tail uniformity mechanism, and finiteness of all detector
components in
Section

A.4.2
. When compactness
of the quotient is needed below, it is assumed explicitly: the normalized
quotient

\mathcal{A}^{\mathrm{tax}}_{\Lambda}/\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}

has compact unit sphere in the older baseline quotient distance, or the class
is a finite-dimensional reduced model with the same property.

Remark A.32

(No automatic compactness)
.

The compact quotient property in
Section

A.4.2
is a
finite-window structural assumption. It is not derived from suitable weak
solutions, bounded
L^{3}
velocity, or the Navier–Stokes equations here.

A.4.3
No-free-coercivity lemma

Lemma A.33

(No pressure/tax coercivity without kernel exclusion)
.

Suppose there exists a non-gauge direction
H
such that

\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},0}(H,\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}})>0,

but

O^{0}_{\Lambda}H=0,\qquad\operatorname{Rep}_{\Lambda}(H)=0,

and

\operatorname{Tax}^{\mathrm{prs}}_{\Lambda}(H)=\operatorname{Tax}^{\mathrm{flux}}_{\Lambda}(H)=\operatorname{Tax}^{\mathrm{gate}}_{\Lambda}(H)=\operatorname{Tax}^{\mathrm{slack}}_{\Lambda}(H)=0.

Assume these detector components and the baseline quotient distance are
positively homogeneous along the ray
\{\lambda H:\lambda\geq 0\}
. Then no
zero-error coercive estimate

\mathfrak{M}^{\mathrm{tax}}_{\Lambda}(D)\geq\kappa\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},0}(D,\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}})

can hold with
\kappa>0
on any class containing this ray.

Proof.

For
\lambda>0
, set
D=\lambda H
. By the vanishing assumptions and
positive homogeneity of the detector components,

\mathfrak{M}^{\mathrm{tax}}_{\Lambda}(\lambda H)=0.

By positive homogeneity of the quotient distance,

\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},0}(\lambda H,\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}})=\lambda\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},0}(H,\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}})>0.

The alleged estimate would therefore give

0=\mathfrak{M}^{\mathrm{tax}}_{\Lambda}(\lambda H)\geq\kappa\lambda\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},0}(H,\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}})>0,

a contradiction.
∎

Remark A.34

(Purpose)
.

Section

A.4.3
is a sanity check. A
positive pressure/tax coercivity theorem requires a kernel-free or visibility
assumption; it cannot be obtained merely by naming tax terms.

A.4.4
Pressure/tax kernel and kernel-free condition

Definition A.35

(Normalized pressure/tax kernel)
.

The normalized pressure/tax kernel is

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}^{\mathrm{tax}}_{\Lambda}:=\{\,D\in\mathcal{A}^{\mathrm{tax}}_{\Lambda}:

\displaystyle O^{0}_{\Lambda}D=0,\ \operatorname{Rep}_{\Lambda}(D)=0,\ \operatorname{Tax}^{\mathrm{prs}}_{\Lambda}(D)=0,

\displaystyle\operatorname{Tax}^{\mathrm{flux}}_{\Lambda}(D)=0,\ \operatorname{Tax}^{\mathrm{gate}}_{\Lambda}(D)=0,\ \operatorname{Tax}^{\mathrm{slack}}_{\Lambda}(D)=0\,\}.

The pressure/tax kernel-free condition is

\mathcal{K}^{\mathrm{tax}}_{\Lambda}\subset\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}.

Equivalently, simultaneous vanishing of the observation, reproduction, and
tax components implies that the package is an admissible gauge direction.

Remark A.36

(Analogy with the clean gap)
.

The kernel-free condition is the finite-window pressure/tax analogue of the
clean anti-phantom kernel-free condition. It is an assumption in this
section, not a theorem about all localized Navier–Stokes packages.

A.4.5
Compact quotient coercivity theorem

Theorem A.37

(Compact quotient pressure/tax coercivity)
.

Assume that:

(i)

\mathcal{A}^{\mathrm{tax}}_{\Lambda}
is stable under admissible
quotient normalization and positive scalar normalization;

(ii)

the unit baseline quotient sphere

S_{\Lambda,0}:=\{D\in\mathcal{A}^{\mathrm{tax}}_{\Lambda}:\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},0}(D,\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}})=1\}

is compact modulo the admissible gauge;

(iii)

\mathfrak{M}^{\mathrm{tax}}_{\Lambda}
is lower semicontinuous on
this quotient;

(iv)

\mathfrak{M}^{\mathrm{tax}}_{\Lambda}
is positively homogeneous
under the quotient normalization; and

(v)

the pressure/tax kernel-free condition holds, and every detector
channel appearing in
\mathcal{K}^{\mathrm{tax}}_{\Lambda}
has positive
weight in
\mathfrak{M}^{\mathrm{tax}}_{\Lambda}
. Equivalently, one may
replace this positivity requirement by the explicit zero-set condition

\{D\in\mathcal{A}^{\mathrm{tax}}_{\Lambda}:\mathfrak{M}^{\mathrm{tax}}_{\Lambda}(D)=0\}\subset\mathcal{K}^{\mathrm{tax}}_{\Lambda}.

Then

\mu^{\mathrm{tax}}_{\Lambda}:=\inf_{D\in S_{\Lambda,0}}\mathfrak{M}^{\mathrm{tax}}_{\Lambda}(D)>0.

Consequently, for every
D\in\mathcal{A}^{\mathrm{tax}}_{\Lambda}
,

\mathfrak{M}^{\mathrm{tax}}_{\Lambda}(D)\geq\mu^{\mathrm{tax}}_{\Lambda}\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},0}(D,\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}).

Proof.

If the infimum on
S_{\Lambda,0}
were zero, compactness modulo gauge would
give a minimizing sequence converging, after passing to quotient
representatives, to some
D_{*}\in S_{\Lambda,0}
. Lower
semicontinuity gives

\mathfrak{M}^{\mathrm{tax}}_{\Lambda}(D_{*})=0.

By the positive-weight hypothesis, or equivalently by the displayed zero-set
condition, vanishing of
\mathfrak{M}^{\mathrm{tax}}_{\Lambda}
implies that
all channels defining
\mathcal{K}^{\mathrm{tax}}_{\Lambda}
vanish. Thus

D_{*}\in\mathcal{K}^{\mathrm{tax}}_{\Lambda}
. By the kernel-free
condition,
D_{*}\in\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}
. This contradicts

\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},0}(D_{*},\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}})=1.

Therefore
\mu^{\mathrm{tax}}_{\Lambda}>0
on the unit quotient sphere.

Now let
D\in\mathcal{A}^{\mathrm{tax}}_{\Lambda}
. If

\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},0}(D,\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}})=0
, the desired estimate is trivial.
Otherwise set

r:=\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},0}(D,\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}})>0

and normalize the quotient representative by
r^{-1}
. Stability under
normalization gives
r^{-1}D\in S_{\Lambda,0}
modulo gauge. Positive
homogeneity gives

r^{-1}\mathfrak{M}^{\mathrm{tax}}_{\Lambda}(D)=\mathfrak{M}^{\mathrm{tax}}_{\Lambda}(r^{-1}D)\geq\mu^{\mathrm{tax}}_{\Lambda}.

Multiplying by
r
proves the estimate.
∎

Remark A.38

(Finite-window status)
.

Theorem

A.37
is a compactness and
kernel-freeness theorem on a fixed finite-window quotient. It is not a
scale-uniform Navier–Stokes coercivity theorem.

A.4.6
Additive-error coercivity theorem

Theorem A.39

(Additive-error pressure/tax coercivity)
.

Assume there is an exact model detector

\widetilde{\mathfrak{M}}^{\mathrm{tax}}_{\Lambda}
satisfying

Theorem

A.37
with gap

\mu^{\mathrm{tax}}_{\Lambda}>0
. Assume also that the realized detector
satisfies

\mathfrak{M}^{\mathrm{tax}}_{\Lambda}(D)+\Delta^{\mathrm{model}}_{\Lambda}\geq\widetilde{\mathfrak{M}}^{\mathrm{tax}}_{\Lambda}(D)

for every package in the class. Then

\mathfrak{M}^{\mathrm{tax}}_{\Lambda}(D)\geq\mu^{\mathrm{tax}}_{\Lambda}\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},0}(D,\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}})-\Delta^{\mathrm{model}}_{\Lambda}.

Proof.

The exact model coercivity theorem gives

\widetilde{\mathfrak{M}}^{\mathrm{tax}}_{\Lambda}(D)\geq\mu^{\mathrm{tax}}_{\Lambda}\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},0}(D,\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}).

Combining this lower bound with

\mathfrak{M}^{\mathrm{tax}}_{\Lambda}(D)\geq\widetilde{\mathfrak{M}}^{\mathrm{tax}}_{\Lambda}(D)-\Delta^{\mathrm{model}}_{\Lambda}

gives the claim.
∎

A.4.7
Sufficient kernel-free criteria

Proposition A.40

(Observation-plus-tax injectivity)
.

If the combined map

D\mapsto\bigl(O^{0}_{\Lambda}D,\,\operatorname{Rep}_{\Lambda}(D),\,\operatorname{Tax}^{\mathrm{prs}}_{\Lambda}(D),\,\operatorname{Tax}^{\mathrm{flux}}_{\Lambda}(D),\,\operatorname{Tax}^{\mathrm{gate}}_{\Lambda}(D),\,\operatorname{Tax}^{\mathrm{slack}}_{\Lambda}(D)\bigr)

is injective on the quotient

\mathcal{A}^{\mathrm{tax}}_{\Lambda}/\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}
, then the pressure/tax
kernel-free condition holds.

Proof.

If all components vanish on
D
, then
D
and the zero gauge
class have the same combined image. Injectivity on the quotient implies
that
D
lies in the admissible gauge class.
∎

Proposition A.41

(Finite-dimensional matrix criterion)
.

In a finite-dimensional reduced package model, suppose the combined
observation, reproduction, and tax map is represented by a finite matrix or a
finite family of nonnegative coordinate functionals
\mathcal{T}_{\Lambda}
.
Then the pressure/tax kernel-free condition is equivalent to

\ker\mathcal{T}_{\Lambda}=\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}

modulo the chosen finite-dimensional coordinates.

Proof.

In finite dimensions, the kernel of the combined detector map is exactly the
set on which all observation, reproduction, and tax components vanish. Thus
the kernel-free condition is precisely the statement that this nullspace is
the admissible gauge subspace.
∎

Proposition A.42

(Gate/slack intersection criterion)
.

Assume that the intersection of the vanishing sets satisfies

\displaystyle\ker O^{0}_{\Lambda}\cap\ker\operatorname{Rep}_{\Lambda}\cap\ker\operatorname{Tax}^{\mathrm{prs}}_{\Lambda}\cap\ker\operatorname{Tax}^{\mathrm{flux}}_{\Lambda}

\displaystyle\cap\ker\operatorname{Tax}^{\mathrm{gate}}_{\Lambda}\cap\ker\operatorname{Tax}^{\mathrm{slack}}_{\Lambda}\subset\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}.

Then the pressure/tax kernel-free condition holds.

Proof.

The displayed intersection is exactly the set of packages for which every
component in the normalized pressure/tax detector vanishes. Containment in

\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}
is the kernel-free condition.
∎

Remark A.43

(Use of the criteria)
.

The preceding propositions are usable finite-window criteria. They do not
verify kernel-freeness for all Navier–Stokes packages.

A.4.8
Coercivity combined with the assembly theorem

Theorem A.44

(Pressure/tax route to baseline detection)
.

Assume additive-error pressure/tax coercivity:

\mathfrak{M}^{\mathrm{tax}}_{\Lambda}(D)\geq\mu^{\mathrm{tax}}_{\Lambda}\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},0}(D,\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}})-\Delta^{\mathrm{model}}_{\Lambda}.

Assume also that the localized detector dominates the normalized tax detector
up to a finite-window detector-comparison error:

M^{\mathrm{loc}}_{\Lambda}(D)+\Delta^{\mathrm{det}}_{\Lambda}\geq a_{\Lambda}\mathfrak{M}^{\mathrm{tax}}_{\Lambda}(D)

for some
a_{\Lambda}>0
. Then

M^{\mathrm{loc}}_{\Lambda}(D)+\Delta^{\mathrm{det}}_{\Lambda}\geq a_{\Lambda}\mu^{\mathrm{tax}}_{\Lambda}\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},0}(D,\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}})-a_{\Lambda}\Delta^{\mathrm{model}}_{\Lambda}.

Proof.

Multiply the pressure/tax coercivity estimate by
a_{\Lambda}
and use the
detector-comparison inequality.
∎

Remark A.45

(Supplementary route)
.

Theorem

A.44
is a supplementary finite-window
route to baseline detection. It does not replace

Theorem

A.24
unless the detector comparison is
also assumed. Both routes remain conditional.

A.4.9
Detection threshold and interpretation

Corollary A.46

(Pressure/tax detection threshold)
.

Assume

\mathfrak{M}^{\mathrm{tax}}_{\Lambda}(D)\geq\mu^{\mathrm{tax}}_{\Lambda}\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},0}(D,\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}})-\Delta^{\mathrm{model}}_{\Lambda}

with
\mu^{\mathrm{tax}}_{\Lambda}>0
. If

\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},0}(D,\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}})>\frac{\Delta^{\mathrm{model}}_{\Lambda}}{\mu^{\mathrm{tax}}_{\Lambda}},

then

\mathfrak{M}^{\mathrm{tax}}_{\Lambda}(D)>0.

Proof.

Substituting the displayed strict inequality into the additive-error
coercivity bound gives a positive lower bound for

\mathfrak{M}^{\mathrm{tax}}_{\Lambda}(D)
.
∎

Remark A.47

(Detection, not regularity)
.

Section

A.4.9
says that a non-gauge baseline
defect above the finite-window modeling threshold must pay a positive
normalized pressure/tax cost. This is detection, not regularity.

A.4.10
Status and limitations

This normalized pressure/tax coercivity branch proves only a finite-window
compact quotient theorem. The positive gap depends on compactness,
homogeneity, lower semicontinuity, and kernel-freeness assumptions. The
paper does not prove that these assumptions hold for all suitable weak
solutions. It does not derive kernel-freeness from the Navier–Stokes
equations. It does not prove scale-uniformity. It does not prove
Navier–Stokes regularity. It does not solve the Clay problem.

A.5
Logical status and remaining PDE-facing inputs

The present paper is complete as a finite-window conditional framework. All
quantities appearing in the pressure-tail, baseline-visibility, projection-tail,
assembly, and pressure/tax detection estimates have been defined, and the
functional-analytic approximation mechanisms used in the estimates have been
proved in the fixed geometry. The remaining inputs are not proof gaps inside
the finite-window theorems; they are structural hypotheses that would need to be
established in a genuinely PDE-facing continuation.

(i)

Finite amplitude.
Several estimates use

\left\|u_{D}\right\|_{L^{3}(Q_{1})}\leq M_{U}
on the conservative representative in order
to linearize the quadratic pressure source. This paper does not derive
such a bound from the Navier–Stokes equations.

(ii)

Baseline visibility.
The older baseline norm does not
automatically control velocity amplitude, clean residual source, or
harmonic pressure coordinates. Baseline coordinate visibility is an
explicit same-gauge assumption.

(iii)

Clean-source compactness.
Uniform projection-tail
