convergence requires compactness, finite-dimensionality, smoothing, or a
comparable approximation mechanism. Boundedness of a Navier–Stokes source
family is not enough.

(iv)

Residual-budget transfer.
The local-to-clean assembly
theorem assumes a residual-budget comparison between the localized detector
and the clean detector. This is treated as a structural input in the finite-window theorem.

(v)

Pressure/tax kernel-freeness.
The compact quotient
pressure/tax result proves that kernel-freeness implies a positive
finite-window gap. It does not prove that the kernel-free condition holds
for all localized Navier–Stokes packages.

(vi)

Scale-uniformity.
Every estimate is fixed-window. The
paper does not prove scale-uniform moving-window control or a
regularity theorem.

The natural next steps are therefore sharply separated: prove finite-amplitude
removal or weakening, derive baseline visibility in concrete localized
classes, obtain compactness or effective approximation of Navier–Stokes
pressure-source images, verify residual-budget transfer, and only then study
scale-uniform propagation.

Appendix B
Componentwise Residual-Ledger Closure: Detailed Proofs

Componentwise Residual-Ledger Closure

B.1
Global Notation and Scope

All cylinders, cutoff functions, reproduction maps, observation norms, and gauge classes are fixed on finite windows. Constants denoted by
C
may change from line to line. Constants such as
C_{\mathrm{prs}}(M_{U})
,
C_{\mathrm{loc}}(M_{U})
,
C_{\mathrm{rep}}(M_{U})
, and
C_{\mathrm{comp}}^{[0,K]}(M_{U})
are finite-window constants; they may depend on the chosen geometry, on cutoff and reproduction maps, on Calderon–Zygmund or harmonic-estimate constants, on the finite amplitude bound
M_{U}
, and, in the final ledger, on the chain length
K
. No scale-uniform or infinite-chain estimate is asserted.

A “same representative” statement always means that all residual coordinates in the relevant estimate are evaluated at one selected gauge representative. The quotient-distance estimates are obtained by combining representative-form residual bounds with a near-minimizer condition for that same representative. This convention is essential: independently minimizing different residual channels over different gauge representatives does not produce a single residual ledger.

Pressure-Source Residual Absorption

B.2
Normalized Local NS Data and Pressure Splitting

B.2.1
Fixed local geometry

Throughout the paper,

Q_{1}=B_{1}\times(-1,0),\qquad B_{1/2}\subset B_{3/4}\subset B_{1}.

We fix a cutoff

\eta\in C_{c}^{\infty}(B_{1}),\qquad 0\leq\eta\leq 1,\qquad\eta\equiv 1\quad\text{on }B_{3/4}.

The source and pressure observation spaces are

X_{\mathrm{src}}=L^{3/2}\bigl((-1,0);L^{3/2}(B_{1})\bigr)^{3\times 3},

Y_{\mathrm{prs}}=L^{3/2}\bigl((-1,0);L^{3/2}(B_{1/2})\bigr),

and the pressure-natural harmonic space is

Y_{\mathrm{harm}}^{(3/2)}=L^{3/2}\bigl((-1,0);L^{3/2}(B_{3/4})\bigr).

Convention B.1

(Fixed source-to-pressure estimate)
.

For sources extended by zero outside
B_{1}
, the finite-window pressure model
uses the estimate

\|R_{i}R_{j}(F_{ij})\|_{Y_{\mathrm{prs}}}\leq C_{CZ}\|F\|_{X_{\mathrm{src}}}.

No scale-uniform estimate is claimed.

Definition B.2

(Pressure-admissible local data)
.

A pair
(u,p)
on
Q_{1}
is pressure-admissible if

u\in L^{3}(Q_{1})^{3},\qquad p\in L^{3/2}(Q_{1}),

and, for almost every
t\in(-1,0)
,

-\Delta p(t,\cdot)=\partial_{i}\partial_{j}(u_{i}u_{j})(t,\cdot)

in the sense of distributions on
B_{1}
. The pressure is understood modulo
time-dependent constants.

Definition B.3

(Localized active pressure)
.

For pressure-admissible data, define

F^{\mathrm{act}}_{ij}:=\eta u_{i}u_{j},\qquad p^{\mathrm{act}}:=R_{i}R_{j}(F^{\mathrm{act}}_{ij}),

with
F^{\mathrm{act}}
extended by zero outside
B_{1}
. The harmonic pressure
coordinate is

p_{\mathrm{harm}}:=p-p^{\mathrm{act}}\quad\text{on }B_{3/4}\times(-1,0).

Proposition B.4

(Pressure splitting and active pressure bound)
.

For pressure-admissible data,

p^{\mathrm{act}}\in L^{3/2}\bigl((-1,0);L^{3/2}(B_{1/2})\bigr)

and

\|p^{\mathrm{act}}\|_{Y_{\mathrm{prs}}}\leq C_{CZ}\|\eta u_{i}u_{j}\|_{X_{\mathrm{src}}}\leq C_{CZ}\|u\|_{L^{3}(Q_{1})}^{2}.

Moreover, for almost every
t\in(-1,0)
,

-\Delta p^{\mathrm{act}}(t,\cdot)=\partial_{i}\partial_{j}(u_{i}u_{j})(t,\cdot)\quad\text{in }\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(B_{3/4}),

and therefore
p_{\mathrm{harm}}=p-p^{\mathrm{act}}
is harmonic in
B_{3/4}
for almost
every time.

Proof.

For almost every fixed time,
u_{i}u_{j}\in L^{3/2}(B_{1})
, and therefore

F^{\mathrm{act}}_{ij}=\eta u_{i}u_{j}
, extended by zero outside
B_{1}
, belongs to

L^{3/2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
. By Convention
B.2.1
,

\|p^{\mathrm{act}}\|_{Y_{\mathrm{prs}}}=\|R_{i}R_{j}(F^{\mathrm{act}}_{ij})\|_{Y_{\mathrm{prs}}}\leq C_{CZ}\|F^{\mathrm{act}}\|_{X_{\mathrm{src}}}.

Since
0\leq\eta\leq 1
, Holder’s inequality gives

\|F^{\mathrm{act}}\|_{X_{\mathrm{src}}}\leq C\|u\otimes u\|_{L^{3/2}(Q_{1})}\leq C\|u\|_{L^{3}(Q_{1})}^{2}.

The harmless finite-component constant is absorbed into
C_{CZ}
.

It remains to verify the local Poisson equation. With the usual Fourier
normalization of the Riesz transforms,

-\Delta R_{i}R_{j}F_{ij}=\partial_{i}\partial_{j}F_{ij}\quad\text{in }\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{3}).

Let
\varphi\in C_{c}^{\infty}(B_{3/4})
. Since
\eta\equiv 1
on

B_{3/4}
,

\displaystyle\langle\partial_{i}\partial_{j}(\eta u_{i}u_{j}),\varphi\rangle

\displaystyle=\int_{B_{1}}\eta u_{i}u_{j}\,\partial_{i}\partial_{j}\varphi\,dx

\displaystyle=\int_{B_{1}}u_{i}u_{j}\,\partial_{i}\partial_{j}\varphi\,dx=\langle\partial_{i}\partial_{j}(u_{i}u_{j}),\varphi\rangle.

Thus
-\Delta p^{\mathrm{act}}=\partial_{i}\partial_{j}(u_{i}u_{j})
in

\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(B_{3/4})
. The pressure-admissibility assumption gives the
same equation for
p
on
B_{1}
, so

-\Delta(p-p^{\mathrm{act}})=0\quad\text{in }\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(B_{3/4})

for almost every time. This is precisely the asserted harmonicity of

p_{\mathrm{harm}}
. Time-dependent additive constants in
p
only add harmonic
constants to
p_{\mathrm{harm}}
.
∎

Remark B.5

(Status)
.

Proposition
B.2.1
records the pressure-splitting endpoint
needed by the package class. It is not a global pressure decomposition and
does not assert any regularity beyond the stated integrability and harmonicity
on the fixed interior ball.

B.3
Sharp Localized Package Class

Definition B.6

(Sharp package datum)
.

A sharp package datum is a tuple

\mathcal{D}_{\Lambda}(u,p)=\bigl(u,\,U,\,R,\,F^{\mathrm{act}},\,F^{\mathrm{mod}},\,E_{F},\,p^{\mathrm{act}},\,p_{\mathrm{harm}},\,\Pi,\,\Phi,\,T,\,s\bigr).

Here
u
is the physical localized velocity,
U
is a selected clean or
modeled velocity coordinate,
R
is a Reynolds/covariance coordinate,

F^{\mathrm{act}}_{ij}=\eta u_{i}u_{j}
,
F^{\mathrm{mod}}
is the model pressure source,

E_{F}
is an additional source residual coordinate,

p^{\mathrm{act}}=R_{i}R_{j}(F^{\mathrm{act}}_{ij})
,

p_{\mathrm{harm}}
is the harmonic pressure coordinate, and

\Pi,\Phi,T,s
are finite-window flux, energy, trace, and slack
coordinates.
Below we write

E^{\mathrm{src}}_{\mathcal{D}}:=E_{F,\mathcal{D}}

for this additional residual source coordinate.

Convention B.7

(Model-source convention A)
.

The main model-source convention is

F^{\mathrm{mod}}_{ij}:=\eta(U_{i}U_{j}+R_{ij}).

The exposed active covariance mismatch is

\mathcal{C}^{0}_{ij}:=F^{\mathrm{act}}_{ij}-F^{\mathrm{mod}}_{ij}=\eta\bigl(u_{i}u_{j}-U_{i}U_{j}-R_{ij}\bigr).

Remark B.8

(Residual-source variant)
.

One may instead use
F^{\mathrm{mod}}_{ij}=\eta u_{i}u_{j}+E^{\mathrm{cl}}_{F,ij}
, so that
the residual is carried by
E_{F}^{\mathrm{cl}}
. This paper uses
Convention
B.3
first because it exposes the covariance mismatch

u_{i}u_{j}-U_{i}U_{j}-R_{ij}
. The additional coordinate
E^{\mathrm{src}}=E_{F}
is kept
separate from this covariance mismatch; it records source error not modeled by
the chosen
U
and
R
coordinates.

Definition B.9

(Sharp package class)
.

The class
\mathcal{A}^{\sharp}_{\Lambda}
consists of finite-window packages of
the form
\mathcal{D}_{\Lambda}(u,p)
satisfying the pressure-admissible data
conditions, the pressure-natural harmonic admissibility

p_{\mathrm{harm}}\in Y_{\mathrm{harm}}^{(3/2)},

the chosen finite-window coordinate admissibility conditions, and the
admissible gauge constraints specified below.

Remark B.10

(NS-generation status)
.

The notation
\mathcal{D}_{\Lambda}(u,p)
records that the physical pressure and
velocity coordinates are generated from local Navier–Stokes pressure data.
The paper does not claim that every suitable weak solution automatically
generates all finite-window coordinates with the needed compactness,
visibility, or kernel-free properties.

B.4
Admissible Gauges and Same-Gauge Representatives

Convention B.11

(Conservative admissible gauge)
.

The admissible gauge class is denoted by
\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}
. A gauge element

\zeta\in\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}
has coordinates

\zeta=(\zeta_{u},\zeta_{U},\zeta_{R},\zeta_{E},\zeta_{h},\ldots),

and the conservative physical-gauge convention is

\zeta_{u}=0.

Thus

u_{\mathcal{D}-\zeta}:=u_{\mathcal{D}},

while

U_{\mathcal{D}-\zeta}:=U_{\mathcal{D}}-\zeta_{U},\qquad R_{\mathcal{D}-\zeta}:=R_{\mathcal{D}}-\zeta_{R},

E_{F,\mathcal{D}-\zeta}:=E_{F,\mathcal{D}}-\zeta_{E},\qquad p_{\mathrm{harm},\mathcal{D}-\zeta}:=p_{\mathrm{harm},\mathcal{D}}-\zeta_{h}.

Equivalently,

E^{\mathrm{src}}_{\mathcal{D}-\zeta}:=E_{F,\mathcal{D}-\zeta}.

The shifted package
\mathcal{D}-\zeta
is a quotient representative. It is not
claimed to be generated by a second Navier–Stokes solution.

Assumption B.12

(Same-gauge representative)
.

For each package
\mathcal{D}
under consideration, there is a selected
representative

\zeta_{*}(\mathcal{D})\in\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}

used simultaneously for the baseline distance, active source residual,
pressure tail, harmonic tail, flux coordinates, and slack residuals.
Separate minimizing representatives for separate errors are not used.

B.5
Sharp Baseline Norms and Amplitude Regimes

Definition B.13

(Core package norm)
.

The core finite-window package norm is denoted by

\|\mathcal{D}\|_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{pkg},0}.

It contains the baseline finite-window coordinates. Its precise coordinate
weights are part of the finite-window package datum.

Definition B.14

(Amplitude regimes)
.

The paper distinguishes three ways of handling the quadratic source term.

Regime I.

Bounded-amplitude packages.
One assumes

\|u_{\mathcal{D}-\zeta_{*}}\|_{L^{3}(Q_{1})}+\|U_{\mathcal{D}-\zeta_{*}}\|_{L^{3}(Q_{1})}\leq M_{U}.

This permits the linearization

\|u_{\mathcal{D}-\zeta_{*}}\|_{L^{3}}^{2}\leq M_{U}\|u_{\mathcal{D}-\zeta_{*}}\|_{L^{3}}.

Regime II.

Normalized quotient packages.
On the normalized quotient
sphere, visibility of the velocity coordinates supplies a finite constant

C_{\mathrm{amp}}
, which replaces
M_{U}
in the bounded-amplitude
proof.

Regime III.

Quadratic sharp baseline geometry.
The quadratic term

\|u\|_{L^{3}}^{2}
is added to the package geometry. This avoids an
external amplitude bound but changes the baseline geometry.

Remark B.15

(Finite-amplitude status)
.

This paper does not remove finite amplitude in the older linear baseline
geometry. It records three controlled finite-window regimes for treating the
quadratic source.

B.6
Component Estimates for the Pressure-Source Residual

B.6.1
Separated-support commutator

Definition B.16

(Cutoff–Riesz commutator)
.

For a tensor
f=(f_{ij})
, define

C_{\eta}(f):=R_{i}R_{j}(\eta f_{ij})-\eta R_{i}R_{j}(f_{ij}).

Proposition B.17

(Separated-support commutator estimate)
.

Let

A_{3/4,1}:=B_{1}\setminus B_{3/4}.

For sources supported in the annular region
A_{3/4,1}
, the fixed-window
estimate is

\|C_{\eta}(f)\|_{L^{3/2}((-1,0);L^{3/2}(B_{1/2}))}\leq C_{\eta}\|f\|_{L^{3/2}((-1,0);L^{3/2}(A_{3/4,1}))^{3\times 3}}.

Proof.

Extend
f
by zero outside
A_{3/4,1}
. For
x\in B_{1/2}
and

y\in A_{3/4,1}
, one has
|x-y|\geq 1/4
. Hence the singular kernel

K_{ij}
of
R_{i}R_{j}
is a bounded smooth kernel on this separated
configuration. Also
\eta(x)=1
for
x\in B_{1/2}
, so for such
x

C_{\eta}(f)(t,x)=\int_{A_{3/4,1}}K_{ij}(x-y)\bigl(\eta(y)-1\bigr)f_{ij}(t,y)\,dy.

The principal value is unnecessary because the supports are separated. Thus

|C_{\eta}(f)(t,x)|\leq C\|f(t,\cdot)\|_{L^{1}(A_{3/4,1})}\leq C\|f(t,\cdot)\|_{L^{3/2}(A_{3/4,1})},

where the last step uses the finite measure of the annulus. Taking the

L^{3/2}(B_{1/2})
norm in
x
and then the
L^{3/2}(-1,0)
norm in
time gives the estimate. All constants depend only on the fixed radii,
dimension, cutoff, and the finite number of tensor components.
∎

Corollary B.18

(Annular commutator control)
.

For
u\in L^{3}(Q_{1})^{3}
,

\|C_{\eta}(u\otimes u)\|_{Y_{\mathrm{prs}}}\leq C_{\eta}\|u\|_{L^{3}((-1,0);L^{3}(A_{3/4,1}))}^{2}.

If, in addition,

\|u\|_{L^{3}(Q_{1})}\leq M_{U},

then

\|C_{\eta}(u\otimes u)\|_{Y_{\mathrm{prs}}}\leq C_{\eta}M_{U}\|u\|_{L^{3}((-1,0);L^{3}(A_{3/4,1}))}.

Proof.

On
B_{1/2}
,
\eta(x)=1
, and the factor

\eta(y)-\eta(x)
vanishes whenever
y\in B_{3/4}
. Therefore

C_{\eta}(u\otimes u)=C_{\eta}(\mathbf{1}_{A_{3/4,1}}u\otimes u)\quad\text{on }B_{1/2}.

Apply Proposition
B.6.1
with

f=\mathbf{1}_{A_{3/4,1}}u\otimes u
, and use

\|u\otimes u\|_{L^{3/2}((-1,0);L^{3/2}(A_{3/4,1}))}\leq C\|u\|_{L^{3}((-1,0);L^{3}(A_{3/4,1}))}^{2}.

The finite-amplitude version follows from

\|u\|_{L^{3}((-1,0);L^{3}(A_{3/4,1}))}\leq\|u\|_{L^{3}(Q_{1})}\leq M_{U}.

∎

B.6.2
Active covariance mismatch

Definition B.19

(Active covariance mismatch)
.

For a shifted package
\mathcal{D}-\zeta
, define

\mathcal{C}_{ij}(\mathcal{D};\zeta):=\eta\bigl[u_{\mathcal{D}-\zeta,i}u_{\mathcal{D}-\zeta,j}-U_{\mathcal{D}-\zeta,i}U_{\mathcal{D}-\zeta,j}-R_{\mathcal{D}-\zeta,ij}\bigr].

Proposition B.20

(Active covariance mismatch estimate)
.

The finite-window estimate

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{D};\zeta)\|_{X_{\mathrm{src}}}

\displaystyle\leq C\|u_{\mathcal{D}-\zeta}-U_{\mathcal{D}-\zeta}\|_{L^{3}(Q_{1})}\bigl(\|u_{\mathcal{D}-\zeta}\|_{L^{3}(Q_{1})}

\displaystyle+\|U_{\mathcal{D}-\zeta}\|_{L^{3}(Q_{1})}\bigr)+C\|R_{\mathcal{D}-\zeta}\|_{L^{3/2}(Q_{1})}.

Under bounded amplitude, this yields

\|\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{D};\zeta)\|_{X_{\mathrm{src}}}\leq CM_{U}\|u_{\mathcal{D}-\zeta}-U_{\mathcal{D}-\zeta}\|_{L^{3}(Q_{1})}+C\|R_{\mathcal{D}-\zeta}\|_{L^{3/2}(Q_{1})}.

Proof.

Use the pointwise identity

u_{i}u_{j}-U_{i}U_{j}=(u_{i}-U_{i})u_{j}+U_{i}(u_{j}-U_{j}),

then apply Holder’s inequality in
L^{3}\cdot L^{3}\to L^{3/2}
. Since

0\leq\eta\leq 1
,

\displaystyle\|\eta(u_{i}u_{j}-U_{i}U_{j})\|_{L^{3/2}(Q_{1})}

\displaystyle\leq\|(u_{i}-U_{i})u_{j}\|_{L^{3/2}(Q_{1})}+\|U_{i}(u_{j}-U_{j})\|_{L^{3/2}(Q_{1})}

\displaystyle\leq\|u_{i}-U_{i}\|_{L^{3}(Q_{1})}\|u_{j}\|_{L^{3}(Q_{1})}+\|U_{i}\|_{L^{3}(Q_{1})}\|u_{j}-U_{j}\|_{L^{3}(Q_{1})}.

Summing over the finite number of tensor components yields the product term in
the displayed estimate. The Reynolds coordinate contributes linearly:

\|\eta R\|_{L^{3/2}(Q_{1})}\leq\|R\|_{L^{3/2}(Q_{1})}.

The bounded-amplitude form follows by replacing

\|u_{\mathcal{D}-\zeta}\|_{L^{3}}+\|U_{\mathcal{D}-\zeta}\|_{L^{3}}
by
M_{U}
, after
absorbing harmless finite-component constants.
∎

B.7
Pressure-Source Residual Functional

Definition B.21

(Annular leakage coordinate)
.

Define

\mathsf{Leak}_{\mathrm{ann}}^{\mathrm{lin}}(\mathcal{D};\zeta):=\|u_{\mathcal{D}-\zeta}\|_{L^{3}((-1,0);L^{3}(A_{3/4,1}))},

and

\mathsf{Leak}_{\mathrm{ann}}(\mathcal{D};\zeta):=\|u_{\mathcal{D}-\zeta}\|_{L^{3}((-1,0);L^{3}(A_{3/4,1}))}^{2}.

In a linearized bounded-amplitude regime this may be replaced by a linear
annular leakage coordinate multiplied by the finite amplitude constant.
When no representative is displayed, these coordinates are evaluated on the
package currently in the norm. Thus, for example,

\mathsf{Leak}_{\mathrm{ann}}^{\mathrm{lin}}(\mathcal{D}-\zeta)
means

\mathsf{Leak}_{\mathrm{ann}}^{\mathrm{lin}}(\mathcal{D};\zeta)
.

Definition B.22

(Pressure-source residual)
.

The pressure-source residual at the representative
\zeta
is

\displaystyle\mathsf{Err}^{\mathrm{prs}}_{\mathrm{src}}(\mathcal{D};\zeta)

\displaystyle:=\|R_{i}R_{j}(\mathcal{C}_{ij}(\mathcal{D};\zeta))\|_{Y_{\mathrm{prs}}}+\|C_{\eta}(u_{\mathcal{D}-\zeta}\otimes u_{\mathcal{D}-\zeta})\|_{Y_{\mathrm{prs}}}

\displaystyle+\|R_{i}R_{j}(E^{\mathrm{src}}_{\mathcal{D}-\zeta,ij})\|_{Y_{\mathrm{prs}}}.

The term
E^{\mathrm{src}}
collects residual source error not captured by the
selected velocity coordinate
U
and covariance coordinate
R
.

Remark B.23

(Component control)
.

Convention
B.2.1
controls the first and third terms by their

X_{\mathrm{src}}
source norms. Proposition
B.6.1
controls the
commutator by annular leakage. The sharp package norms below collect these
component quantities in the same gauge.

B.8
Sharp Package Norms and Quotient Distances

Definition B.24

(Sharp package norm)
.

In the bounded-amplitude regime, we use the following unit-weight sharp
package norm:

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{D}\|_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{pkg}}^{\sharp}

\displaystyle:=\|\mathcal{D}\|_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{pkg},0}+\|u_{\mathcal{D}}-U_{\mathcal{D}}\|_{L^{3}(Q_{1})}+\|R_{\mathcal{D}}\|_{L^{3/2}(Q_{1})}

\displaystyle+\|E^{\mathrm{src}}_{\mathcal{D}}\|_{X_{\mathrm{src}}}+\mathsf{Leak}_{\mathrm{ann}}^{\mathrm{lin}}(\mathcal{D})+\|p_{\mathrm{harm},\mathcal{D}}\|_{Y_{\mathrm{harm}}^{(3/2)}}.

Fixed positive coordinate weights may be inserted without changing any proof;
they only modify the finite-window constants. The corresponding quotient
distance is

\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{pkg}}^{\sharp}(\mathcal{D},\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}):=\inf_{\zeta\in\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}}\|\mathcal{D}-\zeta\|_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{pkg}}^{\sharp}.

Definition B.25

(Quadratic sharp package functional)
.

The quadratic sharp package functional is

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{D}\|_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{pkg}}^{\sharp,\mathrm{quad}}

\displaystyle:=\|\mathcal{D}\|_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{pkg},0}+\|u_{\mathcal{D}}-U_{\mathcal{D}}\|_{L^{3}(Q_{1})}\bigl(\|u_{\mathcal{D}}\|_{L^{3}(Q_{1})}

\displaystyle+\|U_{\mathcal{D}}\|_{L^{3}(Q_{1})}\bigr)+\|R_{\mathcal{D}}\|_{L^{3/2}(Q_{1})}

\displaystyle+\|E^{\mathrm{src}}_{\mathcal{D}}\|_{X_{\mathrm{src}}}+\mathsf{Leak}_{\mathrm{ann}}(\mathcal{D})+\|p_{\mathrm{harm},\mathcal{D}}\|_{Y_{\mathrm{harm}}^{(3/2)}}.

Because of the quadratic factors, this object is a nonnegative finite-window
package functional rather than a linear norm in the strict functional-analytic
sense. We keep the norm notation to emphasize that it is the geometry used to
measure packages. It defines the associated quotient functional

\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{pkg}}^{\sharp,\mathrm{quad}}
by quotienting over
\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}
.

Remark B.26

(Geometry cost)
.

The quadratic sharp geometry handles the
u_{i}u_{j}
nonlinearity by changing
the package geometry. It is not a theorem in the older purely linear baseline
geometry.

B.9
Main Pressure-Source Absorption Theorems

Assumption B.27

(Sharp near-minimizer)
.

The selected representative
\zeta_{*}(\mathcal{D})
satisfies

\|\mathcal{D}-\zeta_{*}\|_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{pkg}}^{\sharp}\leq\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{pkg}}^{\sharp}(\mathcal{D},\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}})+\delta_{\mathrm{pkg}}.

Theorem B.28

(Bounded-amplitude pressure-source absorption)
.

Let
\mathcal{D}\in\mathcal{A}^{\sharp}_{\Lambda}
. Assume the same-gauge representative

\zeta_{*}(\mathcal{D})
, the bounded-amplitude condition

\|u_{\mathcal{D}-\zeta_{*}}\|_{L^{3}(Q_{1})}+\|U_{\mathcal{D}-\zeta_{*}}\|_{L^{3}(Q_{1})}\leq M_{U},

and the sharp near-minimizer condition in
Assumption
B.9
. Then

\mathsf{Err}^{\mathrm{prs}}_{\mathrm{src}}(\mathcal{D};\zeta_{*})\leq C_{\mathrm{prs}}(M_{U})\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{pkg}}^{\sharp}(\mathcal{D},\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}})+C_{\mathrm{prs}}(M_{U})\delta_{\mathrm{pkg}},

where
C_{\mathrm{prs}}(M_{U})
depends only on the fixed geometry,
C_{CZ}
,

C_{\eta}
, and
M_{U}
.

Proof.

Apply Convention
B.2.1
and
Proposition
B.6.2
to the covariance mismatch pressure:

\displaystyle\|R_{i}R_{j}(\mathcal{C}_{ij}(\mathcal{D};\zeta_{*}))\|_{Y_{\mathrm{prs}}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{CZ}\|\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{D};\zeta_{*})\|_{X_{\mathrm{src}}}

\displaystyle\leq CM_{U}\|u_{\mathcal{D}-\zeta_{*}}-U_{\mathcal{D}-\zeta_{*}}\|_{L^{3}(Q_{1})}+C\|R_{\mathcal{D}-\zeta_{*}}\|_{L^{3/2}(Q_{1})}.

By Corollary
B.6.1
,

\|C_{\eta}(u_{\mathcal{D}-\zeta_{*}}\otimes u_{\mathcal{D}-\zeta_{*}})\|_{Y_{\mathrm{prs}}}\leq C_{\eta}M_{U}\mathsf{Leak}_{\mathrm{ann}}^{\mathrm{lin}}(\mathcal{D};\zeta_{*}).

Finally, the residual clean-source pressure is bounded by

\|R_{i}R_{j}(E^{\mathrm{src}}_{\mathcal{D}-\zeta_{*},ij})\|_{Y_{\mathrm{prs}}}\leq C_{CZ}\|E^{\mathrm{src}}_{\mathcal{D}-\zeta_{*}}\|_{X_{\mathrm{src}}}.

Each coordinate on the right-hand side is included in

\|\mathcal{D}-\zeta_{*}\|_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{pkg}}^{\sharp}
. Therefore, after enlarging the
finite-window constant,

\mathsf{Err}^{\mathrm{prs}}_{\mathrm{src}}(\mathcal{D};\zeta_{*})\leq C_{\mathrm{prs}}(M_{U})\|\mathcal{D}-\zeta_{*}\|_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{pkg}}^{\sharp}.

Using Assumption
B.9
gives the stated quotient estimate.
∎

Corollary B.29

(Weighted absorption form)
.

Assume the hypotheses of
Theorem
B.28
. For any prescribed
finite-window coefficient
\eta_{\mathrm{prs}}>0
, define a weighted sharp package
geometry so that

\|\mathcal{D}\|_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{pkg}}^{\sharp,\omega}\geq\frac{C_{\mathrm{prs}}(M_{U})}{\eta_{\mathrm{prs}}}\|\mathcal{D}\|_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{pkg}}^{\sharp}.

If the selected representative satisfies

\|\mathcal{D}-\zeta_{*}\|_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{pkg}}^{\sharp,\omega}\leq\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{pkg}}^{\sharp,\omega}(\mathcal{D},\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}})+\delta_{\mathrm{pkg}}^{\omega},

then the bounded-amplitude estimate takes the
form

\mathsf{Err}^{\mathrm{prs}}_{\mathrm{src}}(\mathcal{D};\zeta_{*})\leq\eta_{\mathrm{prs}}\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{pkg}}^{\sharp,\omega}(\mathcal{D},\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}})+\eta_{\mathrm{prs}}\delta_{\mathrm{pkg}}^{\omega}.

Proof.

By Theorem
B.28
,

\mathsf{Err}^{\mathrm{prs}}_{\mathrm{src}}(\mathcal{D};\zeta_{*})\leq C_{\mathrm{prs}}(M_{U})\|\mathcal{D}-\zeta_{*}\|_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{pkg}}^{\sharp}\leq\eta_{\mathrm{prs}}\|\mathcal{D}-\zeta_{*}\|_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{pkg}}^{\sharp,\omega}.

The weighted near-minimizer property gives the displayed estimate. This is an
accounting normalization, not a new PDE estimate.
∎

Corollary B.30

(Normalized quotient amplitude variant)
.

Assume the same-gauge and sharp near-minimizer hypotheses from
Theorem
B.28
. If the normalized
quotient condition

\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{pkg}}^{\sharp}(\mathcal{D},\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}})=1

holds and the selected representative satisfies

\|u_{\mathcal{D}-\zeta_{*}}\|_{L^{3}(Q_{1})}+\|U_{\mathcal{D}-\zeta_{*}}\|_{L^{3}(Q_{1})}\leq C_{\mathrm{amp}},

then the bounded-amplitude pressure-source absorption theorem applies on the
normalized quotient with
M_{U}=C_{\mathrm{amp}}
.

Remark B.31

(Status of normalized quotient amplitude)
.

This does not remove finite amplitude globally. It says that on the
normalized quotient sphere, amplitude may be treated as part of the package
geometry.

Theorem B.32

(Quadratic sharp baseline variant)
.

Let
\mathcal{D}\in\mathcal{A}^{\sharp}_{\Lambda}
, and let
\zeta_{*}(\mathcal{D})
be the
same-gauge representative from Assumption
B.4
. Assume
that this representative satisfies the quadratic near-minimizer condition

\|\mathcal{D}-\zeta_{*}\|_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{pkg}}^{\sharp,\mathrm{quad}}\leq\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{pkg}}^{\sharp,\mathrm{quad}}(\mathcal{D},\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}})+\delta_{\mathrm{pkg}}^{\mathrm{quad}}.

Then

\mathsf{Err}^{\mathrm{prs}}_{\mathrm{src}}(\mathcal{D};\zeta_{*})\leq C_{\mathrm{prs}}^{\mathrm{quad}}\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{pkg}}^{\sharp,\mathrm{quad}}(\mathcal{D},\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}})+C_{\mathrm{prs}}^{\mathrm{quad}}\delta_{\mathrm{pkg}}^{\mathrm{quad}}.

Proof.

The proof is the same component estimate as in
Theorem
B.28
, except that the
product term is not linearized. The full estimate in
Proposition
B.6.2
gives

\displaystyle\|R_{i}R_{j}(\mathcal{C}_{ij}(\mathcal{D};\zeta_{*}))\|_{Y_{\mathrm{prs}}}

\displaystyle\leq C\|u_{\mathcal{D}-\zeta_{*}}-U_{\mathcal{D}-\zeta_{*}}\|_{L^{3}}\bigl(\|u_{\mathcal{D}-\zeta_{*}}\|_{L^{3}}+\|U_{\mathcal{D}-\zeta_{*}}\|_{L^{3}}\bigr)

\displaystyle+C\|R_{\mathcal{D}-\zeta_{*}}\|_{L^{3/2}}.

The commutator term is bounded by

C_{\eta}\mathsf{Leak}_{\mathrm{ann}}(\mathcal{D};\zeta_{*}),

and the residual source term is bounded by

C_{CZ}\|E^{\mathrm{src}}_{\mathcal{D}-\zeta_{*}}\|_{X_{\mathrm{src}}}.

These are precisely coordinates of

\|\mathcal{D}-\zeta_{*}\|_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{pkg}}^{\sharp,\mathrm{quad}}
, up to fixed
finite-window constants. Therefore

\mathsf{Err}^{\mathrm{prs}}_{\mathrm{src}}(\mathcal{D};\zeta_{*})\leq C_{\mathrm{prs}}^{\mathrm{quad}}\|\mathcal{D}-\zeta_{*}\|_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{pkg}}^{\sharp,\mathrm{quad}}.

The quadratic near-minimizer condition completes the proof.
∎

Localization Leakage Absorption

B.10
Fixed Local Geometry

Throughout the paper,

Q_{1}=B_{1}\times(-1,0),\qquad B_{1/2}\subset B_{5/8}\subset B_{3/4}\subset B_{1}.

Let

\chi\in C_{c}^{\infty}(B_{3/4}),\qquad 0\leq\chi\leq 1,\qquad\chi\equiv 1\quad\text{on }B_{1/2}.

The fixed transition shell is

A_{\chi}:=\operatorname{supp}\nabla\chi\cup\operatorname{supp}\Delta\chi\subset B_{3/4}\setminus B_{1/2}.

All constants in this paper may depend on
\chi
, the fixed radii, and
the dimension. No scale-uniform estimate is claimed.

Convention B.33

(Pressure observation spaces)
.

The pressure-natural observation spaces used in the previous branch are

Y_{\mathrm{prs}}=L^{3/2}\bigl((-1,0);L^{3/2}(B_{1/2})\bigr),

and

Y_{\mathrm{harm}}^{(3/2)}=L^{3/2}\bigl((-1,0);L^{3/2}(B_{3/4})\bigr).

B.11
Imported Sharp Packages and Gauges

Definition B.34

(Sharp localized package datum)
.

A sharp localized package is a tuple

\mathcal{D}_{\Lambda}(u,p)=\bigl(u,\,U,\,R,\,F^{\mathrm{act}},\,F^{\mathrm{mod}},\,E_{F},\,p^{\mathrm{act}},\,p_{\mathrm{harm}},\,\Pi,\,\Phi,\,T,\,s\bigr).

The physical coordinates
u,p
are the localized Navier–Stokes velocity and
pressure data. The coordinates
U,R,E_{F},p^{\mathrm{act}},p_{\mathrm{harm}}
are the
model velocity, covariance, additional source residual, active pressure, and
harmonic pressure coordinates inherited from the pressure-source branch.

Convention B.35

(Conservative admissible gauge)
.

The admissible gauge class is denoted by
\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}
. A gauge element

\zeta=(\zeta_{u},\zeta_{U},\zeta_{R},\zeta_{E},\zeta_{h},\ldots)\in\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}

satisfies the conservative physical-gauge convention

\zeta_{u}=0.

Thus

u_{\mathcal{D}-\zeta}:=u_{\mathcal{D}},

while the model and pressure-tail coordinates may shift:

U_{\mathcal{D}-\zeta}:=U_{\mathcal{D}}-\zeta_{U},\qquad R_{\mathcal{D}-\zeta}:=R_{\mathcal{D}}-\zeta_{R},

E_{F,\mathcal{D}-\zeta}:=E_{F,\mathcal{D}}-\zeta_{E},\qquad p_{\mathrm{harm},\mathcal{D}-\zeta}:=p_{\mathrm{harm},\mathcal{D}}-\zeta_{h}.

The shifted package
\mathcal{D}-\zeta
is a quotient representative; it is not
claimed to be generated by a second Navier–Stokes solution.

Assumption B.36

(Same-gauge localization representative)
.

For each package
\mathcal{D}
under consideration, a representative

\zeta_{*}(\mathcal{D})\in\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}

is selected and used simultaneously in the localization residual, annular
leakage coordinates, pressure leakage coordinates, and sharp localization
quotient distance.

B.12
Localized Momentum Equation and Leakage Terms

Assumption B.37

(Distributional local momentum equation)
.

The physical local data satisfy the finite-window integrability conditions

u\in L^{3}(Q_{1})^{3},\qquad\nabla u\in L^{2}((-1,0);L^{2}(B_{3/4}))^{3\times 3},\qquad p\in L^{3/2}(Q_{1}),

and

\partial_{t}u-\Delta u+\nabla p+\nabla\cdot(u\otimes u)=0

in distributions on
Q_{1}
.

Definition B.38

(Localized velocity)
.

Set

v:=\chi u.

Proposition B.39

(Localized momentum leakage identity)
.

For data satisfying
Section

B.12
, the localized velocity

v=\chi u
satisfies the distributional identity

\partial_{t}(\chi u)-\Delta(\chi u)+\nabla(\chi p)+\nabla\cdot(\chi u\otimes u)=\mathcal{L}_{\chi}^{\mathrm{mom}}(u,p),

where

\mathcal{L}_{\chi}^{\mathrm{mom}}(u,p)=-2\nabla\chi\cdot\nabla u-u\Delta\chi+p\nabla\chi+(u\otimes u)\nabla\chi,

where
((u\otimes u)\nabla\chi)_{i}=u_{i}u_{j}\partial_{j}\chi
.

Proof.

We compute componentwise in distributions. Since
\chi
is independent of
time,

\partial_{t}(\chi u_{i})=\chi\,\partial_{t}u_{i}.

The product rules give

\Delta(\chi u_{i})=\chi\Delta u_{i}+2\partial_{a}\chi\,\partial_{a}u_{i}+u_{i}\Delta\chi,

\partial_{i}(\chi p)=\chi\partial_{i}p+p\,\partial_{i}\chi,

and

\partial_{j}(\chi u_{i}u_{j})=\chi\partial_{j}(u_{i}u_{j})+u_{i}u_{j}\partial_{j}\chi.

Therefore

\displaystyle\partial_{t}(\chi u_{i})-\Delta(\chi u_{i})+\partial_{i}(\chi p)+\partial_{j}(\chi u_{i}u_{j})

\displaystyle=\chi\bigl(\partial_{t}u_{i}-\Delta u_{i}+\partial_{i}p+\partial_{j}(u_{i}u_{j})\bigr)-2\partial_{a}\chi\,\partial_{a}u_{i}-u_{i}\Delta\chi+p\,\partial_{i}\chi+u_{i}u_{j}\partial_{j}\chi.

The term in parentheses vanishes by
Section

B.12
. This
is the stated identity. Each leakage term contains either
\nabla\chi
or

\Delta\chi
, and hence is supported in
A_{\chi}
.
∎

Remark B.40

(Physical identity versus quotient representative)
.

Section

B.12
is an identity for the physical
coordinates
(u,p)
. A shifted package
\mathcal{D}-\zeta
is only a quotient
representative; since the conservative gauge fixes
u
but may change
pressure-tail or model coordinates, the shifted pressure coordinate need not
generate a second Navier–Stokes solution. The residuals below therefore use
the same cutoff expression as an accounting functional on the chosen
representative. No additional distributional equation is asserted for

\mathcal{D}-\zeta
unless it is separately assumed.

B.13
Momentum Leakage Estimate

Definition B.41

(Momentum leakage norm)
.

Set

\mathcal{Y}_{\mathrm{mom}}:=L^{2}\bigl((-1,0);H^{-1}(B_{3/4})\bigr)+L^{3/2}\bigl((-1,0);W^{-1,3/2}(B_{3/4})\bigr).

The first summand is used for diffusion cutoff leakage, and the second for
pressure and nonlinear transport leakage.
The norm is the usual sum-space norm:

\|F\|_{\mathcal{Y}_{\mathrm{mom}}}:=\inf_{F=F_{1}+F_{2}}\left(\|F_{1}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{-1}_{x}}+\|F_{2}\|_{L^{3/2}_{t}W^{-1,3/2}_{x}}\right),

with spatial domains
B_{3/4}
and time interval
(-1,0)
.

Definition B.42

(Annular momentum leakage coordinates)
.

For a shifted package
\mathcal{D}-\zeta
, define

\mathsf{Leak}_{\nabla u}(\mathcal{D};\zeta):=\|\nabla u_{\mathcal{D}-\zeta}\|_{L^{2}((-1,0);L^{2}(A_{\chi}))},

\mathsf{Leak}_{u}(\mathcal{D};\zeta):=\|u_{\mathcal{D}-\zeta}\|_{L^{3}((-1,0);L^{3}(A_{\chi}))},

and

\mathsf{Leak}_{p}(\mathcal{D};\zeta):=\|p_{\mathcal{D}-\zeta}\|_{L^{3/2}((-1,0);L^{3/2}(A_{\chi}))}.

Here
p_{\mathcal{D}-\zeta}
denotes the pressure coordinate used by the package,
for instance

p_{\mathcal{D}-\zeta}=p^{\mathrm{act}}_{\mathcal{D}-\zeta}+p_{\mathrm{harm},\mathcal{D}-\zeta}+p^{\mathrm{rem}}_{\mathcal{D}-\zeta},

when a pressure remainder coordinate is present.
When no representative is displayed, the leakage coordinates are evaluated on
the package currently inside the norm. Thus
\mathsf{Leak}_{u}(\mathcal{D}-\zeta)
means

\mathsf{Leak}_{u}(\mathcal{D};\zeta)
, and
\mathsf{Leak}_{u}(\mathcal{D})
means

\mathsf{Leak}_{u}(\mathcal{D};0)
; the same convention applies to
\mathsf{Leak}_{\nabla u}
and

\mathsf{Leak}_{p}
.

Definition B.43

(Momentum localization residual)
.

Define

\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{loc}}^{\mathrm{mom}}(\mathcal{D};\zeta):=\|\mathcal{L}_{\chi}^{\mathrm{mom}}(u_{\mathcal{D}-\zeta},p_{\mathcal{D}-\zeta})\|_{\mathcal{Y}_{\mathrm{mom}}}.

For a nonphysical shifted pressure coordinate, this is the norm of the
cutoff-leakage accounting expression, not a new localized Navier–Stokes
identity.

Theorem B.44

(Momentum localization leakage estimate)
.

For fixed
\chi
, and for every package representative for which the
displayed leakage coordinates are finite,

\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{loc}}^{\mathrm{mom}}(\mathcal{D};\zeta)\leq C_{\chi}\left[\mathsf{Leak}_{\nabla u}(\mathcal{D};\zeta)+\mathsf{Leak}_{u}(\mathcal{D};\zeta)+\mathsf{Leak}_{p}(\mathcal{D};\zeta)+\mathsf{Leak}_{u}(\mathcal{D};\zeta)^{2}\right].

If

\|u_{\mathcal{D}-\zeta}\|_{L^{3}(Q_{1})}\leq M_{U},

then

\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{loc}}^{\mathrm{mom}}(\mathcal{D};\zeta)\leq C_{\chi}(M_{U})\left[\mathsf{Leak}_{\nabla u}(\mathcal{D};\zeta)+\mathsf{Leak}_{u}(\mathcal{D};\zeta)+\mathsf{Leak}_{p}(\mathcal{D};\zeta)\right].

Proof.

By definition,
\mathcal{L}_{\chi}^{\mathrm{mom}}
is the sum of the four cutoff terms
displayed in
Section

B.12
. The physical identity
justifies this expression for the unshifted Navier–Stokes data; for a shifted
representative it is used as the accounting residual described in

Section

B.12
. The diffusion leakage satisfies

\|\nabla\chi\cdot\nabla u_{\mathcal{D}-\zeta}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{-1}_{x}}\leq C_{\chi}\|\nabla u_{\mathcal{D}-\zeta}\|_{L^{2}((-1,0);L^{2}(A_{\chi}))}=C_{\chi}\mathsf{Leak}_{\nabla u}(\mathcal{D};\zeta),

because
L^{2}(B_{3/4})\hookrightarrow H^{-1}(B_{3/4})
.

The remaining terms are estimated in

L^{3/2}_{t}W^{-1,3/2}_{x}
. Since

L^{3/2}(B_{3/4})\hookrightarrow W^{-1,3/2}(B_{3/4})

on the bounded fixed ball,

\|u_{\mathcal{D}-\zeta}\Delta\chi\|_{L^{3/2}_{t}W^{-1,3/2}_{x}}\leq C_{\chi}\|u_{\mathcal{D}-\zeta}\|_{L^{3/2}((-1,0);L^{3/2}(A_{\chi}))}.

The finite measure of
A_{\chi}\times(-1,0)
gives

\|u_{\mathcal{D}-\zeta}\|_{L^{3/2}_{t}L^{3/2}_{x}(A_{\chi})}\leq C\mathsf{Leak}_{u}(\mathcal{D};\zeta).

Similarly,

\|p_{\mathcal{D}-\zeta}\nabla\chi\|_{L^{3/2}_{t}W^{-1,3/2}_{x}}\leq C_{\chi}\mathsf{Leak}_{p}(\mathcal{D};\zeta).

Finally,

\|(u_{\mathcal{D}-\zeta}\otimes u_{\mathcal{D}-\zeta})\nabla\chi\|_{L^{3/2}_{t}W^{-1,3/2}_{x}}\leq C_{\chi}\|u_{\mathcal{D}-\zeta}\otimes u_{\mathcal{D}-\zeta}\|_{L^{3/2}_{t}L^{3/2}_{x}(A_{\chi})}\leq C_{\chi}\mathsf{Leak}_{u}(\mathcal{D};\zeta)^{2}.

Using the sum-space norm in
\mathcal{Y}_{\mathrm{mom}}
gives the first estimate.

If
\|u_{\mathcal{D}-\zeta}\|_{L^{3}(Q_{1})}\leq M_{U}
, then

\mathsf{Leak}_{u}(\mathcal{D};\zeta)^{2}\leq\|u_{\mathcal{D}-\zeta}\|_{L^{3}(Q_{1})}\mathsf{Leak}_{u}(\mathcal{D};\zeta)\leq M_{U}\mathsf{Leak}_{u}(\mathcal{D};\zeta).

After enlarging the finite-window constant to depend on
M_{U}
, we obtain the
bounded-amplitude estimate.
∎

B.14
Localized Energy and Flux Leakage

Definition B.45

(Flux residual)
.

For the spatial cutoff
\phi=\chi^{2}
, define

\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{loc}}^{\mathrm{flux}}(\mathcal{D};\zeta)
, whenever the displayed integrals are
finite, to be the sum of the absolute values of the localized energy/flux
leakage terms supported in
A_{\chi}\times(-1,0)
:

\displaystyle\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{loc}}^{\mathrm{flux}}(\mathcal{D};\zeta)

\displaystyle:=\left|\int_{-1}^{0}\int_{B_{3/4}}|u_{\mathcal{D}-\zeta}|^{2}\Delta(\chi^{2})\,dx\,dt\right|

\displaystyle+\left|\int_{-1}^{0}\int_{B_{3/4}}|u_{\mathcal{D}-\zeta}|^{2}u_{\mathcal{D}-\zeta}\cdot\nabla(\chi^{2})\,dx\,dt\right|

\displaystyle+2\left|\int_{-1}^{0}\int_{B_{3/4}}p_{\mathcal{D}-\zeta}u_{\mathcal{D}-\zeta}\cdot\nabla(\chi^{2})\,dx\,dt\right|.

If the chosen finite-window geometry includes a time cutoff, the corresponding

\int|u|^{2}\partial_{t}\phi
term is added as an additional leakage
coordinate. This definition is an accounting functional; the local energy
inequality itself is not used in this estimate.

Theorem B.46

(Localized energy/flux leakage estimate)
.

For a fixed spatial cutoff, and for every package representative for which the
displayed leakage coordinates are finite,

\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{loc}}^{\mathrm{flux}}(\mathcal{D};\zeta)\leq C_{\chi}\left[\mathsf{Leak}_{u}(\mathcal{D};\zeta)^{3}+\mathsf{Leak}_{p}(\mathcal{D};\zeta)\mathsf{Leak}_{u}(\mathcal{D};\zeta)+\mathsf{Leak}_{u}(\mathcal{D};\zeta)^{2}\right].

If

\|u_{\mathcal{D}-\zeta}\|_{L^{3}(Q_{1})}\leq M_{U},

then

\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{loc}}^{\mathrm{flux}}(\mathcal{D};\zeta)\leq C_{\chi}(M_{U})\left[\mathsf{Leak}_{u}(\mathcal{D};\zeta)+\mathsf{Leak}_{p}(\mathcal{D};\zeta)\right].

Proof.

Both
\nabla(\chi^{2})
and
\Delta(\chi^{2})
are supported in
A_{\chi}
and
bounded by constants depending only on the fixed cutoff. Hence

\left|\int|u_{\mathcal{D}-\zeta}|^{2}\Delta(\chi^{2})\right|\leq C_{\chi}\|u_{\mathcal{D}-\zeta}\|_{L^{2}((-1,0);L^{2}(A_{\chi}))}^{2}.

Since
A_{\chi}\times(-1,0)
has finite measure,

\|u_{\mathcal{D}-\zeta}\|_{L^{2}(A_{\chi}\times(-1,0))}\leq C\mathsf{Leak}_{u}(\mathcal{D};\zeta),

so the diffusion-flux leakage is bounded by
C_{\chi}\mathsf{Leak}_{u}(\mathcal{D};\zeta)^{2}
.
The cubic flux term satisfies

\left|\int|u_{\mathcal{D}-\zeta}|^{2}u_{\mathcal{D}-\zeta}\cdot\nabla(\chi^{2})\right|\leq C_{\chi}\mathsf{Leak}_{u}(\mathcal{D};\zeta)^{3}.

For the pressure flux term, Holder’s inequality gives

\left|\int p_{\mathcal{D}-\zeta}u_{\mathcal{D}-\zeta}\cdot\nabla(\chi^{2})\right|\leq C_{\chi}\mathsf{Leak}_{p}(\mathcal{D};\zeta)\mathsf{Leak}_{u}(\mathcal{D};\zeta).

Combining the three bounds proves the first estimate.

If
\|u_{\mathcal{D}-\zeta}\|_{L^{3}(Q_{1})}\leq M_{U}
, then

\mathsf{Leak}_{u}(\mathcal{D};\zeta)^{2}\leq M_{U}\mathsf{Leak}_{u}(\mathcal{D};\zeta),\qquad\mathsf{Leak}_{u}(\mathcal{D};\zeta)^{3}\leq M_{U}^{2}\mathsf{Leak}_{u}(\mathcal{D};\zeta),

and

\mathsf{Leak}_{p}(\mathcal{D};\zeta)\mathsf{Leak}_{u}(\mathcal{D};\zeta)\leq M_{U}\mathsf{Leak}_{p}(\mathcal{D};\zeta).

Absorbing the powers of
M_{U}
into
C_{\chi}(M_{U})
gives the
bounded-amplitude estimate.
∎

Remark B.47

(Status of the flux estimate)
.

This is a fixed-window leakage estimate. It is not a global energy inequality
theorem and does not imply regularity.

B.15
Pressure Decomposition for Annular Leakage

Definition B.48

(Annular pressure leakage coordinate)
.

The pressure leakage coordinate is

\mathsf{Leak}_{p}^{\mathrm{ann}}(\mathcal{D};\zeta):=\|p_{\mathcal{D}-\zeta}\|_{L^{3/2}((-1,0);L^{3/2}(A_{\chi}))}.

In the default notation below,
\mathsf{Leak}_{p}=\mathsf{Leak}_{p}^{\mathrm{ann}}
.

Proposition B.49

(Pressure leakage bookkeeping)
.

If

p_{\mathcal{D}-\zeta}=p^{\mathrm{act}}_{\mathcal{D}-\zeta}+p_{\mathrm{harm},\mathcal{D}-\zeta}+p^{\mathrm{rem}}_{\mathcal{D}-\zeta},

then the annular pressure leakage is controlled by the sum of the corresponding
annular active, harmonic, and remainder pressure coordinates.

Proof.

By the triangle inequality in

L^{3/2}((-1,0);L^{3/2}(A_{\chi}))
,

\displaystyle\mathsf{Leak}_{p}(\mathcal{D};\zeta)

\displaystyle\leq\|p^{\mathrm{act}}_{\mathcal{D}-\zeta}\|_{L^{3/2}_{t}L^{3/2}_{x}(A_{\chi})}+\|p_{\mathrm{harm},\mathcal{D}-\zeta}\|_{L^{3/2}_{t}L^{3/2}_{x}(A_{\chi})}

\displaystyle+\|p^{\mathrm{rem}}_{\mathcal{D}-\zeta}\|_{L^{3/2}_{t}L^{3/2}_{x}(A_{\chi})}.

If the package supplies a Calderon–Zygmund source coordinate for

p^{\mathrm{act}}
, then the first term may be estimated by that source
coordinate. No smallness of annular pressure leakage is claimed here; this is
only the bookkeeping decomposition used by the localization norm.
∎

B.16
Localization Leakage Residual Functional

Definition B.50

(Localization leakage residual)
.

The two-component localization residual is

\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathcal{D};\zeta):=\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{loc}}^{\mathrm{mom}}(\mathcal{D};\zeta)+\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{loc}}^{\mathrm{flux}}(\mathcal{D};\zeta).

The localization residual contains only the momentum and flux leakage terms;
trace, slack, and gate coordinates are accounted for separately in the
gate/slack component norm.

Remark B.51

(Trace and slack leakage)
.

Trace and slack leakage may be incorporated through a separate residual
coordinate satisfying an estimate of the form

\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{loc}}^{\mathrm{trace}}(\mathcal{D};\zeta)\leq C_{\mathrm{tr}}\left[\mathsf{Leak}_{u}(\mathcal{D};\zeta)+\mathsf{Leak}_{p}(\mathcal{D};\zeta)+\mathsf{Leak}_{\nabla u}(\mathcal{D};\zeta)\right].

Such terms are not part of the two-component localization residual; they are
accounted for in the gate/slack component geometry used below.

B.17
Sharp Localization Norm and Quotient Distance

Definition B.52

(Sharp localization package norm)
.

Let
\|\mathcal{D}\|_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{pkg}}^{\sharp}
denote the sharp package norm from the
pressure-source branch. Define

\|\mathcal{D}\|_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{pkg}}^{\sharp,\mathrm{loc}}:=\|\mathcal{D}\|_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{pkg}}^{\sharp}+\mathsf{Leak}_{\nabla u}(\mathcal{D})+\mathsf{Leak}_{u}(\mathcal{D})+\mathsf{Leak}_{p}(\mathcal{D}).

The corresponding quotient distance is

\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{pkg}}^{\sharp,\mathrm{loc}}(\mathcal{D},\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}):=\inf_{\zeta\in\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}}\|\mathcal{D}-\zeta\|_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{pkg}}^{\sharp,\mathrm{loc}}.

Definition B.53

(Quadratic localization package norm)
.

The quadratic localization norm is

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{D}\|_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{pkg}}^{\sharp,\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{quad}}

\displaystyle:=\|\mathcal{D}\|_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{pkg}}^{\sharp}+\mathsf{Leak}_{\nabla u}(\mathcal{D})+\mathsf{Leak}_{u}(\mathcal{D})+\mathsf{Leak}_{u}(\mathcal{D})^{2}+\mathsf{Leak}_{u}(\mathcal{D})^{3}

\displaystyle+\mathsf{Leak}_{p}(\mathcal{D})\mathsf{Leak}_{u}(\mathcal{D})+\mathsf{Leak}_{p}(\mathcal{D}).

It defines

\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{pkg}}^{\sharp,\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{quad}}(\mathcal{D},\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}})
by quotienting over

\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}
.

Assumption B.54

(Sharp localization near-minimizer)
.

The selected same-gauge representative satisfies

\|\mathcal{D}-\zeta_{*}\|_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{pkg}}^{\sharp,\mathrm{loc}}\leq\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{pkg}}^{\sharp,\mathrm{loc}}(\mathcal{D},\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}})+\delta_{\mathrm{loc}}.

B.18
Main Localization Absorption Theorems

Theorem B.55

(Bounded-amplitude localization leakage absorption)
.

Let
\mathcal{D}
be a sharp localized package. Assume the same-gauge
representative
\zeta_{*}(\mathcal{D})
, the sharp localization near-minimizer
condition in
Section

B.17
, and the finite-amplitude bound

\|u_{\mathcal{D}-\zeta_{*}}\|_{L^{3}(Q_{1})}\leq M_{U}.

Then

\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathcal{D};\zeta_{*})\leq C_{\mathrm{loc}}(M_{U})\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{pkg}}^{\sharp,\mathrm{loc}}(\mathcal{D},\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}})+C_{\mathrm{loc}}(M_{U})\delta_{\mathrm{loc}}.

Proof.

By
Theorems

B.44
and
B.46
, applied
at
\zeta_{*}
, the momentum and flux components satisfy

\displaystyle\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{loc}}^{\mathrm{mom}}(\mathcal{D};\zeta_{*})+\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{loc}}^{\mathrm{flux}}(\mathcal{D};\zeta_{*})

\displaystyle\leq C_{\mathrm{loc}}(M_{U})\bigl[\mathsf{Leak}_{\nabla u}(\mathcal{D};\zeta_{*})+\mathsf{Leak}_{u}(\mathcal{D};\zeta_{*})

\displaystyle+\mathsf{Leak}_{p}(\mathcal{D};\zeta_{*})\bigr].

By definition,

\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathcal{D};\zeta_{*})=\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{loc}}^{\mathrm{mom}}(\mathcal{D};\zeta_{*})+\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{loc}}^{\mathrm{flux}}(\mathcal{D};\zeta_{*}).

The leakage coordinates on the right-hand side are included in

\|\mathcal{D}-\zeta_{*}\|_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{pkg}}^{\sharp,\mathrm{loc}}
. Thus

\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathcal{D};\zeta_{*})\leq C_{\mathrm{loc}}(M_{U})\|\mathcal{D}-\zeta_{*}\|_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{pkg}}^{\sharp,\mathrm{loc}}.

The sharp localization near-minimizer property in

Section

B.17
completes the proof.
∎

Corollary B.56

(Weighted absorption)
.

Assume the hypotheses of

Theorem

B.55
.
For a prescribed
\eta_{\mathrm{loc}}>0
, define a weighted localization norm

\|\cdot\|_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{pkg}}^{\sharp,\mathrm{loc},\omega}
satisfying

\|\mathcal{D}\|_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{pkg}}^{\sharp,\mathrm{loc},\omega}\geq\frac{C_{\mathrm{loc}}(M_{U})}{\eta_{\mathrm{loc}}}\|\mathcal{D}\|_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{pkg}}^{\sharp,\mathrm{loc}}.

If the selected representative is a near-minimizer in the weighted norm with
error
\delta_{\mathrm{loc}}^{\omega}
, then

\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathcal{D};\zeta_{*})\leq\eta_{\mathrm{loc}}\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{pkg}}^{\sharp,\mathrm{loc},\omega}(\mathcal{D},\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}})+\eta_{\mathrm{loc}}\delta_{\mathrm{loc}}^{\omega}.

Proof.

Repeating the first estimate in the proof of

Theorem

B.55
gives

\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathcal{D};\zeta_{*})\leq C_{\mathrm{loc}}(M_{U})\|\mathcal{D}-\zeta_{*}\|_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{pkg}}^{\sharp,\mathrm{loc}}\leq\eta_{\mathrm{loc}}\|\mathcal{D}-\zeta_{*}\|_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{pkg}}^{\sharp,\mathrm{loc},\omega}.

The weighted near-minimizer property gives the displayed estimate. This is an
accounting normalization, not a new PDE estimate.
∎

Theorem B.57

(Quadratic localization geometry)
.

Let
\mathcal{D}
be a sharp localized package, and let
\zeta_{*}(\mathcal{D})
be the
same-gauge representative from
Section

B.11
. Assume that the
selected representative satisfies a quadratic localization near-minimizer
condition

\|\mathcal{D}-\zeta_{*}\|_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{pkg}}^{\sharp,\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{quad}}\leq\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{pkg}}^{\sharp,\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{quad}}(\mathcal{D},\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}})+\delta_{\mathrm{loc}}^{\mathrm{quad}}.

Then

\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathcal{D};\zeta_{*})\leq C_{\mathrm{loc}}^{\mathrm{quad}}\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{pkg}}^{\sharp,\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{quad}}(\mathcal{D},\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}})+C_{\mathrm{loc}}^{\mathrm{quad}}\delta_{\mathrm{loc}}^{\mathrm{quad}}.

Proof.

Use the unlinearized estimates in

Theorems

B.44
and
B.46
. Their
right-hand sides are bounded by a fixed finite-window constant times

\displaystyle\mathsf{Leak}_{\nabla u}(\mathcal{D};\zeta_{*})+\mathsf{Leak}_{u}(\mathcal{D};\zeta_{*})+\mathsf{Leak}_{u}(\mathcal{D};\zeta_{*})^{2}+\mathsf{Leak}_{u}(\mathcal{D};\zeta_{*})^{3}

\displaystyle+\mathsf{Leak}_{p}(\mathcal{D};\zeta_{*})\mathsf{Leak}_{u}(\mathcal{D};\zeta_{*})+\mathsf{Leak}_{p}(\mathcal{D};\zeta_{*}).

These are precisely the localization leakage coordinates included in

\|\mathcal{D}-\zeta_{*}\|_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{pkg}}^{\sharp,\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{quad}}
, up to fixed
finite-window constants. Therefore

\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathcal{D};\zeta_{*})\leq C_{\mathrm{loc}}^{\mathrm{quad}}\|\mathcal{D}-\zeta_{*}\|_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{pkg}}^{\sharp,\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{quad}}.

The quadratic near-minimizer property gives the result. The cost is that the
geometry has changed.
∎

Corollary B.58

(Normalized quotient amplitude variant)
.

Assume the same-gauge representative and sharp localization near-minimizer
hypotheses from
Theorem

B.55
. If on
the normalized quotient sphere

\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{pkg}}^{\sharp,\mathrm{loc}}(\mathcal{D},\Gamma^{\mathrm{int}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}})=1,

the selected representative satisfies

\|u_{\mathcal{D}-\zeta_{*}}\|_{L^{3}(Q_{1})}\leq C_{\mathrm{amp}}.

Then the bounded-amplitude localization absorption theorem applies on that
normalized quotient with
M_{U}=C_{\mathrm{amp}}
.

Remark B.59

(Status of normalized quotient amplitude)
.

This does not remove finite amplitude globally. It only says that, on the
normalized quotient sphere, amplitude may be treated as part of the package
geometry.

Reproduction Drift Absorption

B.19
Two-Window Geometry and Reproduction Maps

B.19.1
Fixed windows

Let

Q^{(0)},\qquad Q^{(1)}

be two normalized finite windows, each identified with a copy of

Q_{1}=B_{1}\times(-1,0).

All constants in this paper are finite-window constants. They may depend
on the chosen windows, cutoffs, and reproduction maps. No scale-uniform
boundedness is claimed.

Definition B.60

(Reproduction map)
.

A fixed reproduction map

\mathcal{R}_{0\to 1}

consists of coordinate maps

\mathcal{R}^{u}:L^{3}(Q^{(0)})^{3}\to L^{3}(Q^{(1)})^{3},

\mathcal{R}^{\nabla u}:L^{2}_{t}L^{2}_{x}(Q^{(0)})^{3\times 3}\to L^{2}_{t}L^{2}_{x}(Q^{(1)})^{3\times 3},

\mathcal{R}^{\mathrm{src}}:X_{\mathrm{src}}^{(0)}\to X_{\mathrm{src}}^{(1)},\qquad\mathcal{R}^{\mathrm{prs}}:Y_{\mathrm{prs}}^{(0)}\to Y_{\mathrm{prs}}^{(1)},\qquad\mathcal{R}^{\mathrm{harm}}:Y_{\mathrm{harm}}^{(3/2),(0)}\to Y_{\mathrm{harm}}^{(3/2),(1)},

and finite-window coordinate maps

\mathcal{R}^{U},\quad\mathcal{R}^{R},\quad\mathcal{R}^{E},\quad\mathcal{R}^{\Pi},\quad\mathcal{R}^{\Phi},\quad\mathcal{R}^{T},\quad\mathcal{R}^{s}.

All these maps are fixed throughout the two-window problem, and their operator
norms may enter the constants.

Remark B.61

(Finite-window status)
.

The maps in
Section

B.19.1
are structural inputs. The paper
does not prove that they form a scale-uniform semigroup or an exact
Navier–Stokes evolution operator.

B.20
Two-Window Sharp Packages

Definition B.62

(Two-window package pair)
.

For
j=0,1
, let

D_{j}=\bigl(u_{j},\,U_{j},\,R_{j},\,F^{\mathrm{act}}_{j},\,F^{\mathrm{mod}}_{j},\,E_{F,j},\,p^{\mathrm{act}}_{j},\,p_{\mathrm{harm},j},\,\Pi_{j},\,\Phi_{j},\,T_{j},\,s_{j}\bigr)

be sharp localized packages on
Q^{(j)}
. The two-window package pair is

\mathbf{D}=(D_{0},D_{1}).

The reproduction problem compares
D_{1}
with
\mathcal{R}_{0\to 1}D_{0}
.

B.21
Chain Gauge and Same-Chain Representative

Definition B.63

(Chain gauge class)
.

The admissible chain gauge class is

\Gamma_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}^{\mathrm{chain}}\subset\Gamma_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}^{(0)}\times\Gamma_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}^{(1)}.

A chain gauge element is

\boldsymbol{\zeta}=(\zeta_{0},\zeta_{1}).

The conservative physical gauge convention is imposed on both windows:

(\zeta_{0})_{u}=0,\qquad(\zeta_{1})_{u}=0.

Thus

u_{D_{j}-\zeta_{j}}=u_{j},\qquad j=0,1.

Model, source, and pressure-tail coordinates may shift.

Assumption B.64

(Same-chain representative)
.

For every two-window package pair
\mathbf{D}
, a representative

\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{*}(\mathbf{D})=(\zeta_{0,*},\zeta_{1,*})\in\Gamma_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}^{\mathrm{chain}}

is selected and used simultaneously in every reproduction drift channel and in
the sharp chain quotient distance. Different drift channels are not allowed
to optimize over different gauges.

Remark B.65

(Gauge status)
.

The shifted packages
D_{j}-\zeta_{j}
are quotient representatives. They are
not claimed to be generated by different Navier–Stokes solutions.

B.22
Basic Reproduction Drift Coordinates

Definition B.66

(Primitive drift coordinates)
.

For a pair
\mathbf{D}=(D_{0},D_{1})
and a chain representative

\boldsymbol{\zeta}=(\zeta_{0},\zeta_{1})
, define

\mathsf{Rep}_{u}(\mathbf{D};\boldsymbol{\zeta}):=\|u_{1}-\mathcal{R}^{u}u_{0}\|_{L^{3}(Q^{(1)})}.

If gradient drift is used, set

\mathsf{Rep}_{\nabla u}(\mathbf{D};\boldsymbol{\zeta}):=\|\nabla u_{1}-\mathcal{R}^{\nabla u}\nabla u_{0}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{2}_{x}(Q^{(1)})}.

The model and covariance drift coordinates are

\mathsf{Rep}_{U}(\mathbf{D};\boldsymbol{\zeta}):=\|U_{1-\zeta_{1}}-\mathcal{R}^{U}U_{0-\zeta_{0}}\|_{L^{3}(Q^{(1)})},

\mathsf{Rep}_{R}(\mathbf{D};\boldsymbol{\zeta}):=\|R_{1-\zeta_{1}}-\mathcal{R}^{R}R_{0-\zeta_{0}}\|_{L^{3/2}(Q^{(1)})}.

The residual-source and harmonic pressure drift coordinates are

\mathsf{Rep}_{E}(\mathbf{D};\boldsymbol{\zeta}):=\|E_{F,1-\zeta_{1}}-\mathcal{R}^{E}E_{F,0-\zeta_{0}}\|_{X_{\mathrm{src}}^{(1)}},

\mathsf{Rep}_{\mathrm{harm}}(\mathbf{D};\boldsymbol{\zeta}):=\|p_{\mathrm{harm},1-\zeta_{1}}-\mathcal{R}^{\mathrm{harm}}p_{\mathrm{harm},0-\zeta_{0}}\|_{Y_{\mathrm{harm}}^{(3/2),(1)}}.

Finally define the auxiliary finite-window drift coordinates

\mathsf{Rep}_{\Pi}:=\|\Pi_{1-\zeta_{1}}-\mathcal{R}^{\Pi}\Pi_{0-\zeta_{0}}\|_{\mathcal{P}},

\mathsf{Rep}_{\Phi}:=\|\Phi_{1-\zeta_{1}}-\mathcal{R}^{\Phi}\Phi_{0-\zeta_{0}}\|_{\mathcal{F}},\qquad\mathsf{Rep}_{T}:=\|T_{1-\zeta_{1}}-\mathcal{R}^{T}T_{0-\zeta_{0}}\|_{\mathcal{T}}.

Remark B.67

(Gate/slack convention)
.

The slack coordinate
s
may be transported passively in the reproduction
coordinates. Gate and slack mismatches are accounted for in the dedicated
gate/slack component geometry.

B.23
Active Source Reproduction Estimate

Assumption B.68

(Active source realization)
.

On the representatives used in this section,

F^{\mathrm{act}}_{1-\zeta_{1}}=\eta_{1}u_{1}\otimes u_{1},

where
\eta_{1}
is a fixed bounded cutoff multiplier on
Q^{(1)}
. The
source
F^{\mathrm{act}}_{0-\zeta_{0}}
is transported by the fixed map

\mathcal{R}^{\mathrm{src}}
. No commutation between
\mathcal{R}^{\mathrm{src}}
and the quadratic
velocity source is assumed except through the leakage coordinate below.

Definition B.69

(Active source drift and leakage)
.

The active source drift is

\mathsf{Rep}_{F^{\mathrm{act}}}(\mathbf{D};\boldsymbol{\zeta}):=\|F^{\mathrm{act}}_{1-\zeta_{1}}-\mathcal{R}^{\mathrm{src}}F^{\mathrm{act}}_{0-\zeta_{0}}\|_{X_{\mathrm{src}}^{(1)}}.

The reproduction source leakage coordinate is

\mathsf{Leak}_{\mathrm{rep}}^{F}(\mathbf{D}):=\|\eta_{1}(\mathcal{R}^{u}u_{0})\otimes(\mathcal{R}^{u}u_{0})-\mathcal{R}^{\mathrm{src}}F^{\mathrm{act}}_{0-\zeta_{0}}\|_{X_{\mathrm{src}}^{(1)}}.

This term records cutoff or reproduction-map mismatch not captured by

\mathsf{Rep}_{u}
.

Proposition B.70

(Active source reproduction)
.

Assume
Section

B.23
. Assume also that

X_{\mathrm{src}}^{(1)}=L^{3/2}(Q^{(1)})^{3\times 3}
, or that the

X_{\mathrm{src}}^{(1)}
-norm is dominated by a fixed multiple of this norm on the
sources considered here.
Assume

\|u_{1}\|_{L^{3}(Q^{(1)})}+\|\mathcal{R}^{u}u_{0}\|_{L^{3}(Q^{(1)})}\leq M_{U}.

Then

\mathsf{Rep}_{F^{\mathrm{act}}}(\mathbf{D};\boldsymbol{\zeta})\leq C_{\eta}M_{U}\mathsf{Rep}_{u}(\mathbf{D};\boldsymbol{\zeta})+C\mathsf{Leak}_{\mathrm{rep}}^{F}(\mathbf{D}).

Proof.

By the triangle inequality and
Section

B.23
,

\displaystyle\mathsf{Rep}_{F^{\mathrm{act}}}(\mathbf{D};\boldsymbol{\zeta})

\displaystyle\leq\|\eta_{1}u_{1}\otimes u_{1}-\eta_{1}(\mathcal{R}^{u}u_{0})\otimes(\mathcal{R}^{u}u_{0})\|_{X_{\mathrm{src}}^{(1)}}

\displaystyle+\|\eta_{1}(\mathcal{R}^{u}u_{0})\otimes(\mathcal{R}^{u}u_{0})-\mathcal{R}^{\mathrm{src}}F^{\mathrm{act}}_{0-\zeta_{0}}\|_{X_{\mathrm{src}}^{(1)}}.

The second term is
\mathsf{Leak}_{\mathrm{rep}}^{F}(\mathbf{D})
. For the first term, write

u_{1}\otimes u_{1}-(\mathcal{R}^{u}u_{0})\otimes(\mathcal{R}^{u}u_{0})

as

(u_{1}-\mathcal{R}^{u}u_{0})\otimes u_{1}+(\mathcal{R}^{u}u_{0})\otimes(u_{1}-\mathcal{R}^{u}u_{0}),

and use Hölder’s inequality
L^{3}\cdot L^{3}\to L^{3/2}
. Since

\eta_{1}
is fixed and bounded,

\displaystyle\|\eta_{1}u_{1}\otimes u_{1}-\eta_{1}(\mathcal{R}^{u}u_{0})\otimes(\mathcal{R}^{u}u_{0})\|_{X_{\mathrm{src}}^{(1)}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\eta}\|u_{1}-\mathcal{R}^{u}u_{0}\|_{L^{3}(Q^{(1)})}\left(\|u_{1}\|_{L^{3}(Q^{(1)})}+\|\mathcal{R}^{u}u_{0}\|_{L^{3}(Q^{(1)})}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C_{\eta}M_{U}\mathsf{Rep}_{u}(\mathbf{D};\boldsymbol{\zeta}).

Combining the two bounds proves the estimate.
∎

B.24
Model Source Reproduction Estimate

Assumption B.71

(Model source realization)
.

On the representatives used in this section,

F^{\mathrm{mod}}_{1-\zeta_{1}}=\eta_{1}\bigl(U_{1-\zeta_{1}}\otimes U_{1-\zeta_{1}}+R_{1-\zeta_{1}}\bigr),

with the same fixed bounded multiplier
\eta_{1}
. The transported model
source
\mathcal{R}^{\mathrm{src}}F^{\mathrm{mod}}_{0-\zeta_{0}}
is compared with the
model expression built from
\mathcal{R}^{U}U_{0-\zeta_{0}}
and

\mathcal{R}^{R}R_{0-\zeta_{0}}
through the leakage coordinate below.

Definition B.72

(Model source drift and leakage)
.

The model source drift is

\mathsf{Rep}_{F^{\mathrm{mod}}}(\mathbf{D};\boldsymbol{\zeta}):=\|F^{\mathrm{mod}}_{1-\zeta_{1}}-\mathcal{R}^{\mathrm{src}}F^{\mathrm{mod}}_{0-\zeta_{0}}\|_{X_{\mathrm{src}}^{(1)}}.

The model reproduction leakage coordinate

\mathsf{Leak}_{\mathrm{rep}}^{F,\mathrm{mod}}(\mathbf{D};\boldsymbol{\zeta})

is defined by

\displaystyle\mathsf{Leak}_{\mathrm{rep}}^{F,\mathrm{mod}}(\mathbf{D};\boldsymbol{\zeta})

\displaystyle:=\|\eta_{1}[(\mathcal{R}^{U}U_{0-\zeta_{0}})\otimes(\mathcal{R}^{U}U_{0-\zeta_{0}})+\mathcal{R}^{R}R_{0-\zeta_{0}}]

\displaystyle-\mathcal{R}^{\mathrm{src}}F^{\mathrm{mod}}_{0-\zeta_{0}}\|_{X_{\mathrm{src}}^{(1)}}.

It records cutoff and reproduction-map noncommutation for

F^{\mathrm{mod}}=\eta(U\otimes U+R)
.

Proposition B.73

(Model source reproduction)
.

Assume
Section

B.24
. Assume also that

X_{\mathrm{src}}^{(1)}=L^{3/2}(Q^{(1)})^{3\times 3}
, or that the

X_{\mathrm{src}}^{(1)}
-norm is dominated by a fixed multiple of this norm on the
sources considered here.
Assume

\|U_{1-\zeta_{1}}\|_{L^{3}(Q^{(1)})}+\|\mathcal{R}^{U}U_{0-\zeta_{0}}\|_{L^{3}(Q^{(1)})}\leq M_{U}.

Then

\mathsf{Rep}_{F^{\mathrm{mod}}}(\mathbf{D};\boldsymbol{\zeta})\leq C_{\eta}M_{U}\mathsf{Rep}_{U}(\mathbf{D};\boldsymbol{\zeta})+C_{\eta}\mathsf{Rep}_{R}(\mathbf{D};\boldsymbol{\zeta})+C\mathsf{Leak}_{\mathrm{rep}}^{F,\mathrm{mod}}(\mathbf{D};\boldsymbol{\zeta}).

Proof.

By the triangle inequality and
Section

B.24
,

\displaystyle\mathsf{Rep}_{F^{\mathrm{mod}}}(\mathbf{D};\boldsymbol{\zeta})

\displaystyle\leq\|\eta_{1}[U_{1-\zeta_{1}}\otimes U_{1-\zeta_{1}}-(\mathcal{R}^{U}U_{0-\zeta_{0}})\otimes(\mathcal{R}^{U}U_{0-\zeta_{0}})]\|_{X_{\mathrm{src}}^{(1)}}

\displaystyle+\|\eta_{1}[R_{1-\zeta_{1}}-\mathcal{R}^{R}R_{0-\zeta_{0}}]\|_{X_{\mathrm{src}}^{(1)}}+\mathsf{Leak}_{\mathrm{rep}}^{F,\mathrm{mod}}(\mathbf{D};\boldsymbol{\zeta}).

The quadratic term is estimated by expanding the difference of tensor products
and using
L^{3}\cdot L^{3}\to L^{3/2}
:

\displaystyle\|\eta_{1}[U_{1-\zeta_{1}}\otimes U_{1-\zeta_{1}}-(\mathcal{R}^{U}U_{0-\zeta_{0}})\otimes(\mathcal{R}^{U}U_{0-\zeta_{0}})]\|_{X_{\mathrm{src}}^{(1)}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\eta}\mathsf{Rep}_{U}(\mathbf{D};\boldsymbol{\zeta})\left(\|U_{1-\zeta_{1}}\|_{L^{3}(Q^{(1)})}+\|\mathcal{R}^{U}U_{0-\zeta_{0}}\|_{L^{3}(Q^{(1)})}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C_{\eta}M_{U}\mathsf{Rep}_{U}(\mathbf{D};\boldsymbol{\zeta}).

The covariance term is linear:

\|\eta_{1}[R_{1-\zeta_{1}}-\mathcal{R}^{R}R_{0-\zeta_{0}}]\|_{X_{\mathrm{src}}^{(1)}}\leq C_{\eta}\mathsf{Rep}_{R}(\mathbf{D};\boldsymbol{\zeta}).

Combining the estimates proves the claim.
∎

B.25
Pressure Reproduction and Riesz-Map Intertwining

Definition B.74

(Pressure reproduction commutator)
.

For a source
F
, define

\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{R}}^{\mathrm{prs}}(F):=R_{i}R_{j}(\mathcal{R}^{\mathrm{src}}F_{ij})-\mathcal{R}^{\mathrm{prs}}R_{i}R_{j}(F_{ij}).

This measures the failure of the reproduction map to intertwine with the
Riesz-transform pressure operator.

Assumption B.75

(Fixed-window pressure intertwining budget)
.

There is a reproduction pressure-leakage coordinate

\mathsf{Leak}_{\mathcal{R}}^{\mathrm{prs}}(F)
and a finite-window constant
C_{\mathcal{R}}
such
that

\|\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{R}}^{\mathrm{prs}}(F)\|_{Y_{\mathrm{prs}}^{(1)}}\leq C_{\mathcal{R}}\mathsf{Leak}_{\mathcal{R}}^{\mathrm{prs}}(F).

Definition B.76

(Active pressure drift)
.

The active pressure reproduction drift is

\mathsf{Rep}_{p^{\mathrm{act}}}(\mathbf{D};\boldsymbol{\zeta}):=\|p^{\mathrm{act}}_{1-\zeta_{1}}-\mathcal{R}^{\mathrm{prs}}p^{\mathrm{act}}_{0-\zeta_{0}}\|_{Y_{\mathrm{prs}}^{(1)}}.

Assumption B.77

(Active pressure realization)
.

On the representatives used in this section,

p^{\mathrm{act}}_{j-\zeta_{j}}=R_{i}R_{j}(F^{\mathrm{act}}_{j-\zeta_{j},ij}),\qquad j=0,1.

Proposition B.78

(Active pressure reproduction)
.

Assume
Section

B.25
and

Section

B.25
. Assume also the fixed-window
Calderon–Zygmund source-to-pressure bound

\|R_{i}R_{j}G_{ij}\|_{Y_{\mathrm{prs}}^{(1)}}\leq C_{CZ}\|G\|_{X_{\mathrm{src}}^{(1)}}.

Then

\mathsf{Rep}_{p^{\mathrm{act}}}(\mathbf{D};\boldsymbol{\zeta})\leq C_{CZ}\mathsf{Rep}_{F^{\mathrm{act}}}(\mathbf{D};\boldsymbol{\zeta})+C_{\mathcal{R}}\mathsf{Leak}_{\mathcal{R}}^{\mathrm{prs}}(F^{\mathrm{act}}_{0-\zeta_{0}}).

Proof.

By
Section

B.25
,

\displaystyle p^{\mathrm{act}}_{1-\zeta_{1}}-\mathcal{R}^{\mathrm{prs}}p^{\mathrm{act}}_{0-\zeta_{0}}

\displaystyle=R_{i}R_{j}(F^{\mathrm{act}}_{1-\zeta_{1},ij})-\mathcal{R}^{\mathrm{prs}}R_{i}R_{j}(F^{\mathrm{act}}_{0-\zeta_{0},ij})

\displaystyle=R_{i}R_{j}\bigl(F^{\mathrm{act}}_{1-\zeta_{1},ij}-\mathcal{R}^{\mathrm{src}}F^{\mathrm{act}}_{0-\zeta_{0},ij}\bigr)+\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{R}}^{\mathrm{prs}}(F^{\mathrm{act}}_{0-\zeta_{0}}).

Taking the
Y_{\mathrm{prs}}^{(1)}
-norm, applying the Calderon–Zygmund bound to the
first term, and using
Section

B.25
for the second
term gives the estimate.
∎

Remark B.79

(No silent pressure transport)
.

This section is deliberately conditional. The paper does not assume that
Riesz transforms commute with reproduction maps without the commutator budget
in
Section

B.25
.

B.26
Harmonic Pressure Reproduction

Definition B.80

(Harmonic pressure drift)
.

The harmonic pressure drift is the coordinate

\mathsf{Rep}_{\mathrm{harm}}(\mathbf{D};\boldsymbol{\zeta})=\|p_{\mathrm{harm},1-\zeta_{1}}-\mathcal{R}^{\mathrm{harm}}p_{\mathrm{harm},0-\zeta_{0}}\|_{Y_{\mathrm{harm}}^{(3/2),(1)}}.

Remark B.81

(Coordinate absorption convention)
.

In the main theorem,
\mathsf{Rep}_{\mathrm{harm}}
is included directly in the sharp
chain norm. This is a coordinate absorption convention: no separate harmonic
reproduction approximation theorem is required for the finite-window closure
proved here.

B.27
Total Reproduction Residual

Definition B.82

(Total reproduction residual)
.

The total reproduction residual is

\displaystyle\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{rep}}(\mathbf{D};\boldsymbol{\zeta})

\displaystyle:=\mathsf{Rep}_{u}+\mathsf{Rep}_{U}+\mathsf{Rep}_{R}+\mathsf{Rep}_{E}+\mathsf{Rep}_{F^{\mathrm{act}}}+\mathsf{Rep}_{F^{\mathrm{mod}}}

\displaystyle+\mathsf{Rep}_{p^{\mathrm{act}}}+\mathsf{Rep}_{\mathrm{harm}}+\mathsf{Rep}_{\Pi}+\mathsf{Rep}_{\Phi}+\mathsf{Rep}_{T},

with all terms evaluated at
(\mathbf{D};\boldsymbol{\zeta})
.

Remark B.83

(Residual splitting)
.

One may split

\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{rep}}=\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{rep}}^{\mathrm{phys}}+\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{rep}}^{\mathrm{src}}+\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{rep}}^{\mathrm{prs}}+\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{rep}}^{\mathrm{aux}},

but the present paper keeps the single total residual notation.

B.28
Sharp Chain Reproduction Norm

Definition B.84

(Sharp chain reproduction norm)
.

Let
\|D_{j}\|_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{pkg}}^{\sharp}
denote the sharp single-window package
norm imported from the previous branches. Define

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{D}\|_{\mathrm{chain}}^{\sharp,\mathrm{rep}}

\displaystyle:=\|D_{0}\|_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{pkg}}^{\sharp}+\|D_{1}\|_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{pkg}}^{\sharp}+\mathsf{Rep}_{u}+\mathsf{Rep}_{U}+\mathsf{Rep}_{R}+\mathsf{Rep}_{E}+\mathsf{Rep}_{\mathrm{harm}}

\displaystyle+\mathsf{Rep}_{\Pi}+\mathsf{Rep}_{\Phi}+\mathsf{Rep}_{T}+\mathsf{Leak}_{\mathrm{rep}}^{F}+\mathsf{Leak}_{\mathrm{rep}}^{F,\mathrm{mod}}+\mathsf{Leak}_{\mathcal{R}}^{\mathrm{prs}}(F^{\mathrm{act}}_{0-\zeta_{0}}).

The corresponding quotient distance is

\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{chain}}^{\sharp,\mathrm{rep}}(\mathbf{D},\Gamma_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}^{\mathrm{chain}}):=\inf_{\boldsymbol{\zeta}\in\Gamma_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}^{\mathrm{chain}}}\|\mathbf{D}-\boldsymbol{\zeta}\|_{\mathrm{chain}}^{\sharp,\mathrm{rep}}.

Assumption B.85

(Sharp chain near-minimizer)
.

The selected same-chain representative satisfies

\|\mathbf{D}-\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{*}\|_{\mathrm{chain}}^{\sharp,\mathrm{rep}}\leq\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{chain}}^{\sharp,\mathrm{rep}}(\mathbf{D},\Gamma_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}^{\mathrm{chain}})+\delta_{\mathrm{rep}}.

B.29
Main Reproduction Absorption Theorems

Theorem B.86

(Bounded-amplitude reproduction drift absorption)
.

Let
\mathbf{D}=(D_{0},D_{1})
be a two-window sharp package pair. Assume:

(i)

a fixed finite-window reproduction map
\mathcal{R}_{0\to 1}
;

(ii)

the same-chain representative
\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{*}
;

(iii)

the near-minimizer condition in
Section

B.28
;

(iv)

the source and pressure realization hypotheses

Sections

B.23
,
B.24
and
B.25
;

(v)

the bounded-amplitude condition

\|u_{1}\|_{L^{3}}+\|\mathcal{R}^{u}u_{0}\|_{L^{3}}+\|U_{1-\zeta_{1,*}}\|_{L^{3}}+\|\mathcal{R}^{U}U_{0-\zeta_{0,*}}\|_{L^{3}}\leq M_{U};

(vi)

the pressure-intertwining budget in

Section

B.25
.

Then

\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{rep}}(\mathbf{D};\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{*})\leq C_{\mathrm{rep}}(M_{U})\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{chain}}^{\sharp,\mathrm{rep}}(\mathbf{D},\Gamma_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}^{\mathrm{chain}})+C_{\mathrm{rep}}(M_{U})\delta_{\mathrm{rep}}.

Proof.

All estimates below are evaluated at the same-chain representative

\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{*}
. By
Section

B.23
,

\mathsf{Rep}_{F^{\mathrm{act}}}(\mathbf{D};\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{*})\leq C(M_{U})\bigl[\mathsf{Rep}_{u}(\mathbf{D};\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{*})+\mathsf{Leak}_{\mathrm{rep}}^{F}(\mathbf{D})\bigr].

By
Section

B.24
,

\mathsf{Rep}_{F^{\mathrm{mod}}}(\mathbf{D};\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{*})\leq C(M_{U})\bigl[\mathsf{Rep}_{U}(\mathbf{D};\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{*})+\mathsf{Rep}_{R}(\mathbf{D};\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{*})+\mathsf{Leak}_{\mathrm{rep}}^{F,\mathrm{mod}}(\mathbf{D};\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{*})\bigr].

By
Section

B.25
and the active source estimate,

\mathsf{Rep}_{p^{\mathrm{act}}}(\mathbf{D};\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{*})\leq C(M_{U})\bigl[\mathsf{Rep}_{u}(\mathbf{D};\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{*})+\mathsf{Leak}_{\mathrm{rep}}^{F}(\mathbf{D})+\mathsf{Leak}_{\mathcal{R}}^{\mathrm{prs}}(F^{\mathrm{act}}_{0-\zeta_{0,*}})\bigr].

The remaining terms in
\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{rep}}
,

\mathsf{Rep}_{u},\quad\mathsf{Rep}_{U},\quad\mathsf{Rep}_{R},\quad\mathsf{Rep}_{E},\quad\mathsf{Rep}_{\mathrm{harm}},\quad\mathsf{Rep}_{\Pi},\quad\mathsf{Rep}_{\Phi},\quad\mathsf{Rep}_{T},

are primitive coordinates included in

\|\mathbf{D}-\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{*}\|_{\mathrm{chain}}^{\sharp,\mathrm{rep}}
. Therefore

\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{rep}}(\mathbf{D};\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{*})\leq C_{\mathrm{rep}}(M_{U})\|\mathbf{D}-\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{*}\|_{\mathrm{chain}}^{\sharp,\mathrm{rep}}.

The near-minimizer condition in
Section

B.28
gives the
claimed quotient-distance estimate.
∎

Corollary B.87

(Weighted reproduction absorption)
.

If a weighted norm satisfies

\|\mathbf{D}\|_{\mathrm{chain}}^{\sharp,\mathrm{rep},\omega}\geq\frac{C_{\mathrm{rep}}(M_{U})}{\eta_{\mathrm{rep}}}\|\mathbf{D}\|_{\mathrm{chain}}^{\sharp,\mathrm{rep}},

and the same-chain representative is a weighted near-minimizer with error

\delta_{\mathrm{rep}}^{\omega}
, then

\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{rep}}(\mathbf{D};\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{*})\leq\eta_{\mathrm{rep}}\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{chain}}^{\sharp,\mathrm{rep},\omega}(\mathbf{D},\Gamma_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}^{\mathrm{chain}})+\eta_{\mathrm{rep}}\delta_{\mathrm{rep}}^{\omega}.

Proof.

The proof of
Theorem

B.86
first gives

\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{rep}}(\mathbf{D};\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{*})\leq C_{\mathrm{rep}}(M_{U})\|\mathbf{D}-\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{*}\|_{\mathrm{chain}}^{\sharp,\mathrm{rep}}.

The weighted norm domination implies

C_{\mathrm{rep}}(M_{U})\|\mathbf{D}-\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{*}\|_{\mathrm{chain}}^{\sharp,\mathrm{rep}}\leq\eta_{\mathrm{rep}}\|\mathbf{D}-\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{*}\|_{\mathrm{chain}}^{\sharp,\mathrm{rep},\omega}.

Applying the weighted near-minimizer property gives the result. This is a
normalization of the accounting norm, not a new PDE estimate.
∎

Definition B.88

(Quadratic chain reproduction norm)
.

The quadratic chain norm is obtained from the sharp chain norm by retaining the
nonlinear product factors

\mathsf{Rep}_{u}\bigl(\|u_{1}\|_{L^{3}}+\|\mathcal{R}^{u}u_{0}\|_{L^{3}}\bigr)

and

\mathsf{Rep}_{U}\bigl(\|U_{1-\zeta_{1}}\|_{L^{3}}+\|\mathcal{R}^{U}U_{0-\zeta_{0}}\|_{L^{3}}\bigr)

instead of replacing them by a finite-amplitude constant. More precisely, it
is any chain functional
\|\cdot\|_{\mathrm{chain}}^{\sharp,\mathrm{rep},\mathrm{quad}}
that
dominates the primitive drift coordinates, the leakage coordinates, and the
two displayed nonlinear products. It defines the quotient distance

\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{chain}}^{\sharp,\mathrm{rep},\mathrm{quad}}(\mathbf{D},\Gamma_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}^{\mathrm{chain}}):=\inf_{\boldsymbol{\zeta}\in\Gamma_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}^{\mathrm{chain}}}\|\mathbf{D}-\boldsymbol{\zeta}\|_{\mathrm{chain}}^{\sharp,\mathrm{rep},\mathrm{quad}}.

Theorem B.89

(Quadratic chain geometry)
.

Assume the hypotheses of

Sections

B.23
,
B.24
,
B.25
and
B.25
,
but do not assume bounded amplitude. Suppose the selected same-chain
representative satisfies the quadratic near-minimizer condition

\|\mathbf{D}-\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{*}\|_{\mathrm{chain}}^{\sharp,\mathrm{rep},\mathrm{quad}}\leq\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{chain}}^{\sharp,\mathrm{rep},\mathrm{quad}}(\mathbf{D},\Gamma_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}^{\mathrm{chain}})+\delta_{\mathrm{rep}}^{\mathrm{quad}}.

Then

\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{rep}}(\mathbf{D};\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{*})\leq C_{\mathrm{rep}}^{\mathrm{quad}}\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{chain}}^{\sharp,\mathrm{rep},\mathrm{quad}}(\mathbf{D},\Gamma_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}^{\mathrm{chain}})+C_{\mathrm{rep}}^{\mathrm{quad}}\delta_{\mathrm{rep}}^{\mathrm{quad}}.

Proof.

Repeat the proofs of

Sections

B.23
and
B.24
without
linearizing the amplitude factors by
M_{U}
. This gives bounds by the
quadratic products built into

\|\mathbf{D}-\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{*}\|_{\mathrm{chain}}^{\sharp,\mathrm{rep},\mathrm{quad}}
, together with the
primitive and leakage coordinates. The active pressure estimate in

Section

B.25
then bounds the pressure drift by the
same quadratic chain coordinates and the pressure-intertwining leakage. Hence

\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{rep}}(\mathbf{D};\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{*})\leq C_{\mathrm{rep}}^{\mathrm{quad}}\|\mathbf{D}-\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{*}\|_{\mathrm{chain}}^{\sharp,\mathrm{rep},\mathrm{quad}}.

The quadratic near-minimizer condition completes the proof. The price is that
the geometry has changed; this is not a theorem in the original linear chain
geometry.
∎

Corollary B.90

(Normalized quotient amplitude)
.

On the normalized chain quotient sphere

\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{chain}}^{\sharp,\mathrm{rep}}(\mathbf{D},\Gamma_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}^{\mathrm{chain}})=1,

assume the selected representative satisfies

\|u_{1}\|_{L^{3}}+\|\mathcal{R}^{u}u_{0}\|_{L^{3}}+\|U_{1-\zeta_{1,*}}\|_{L^{3}}+\|\mathcal{R}^{U}U_{0-\zeta_{0,*}}\|_{L^{3}}\leq C_{\mathrm{amp}}.

Then the bounded-amplitude reproduction absorption theorem applies on that
normalized quotient
with
M_{U}=C_{\mathrm{amp}}
.

Proof.

This is exactly the bounded-amplitude hypothesis in

Theorem

B.86
, with

M_{U}=C_{\mathrm{amp}}
, restricted to the normalized quotient sphere.
∎

Corollary B.91

(Finite-chain bookkeeping)
.

For a finite chain
D_{0},\ldots,D_{K}
, define

\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{rep}}^{[0,K]}:=\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{rep}}(D_{k},D_{k+1};\zeta_{k},\zeta_{k+1}).

Assume the finite-chain quotient distance is defined from the sum of the
pairwise chain norms. If each neighboring pair satisfies the
bounded-amplitude reproduction theorem with constants bounded on the finite
chain, then

\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{rep}}^{[0,K]}\leq C_{\mathrm{rep}}^{[0,K]}\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{chain}}^{\sharp,\mathrm{rep},[0,K]}((D_{0},\ldots,D_{K}),\Gamma_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}^{\mathrm{chain},[0,K]})+C_{\mathrm{rep}}^{[0,K]}\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\delta_{\mathrm{rep},k}.

Proof.

Apply
Theorem

B.86
to each neighboring
pair and sum over
0\leq k<K
. Since the chain is finite, the pairwise
constants are bounded by a finite constant
C_{\mathrm{rep}}^{[0,K]}
. The sum of
the pairwise chain distances is bounded by the finite-chain quotient distance
by the definition of the latter. This yields the displayed estimate.
∎

Remark B.92

(Finite-chain status)
.

The finite-chain constant may depend on
K
. This is not a scale-uniform
iteration theorem.

Gate/Slack Budget-Violation Absorption

B.30
Channel Index Set and Budget Variables

Definition B.93

(Gate/slack channel set)
.

Let
\mathfrak{A}
be a finite set of gate/slack channels. A typical model is

\mathfrak{A}\subset\{\mathrm{prs},\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{rep},\mathrm{flux},\mathrm{trace},\mathrm{tax},\mathrm{adm}\}.

The exact finite set is part of the finite-window package specification.

Definition B.94

(Budget, threshold, and slack coordinates)
.

For every
a\in\mathfrak{A}
, a package representative
D-\zeta
carries:

(i)

a nonnegative used-budget functional
B_{a}(D;\zeta)\geq 0
;

(ii)

a nonnegative allowed-threshold functional
\tau_{a}(D;\zeta)\geq 0
;

(iii)

a nonnegative slack coordinate
s_{a}(D;\zeta)\geq 0
;

(iv)

weights
\omega_{a}>0
and
\lambda_{a}>0
.

The ideal finite-window ledger relation is

B_{a}(D;\zeta)+s_{a}(D;\zeta)=\tau_{a}(D;\zeta).

If this identity holds with
s_{a}(D;\zeta)\geq 0
, then the gate is admissible:

B_{a}(D;\zeta)\leq\tau_{a}(D;\zeta).

Remark B.95

(Finite-window interpretation)
.

The quantities
B_{a},\tau_{a},s_{a}
are package coordinates or functionals. This
paper does not derive them from a PDE estimate; it specifies how they are used
once included in the finite-window package.

B.31
Positive-Part Gate Violation

Definition B.96

(Positive-part gate violation)
.

For
a\in\mathfrak{A}
, define

\mathsf{Gate}_{a}(D;\zeta):=\bigl(B_{a}(D;\zeta)-\tau_{a}(D;\zeta)\bigr)_{+}.

Thus
\mathsf{Gate}_{a}(D;\zeta)=0
when
B_{a}(D;\zeta)\leq\tau_{a}(D;\zeta)
, and

\mathsf{Gate}_{a}(D;\zeta)=B_{a}(D;\zeta)-\tau_{a}(D;\zeta)

when
B_{a}(D;\zeta)>\tau_{a}(D;\zeta)
.

Lemma B.97

(Positive-part Lipschitz estimate)
.

For scalar budget pairs
(B,\tau)
and

(\widetilde{B},\widetilde{\tau})
,

\left|(B-\tau)_{+}-(\widetilde{B}-\widetilde{\tau})_{+}\right|\leq|B-\widetilde{B}|+|\tau-\widetilde{\tau}|.

Proof.

For real numbers
x,y
, the map
x\mapsto x_{+}
is
1
-Lipschitz. Indeed,
if
x\geq y
, then

x_{+}-y_{+}\leq x-y,

because either
y\geq 0
, in which case equality holds, or
y<0
, in which
case
x_{+}-y_{+}\leq x_{+}\leq x-y
. Exchanging
x
and
y
gives

|x_{+}-y_{+}|\leq|x-y|

for all
x,y\in\mathbb{R}
. Apply this with
x=B-\tau
and

y=\widetilde{B}-\widetilde{\tau}
. Then

|x-y|=|(B-\widetilde{B})-(\tau-\widetilde{\tau})|\leq|B-\widetilde{B}|+|\tau-\widetilde{\tau}|,

which proves the claim.
∎

B.32
Slack Identity Mismatch

Definition B.98

(Slack identity mismatch)
.

For
a\in\mathfrak{A}
, define

\mathsf{Slack}_{a}(D;\zeta):=|B_{a}(D;\zeta)+s_{a}(D;\zeta)-\tau_{a}(D;\zeta)|.

This measures the failure of the finite-window ledger identity.

Lemma B.99

(Slack mismatch controls gate violation)
.

Assume
s_{a}(D;\zeta)\geq 0
. Then

\mathsf{Gate}_{a}(D;\zeta)\leq\mathsf{Slack}_{a}(D;\zeta).

Proof.

Write
B_{a}=B_{a}(D;\zeta)
,
\tau_{a}=\tau_{a}(D;\zeta)
, and

s_{a}=s_{a}(D;\zeta)
. Since
s_{a}\geq 0
,

B_{a}-\tau_{a}=(B_{a}+s_{a}-\tau_{a})-s_{a}\leq|B_{a}+s_{a}-\tau_{a}|.

Taking the positive part of the left-hand side gives

(B_{a}-\tau_{a})_{+}\leq|B_{a}+s_{a}-\tau_{a}|.

This is exactly
\mathsf{Gate}_{a}(D;\zeta)\leq\mathsf{Slack}_{a}(D;\zeta)
.
∎

B.33
Single-Window Gate/Slack Residual

Definition B.100

(Single-window gate/slack residual)
.

Define

\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{gs}}^{\mathrm{win}}(D;\zeta):=\sum_{a\in\mathfrak{A}}\omega_{a}\mathsf{Gate}_{a}(D;\zeta)+\sum_{a\in\mathfrak{A}}\lambda_{a}\mathsf{Slack}_{a}(D;\zeta).

Equivalently,

\displaystyle\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{gs}}^{\mathrm{win}}(D;\zeta)

\displaystyle=\sum_{a\in\mathfrak{A}}\omega_{a}\bigl(B_{a}(D;\zeta)-\tau_{a}(D;\zeta)\bigr)_{+}

\displaystyle+\sum_{a\in\mathfrak{A}}\lambda_{a}|B_{a}(D;\zeta)+s_{a}(D;\zeta)-\tau_{a}(D;\zeta)|.

Remark B.101

(Meaning of the residual)
.

If the package uses exact slack identities, then the second sum vanishes. If
the budgets are admissible, then the first sum vanishes. Thus

\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{gs}}^{\mathrm{win}}
measures both admissibility violation and
slack-ledger inconsistency.

B.34
Gate/Slack Chain Mismatch

Definition B.102

(Scalar reproduction maps)
.

For a two-window pair
\mathbf{D}=(D_{0},D_{1})
, assume that each channel
a\in\mathfrak{A}

comes with scalar reproduction maps

\mathcal{R}_{a}^{B},\qquad\mathcal{R}_{a}^{\tau},\qquad\mathcal{R}_{a}^{s},\qquad\mathcal{R}_{a}^{g}.

In the simplest finite-window model, these are identity maps on scalar budget
coordinates. They are kept explicit to allow later transport or rescaling
models.

Definition B.103

(Budget, threshold, slack, and gate reproduction mismatch)
.

For
\mathbf{D}=(D_{0},D_{1})
and
\boldsymbol{\zeta}=(\zeta_{0},\zeta_{1})
, define

\mathsf{Rep}_{a}^{B}(\mathbf{D};\boldsymbol{\zeta}):=|B_{a}(D_{1};\zeta_{1})-\mathcal{R}_{a}^{B}B_{a}(D_{0};\zeta_{0})|,

\mathsf{Rep}_{a}^{\tau}(\mathbf{D};\boldsymbol{\zeta}):=|\tau_{a}(D_{1};\zeta_{1})-\mathcal{R}_{a}^{\tau}\tau_{a}(D_{0};\zeta_{0})|,

\mathsf{Rep}_{a}^{s}(\mathbf{D};\boldsymbol{\zeta}):=|s_{a}(D_{1};\zeta_{1})-\mathcal{R}_{a}^{s}s_{a}(D_{0};\zeta_{0})|,

and

\mathsf{Rep}_{a}^{\mathrm{gate}}(\mathbf{D};\boldsymbol{\zeta}):=|\mathsf{Gate}_{a}(D_{1};\zeta_{1})-\mathcal{R}_{a}^{g}\mathsf{Gate}_{a}(D_{0};\zeta_{0})|.

Assumption B.104

(Compatibility of scalar gate transport)
.

For every channel
a\in\mathfrak{A}
, the scalar gate reproduction map is
compatible with the budget and threshold reproduction maps on the scalar
coordinates under consideration:

\mathcal{R}_{a}^{g}\bigl((B_{0}-\tau_{0})_{+}\bigr)=\bigl(\mathcal{R}_{a}^{B}B_{0}-\mathcal{R}_{a}^{\tau}\tau_{0}\bigr)_{+}.

In the identity scalar model this assumption is automatic.

Lemma B.105

(Gate mismatch from budget and threshold mismatch)
.

Assume the scalar gate compatibility condition

Section

B.34
. Then

\mathsf{Rep}_{a}^{\mathrm{gate}}(\mathbf{D};\boldsymbol{\zeta})\leq\mathsf{Rep}_{a}^{B}(\mathbf{D};\boldsymbol{\zeta})+\mathsf{Rep}_{a}^{\tau}(\mathbf{D};\boldsymbol{\zeta}).

Proof.

Set

B_{1}=B_{a}(D_{1};\zeta_{1}),\qquad\tau_{1}=\tau_{a}(D_{1};\zeta_{1}),

and

\widetilde{B}_{0}=\mathcal{R}_{a}^{B}B_{a}(D_{0};\zeta_{0}),\qquad\widetilde{\tau}_{0}=\mathcal{R}_{a}^{\tau}\tau_{a}(D_{0};\zeta_{0}).

By
Section

B.34
,

\mathcal{R}_{a}^{g}\mathsf{Gate}_{a}(D_{0};\zeta_{0})=(\widetilde{B}_{0}-\widetilde{\tau}_{0})_{+}.

Therefore

\displaystyle\mathsf{Rep}_{a}^{\mathrm{gate}}(\mathbf{D};\boldsymbol{\zeta})

\displaystyle=\left|(B_{1}-\tau_{1})_{+}-(\widetilde{B}_{0}-\widetilde{\tau}_{0})_{+}\right|

\displaystyle\leq|B_{1}-\widetilde{B}_{0}|+|\tau_{1}-\widetilde{\tau}_{0}|

by
Section

B.31
. The last line is

\mathsf{Rep}_{a}^{B}(\mathbf{D};\boldsymbol{\zeta})+\mathsf{Rep}_{a}^{\tau}(\mathbf{D};\boldsymbol{\zeta})
.
∎

Definition B.106

(Chain gate/slack residual)
.

Define

\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{gs}}^{\mathrm{chain}}(\mathbf{D};\boldsymbol{\zeta}):=\sum_{a\in\mathfrak{A}}\omega_{a}\mathsf{Rep}_{a}^{\mathrm{gate}}(\mathbf{D};\boldsymbol{\zeta})+\sum_{a\in\mathfrak{A}}\lambda_{a}\bigl(\mathsf{Rep}_{a}^{B}+\mathsf{Rep}_{a}^{\tau}+\mathsf{Rep}_{a}^{s}\bigr)(\mathbf{D};\boldsymbol{\zeta}).

This measures how budgets, thresholds, gates, and slack variables fail to
reproduce across adjacent windows.

B.35
Sharp Gate/Slack Package Norm

Definition B.107

(Sharp gate/slack package norm)
.

Let
