\|D\|_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{pkg}}^{\sharp}
denote the sharp package norm from the
previous component branches. Define

\|D\|_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{pkg}}^{\sharp,\mathrm{gs}}:=\|D\|_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{pkg}}^{\sharp}+\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{gs}}^{\mathrm{win}}(D;0).

For a shifted package,
\|D-\zeta\|_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{pkg}}^{\sharp,\mathrm{gs}}
means the same
norm evaluated on the shifted package coordinates. Define

\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{pkg}}^{\sharp,\mathrm{gs}}(D,\Gamma_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}^{\mathrm{int}}):=\inf_{\zeta\in\Gamma_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}^{\mathrm{int}}}\|D-\zeta\|_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{pkg}}^{\sharp,\mathrm{gs}}.

Assumption B.108

(Same-gauge near-minimizer)
.

The selected representative
\zeta_{*}(D)\in\Gamma_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}^{\mathrm{int}}
satisfies

\|D-\zeta_{*}\|_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{pkg}}^{\sharp,\mathrm{gs}}\leq\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{pkg}}^{\sharp,\mathrm{gs}}(D,\Gamma_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}^{\mathrm{int}})+\delta_{\mathrm{gs}}.

The same
\zeta_{*}
is used in all gate/slack coordinates.

B.36
Single-Window Gate/Slack Absorption

Theorem B.109

(Single-window absorption)
.

Assume the same-gauge near-minimizer condition in

Section

B.35
. Then

\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{gs}}^{\mathrm{win}}(D;\zeta_{*})\leq\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{pkg}}^{\sharp,\mathrm{gs}}(D,\Gamma_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}^{\mathrm{int}})+\delta_{\mathrm{gs}}.

More generally, if the sharp gate/slack norm controls the gate/slack residual
with finite constant
C_{\mathrm{gs}}
, meaning

\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{gs}}^{\mathrm{win}}(D;\zeta)\leq C_{\mathrm{gs}}\|D-\zeta\|_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{pkg}}^{\sharp,\mathrm{gs}}

for all admissible representatives, then

\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{gs}}^{\mathrm{win}}(D;\zeta_{*})\leq C_{\mathrm{gs}}\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{pkg}}^{\sharp,\mathrm{gs}}(D,\Gamma_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}^{\mathrm{int}})+C_{\mathrm{gs}}\delta_{\mathrm{gs}}.

Proof.

By definition,

\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{gs}}^{\mathrm{win}}(D;\zeta_{*})\leq\|D-\zeta_{*}\|_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{pkg}}^{\sharp,\mathrm{gs}}.

The near-minimizer condition then gives the target estimate.

The same proof gives the comparable-weight version: first apply

\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{gs}}^{\mathrm{win}}(D;\zeta_{*})\leq C_{\mathrm{gs}}\|D-\zeta_{*}\|_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{pkg}}^{\sharp,\mathrm{gs}},

and then use
Section

B.35
.
∎

Remark B.110

(Accounting status)
.

This is an accounting absorption theorem. It is not a new PDE estimate. Its
role is to make admissibility and slack violation visible to the package
geometry.

B.37
Sharp Chain Gate/Slack Norm

Definition B.111

(Sharp chain gate/slack norm)
.

Let
\|\mathbf{D}\|_{\mathrm{chain}}^{\sharp,\mathrm{rep}}
denote the sharp chain reproduction norm
from the reproduction-drift branch. Define

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{D}\|_{\mathrm{chain}}^{\sharp,\mathrm{gs}}

\displaystyle:=\|\mathbf{D}\|_{\mathrm{chain}}^{\sharp,\mathrm{rep}}+\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{gs}}^{\mathrm{win}}(D_{0};0)+\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{gs}}^{\mathrm{win}}(D_{1};0)

\displaystyle+\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{gs}}^{\mathrm{chain}}(\mathbf{D};0).

For shifted chain representatives,

\|\mathbf{D}-\boldsymbol{\zeta}\|_{\mathrm{chain}}^{\sharp,\mathrm{gs}}
means all terms are evaluated on

(D_{0}-\zeta_{0},D_{1}-\zeta_{1})
. Define

\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{chain}}^{\sharp,\mathrm{gs}}(\mathbf{D},\Gamma_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}^{\mathrm{chain}}):=\inf_{\boldsymbol{\zeta}\in\Gamma_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}^{\mathrm{chain}}}\|\mathbf{D}-\boldsymbol{\zeta}\|_{\mathrm{chain}}^{\sharp,\mathrm{gs}}.

Assumption B.112

(Same-chain gate/slack near-minimizer)
.

The selected same-chain representative
\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{*}\in\Gamma_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}^{\mathrm{chain}}
satisfies

\|\mathbf{D}-\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{*}\|_{\mathrm{chain}}^{\sharp,\mathrm{gs}}\leq\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{chain}}^{\sharp,\mathrm{gs}}(\mathbf{D},\Gamma_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}^{\mathrm{chain}})+\delta_{\mathrm{gs}}^{\mathrm{chain}}.

B.38
Main Gate/Slack Absorption Theorems

Theorem B.113

(Chain gate/slack mismatch absorption)
.

Let
\mathbf{D}=(D_{0},D_{1})
be a two-window sharp package pair. Assume the
same-chain near-minimizer condition in
Section

B.37
. Then

\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{gs}}^{\mathrm{chain}}(\mathbf{D};\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{*})\leq\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{chain}}^{\sharp,\mathrm{gs}}(\mathbf{D},\Gamma_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}^{\mathrm{chain}})+\delta_{\mathrm{gs}}^{\mathrm{chain}}.

More generally, if the sharp chain norm controls the chain gate/slack residual
with finite constant
C_{\mathrm{gs}}^{\mathrm{chain}}
, meaning

\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{gs}}^{\mathrm{chain}}(\mathbf{D};\boldsymbol{\zeta})\leq C_{\mathrm{gs}}^{\mathrm{chain}}\|\mathbf{D}-\boldsymbol{\zeta}\|_{\mathrm{chain}}^{\sharp,\mathrm{gs}},

then

\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{gs}}^{\mathrm{chain}}(\mathbf{D};\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{*})\leq C_{\mathrm{gs}}^{\mathrm{chain}}\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{chain}}^{\sharp,\mathrm{gs}}(\mathbf{D},\Gamma_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}^{\mathrm{chain}})+C_{\mathrm{gs}}^{\mathrm{chain}}\delta_{\mathrm{gs}}^{\mathrm{chain}}.

Proof.

The chain gate/slack residual is one of the nonnegative components of the
sharp chain gate/slack norm. Hence

\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{gs}}^{\mathrm{chain}}(\mathbf{D};\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{*})\leq\|\mathbf{D}-\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{*}\|_{\mathrm{chain}}^{\sharp,\mathrm{gs}}.

The same-chain near-minimizer property gives the target estimate.

The comparable-weight version follows by inserting the assumed bound

\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{gs}}^{\mathrm{chain}}(\mathbf{D};\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{*})\leq C_{\mathrm{gs}}^{\mathrm{chain}}\|\mathbf{D}-\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{*}\|_{\mathrm{chain}}^{\sharp,\mathrm{gs}}

before applying
Section

B.37
.
∎

B.39
Weighted Gate/Slack Absorption

Corollary B.114

(Weighted single-window gate/slack absorption)
.

If a weighted norm satisfies

\|D\|_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{pkg}}^{\sharp,\mathrm{gs},\omega}\geq\frac{C_{\mathrm{gs}}}{\eta_{\mathrm{gs}}}\|D\|_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{pkg}}^{\sharp,\mathrm{gs}},

and the selected representative is a weighted near-minimizer with error

\delta_{\mathrm{gs}}^{\omega}
, the target estimate is

\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{gs}}^{\mathrm{win}}(D;\zeta_{*})\leq\eta_{\mathrm{gs}}\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{pkg}}^{\sharp,\mathrm{gs},\omega}(D,\Gamma_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}^{\mathrm{int}})+\eta_{\mathrm{gs}}\delta_{\mathrm{gs}}^{\omega}.

Proof.

By the comparable-weight form of
Theorem

B.109
,

\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{gs}}^{\mathrm{win}}(D;\zeta_{*})\leq C_{\mathrm{gs}}\|D-\zeta_{*}\|_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{pkg}}^{\sharp,\mathrm{gs}}.

The domination assumption gives

C_{\mathrm{gs}}\|D-\zeta_{*}\|_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{pkg}}^{\sharp,\mathrm{gs}}\leq\eta_{\mathrm{gs}}\|D-\zeta_{*}\|_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{pkg}}^{\sharp,\mathrm{gs},\omega}.

Using the weighted near-minimizer property yields the stated estimate.
∎

Corollary B.115

(Weighted chain gate/slack absorption)
.

If a weighted chain norm satisfies the analogous domination condition, and the
same-chain representative is a weighted near-minimizer with error

\delta_{\mathrm{gs}}^{\mathrm{chain},\omega}
, the target estimate is

\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{gs}}^{\mathrm{chain}}(\mathbf{D};\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{*})\leq\eta_{\mathrm{gs}}\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{chain}}^{\sharp,\mathrm{gs},\omega}(\mathbf{D},\Gamma_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}^{\mathrm{chain}})+\eta_{\mathrm{gs}}\delta_{\mathrm{gs}}^{\mathrm{chain},\omega}.

Proof.

The proof is the same as the single-window proof, with

\|\cdot\|_{\mathrm{chain}}^{\sharp,\mathrm{gs}}
,

\|\cdot\|_{\mathrm{chain}}^{\sharp,\mathrm{gs},\omega}
, and

\delta_{\mathrm{gs}}^{\mathrm{chain},\omega}
replacing the corresponding single-window
objects.
∎

Remark B.116

(Weighted status)
.

Weighted absorption is a normalization of the accounting norm. It is not a
new PDE estimate.

B.40
Relation to Operator Gate/Tax Mismatch Models

Definition B.117

(Abstract operator mismatch model)
.

Earlier finite-window frameworks may use operator mismatch quantities such as

R_{\mathrm{gate}}^{\mathrm{det}}(D),\qquad R_{\mathrm{gate}}^{\mathrm{tax}}(D),\qquad R_{\mathrm{gate}}^{\mathrm{slack}}(D).

The present paper instead uses positive-part budget violation and slack
identity mismatch.

Assumption B.118

(Optional comparison with operator mismatch)
.

When needed, one may assume either

R_{\mathrm{gate}}^{\mathrm{op}}(D;\zeta)\leq C_{\mathrm{op}}\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{gs}}^{\mathrm{win}}(D;\zeta)+\Delta_{\mathrm{op}},

or conversely

\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{gs}}^{\mathrm{win}}(D;\zeta)\leq C_{\mathrm{op}}R_{\mathrm{gate}}^{\mathrm{op}}(D;\zeta)+\Delta_{\mathrm{op}}.

Remark B.119

(No automatic equivalence)
.

This paper does not claim equivalence between the positive-part
gate/slack model and the older operator model unless a comparison assumption
such as
Section

B.40
is separately supplied.

B.41
Finite-Chain Gate/Slack Corollary

Corollary B.120

(Finite-chain bookkeeping)
.

For a finite chain
D_{0},\ldots,D_{K}
, define

\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{gs}}^{[0,K]}:=\sum_{k=0}^{K}\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{gs}}^{\mathrm{win}}(D_{k};\zeta_{k})+\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{gs}}^{\mathrm{chain}}(D_{k},D_{k+1};\zeta_{k},\zeta_{k+1}).

Assume the finite-chain quotient distance is defined by summing the
corresponding window and chain gate/slack norms, and assume the selected
finite-chain representative satisfies the near-minimizer bound with errors

\delta_{\mathrm{gs},0},\ldots,\delta_{\mathrm{gs},K}
. Then

\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{gs}}^{[0,K]}\leq C_{\mathrm{gs}}^{[0,K]}\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{chain}}^{\sharp,\mathrm{gs},[0,K]}((D_{0},\ldots,D_{K}),\Gamma_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}^{\mathrm{chain},[0,K]})+C_{\mathrm{gs}}^{[0,K]}\sum_{k=0}^{K}\delta_{\mathrm{gs},k}.

Proof.

The finite-chain residual is a sum of nonnegative residual components that are
included in the corresponding finite-chain sharp norm. Therefore, evaluated
on the selected finite-chain representative,

\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{gs}}^{[0,K]}\leq C_{\mathrm{gs}}^{[0,K]}\|(D_{0},\ldots,D_{K})-(\zeta_{0},\ldots,\zeta_{K})\|_{\mathrm{chain}}^{\sharp,\mathrm{gs},[0,K]}.

The finite-chain near-minimizer bound gives the displayed estimate.
∎

Remark B.121

(Finite-chain status)
.

The constant may depend on
K
. This is not a scale-uniform theorem.

Componentwise Residual-Ledger Closure

B.42
Unified Package and Chain Geometry

Definition B.122

(Finite chain of sharp packages)
.

Fix a finite integer
K\geq 0
. A component chain is a tuple

\mathcal{D}=(D_{0},D_{1},\ldots,D_{K}),

where each
D_{k}
is a sharp localized package carrying the coordinates needed
by the pressure-source, localization, gate/slack, and reproduction branches.
For
0\leq k\leq K-1
, write

\mathbf{D}_{k}=(D_{k},D_{k+1})

for the adjacent two-window package pair.

Definition B.123

(Unified admissible component gauge)
.

The finite-chain admissible gauge class is a subset

\Gamma_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}^{\mathrm{comp}}\subset\prod_{k=0}^{K}\Gamma_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}^{(k)}.

An element of
\Gamma_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}^{\mathrm{comp}}
is written

\boldsymbol{\zeta}=(\zeta_{0},\ldots,\zeta_{K}).

We impose the conservative physical convention in every window:

(\zeta_{k})_{u}=0,\qquad 0\leq k\leq K.

Thus the physical velocity is not gauged away.

Remark B.124

(Finite-window scope)
.

All definitions are made on a fixed finite chain. Constants may depend on

K
, on the chosen windows, and on the component package constants. No
scale-uniformity is claimed.

B.43
Same-Chain Representative Convention

Assumption B.125

(Same-chain component representative)
.

For each finite chain
\mathcal{D}
, choose one representative

\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{*}(\mathcal{D})=(\zeta_{0,*},\ldots,\zeta_{K,*})\in\Gamma_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}^{\mathrm{comp}}.

The same representative is used simultaneously in all component channels:
pressure-source, localization, reproduction, single-window gate/slack, chain
gate/slack, and the unified component quotient distance.

Remark B.126

(Why this convention is necessary)
.

The component estimates do not combine if each residual channel is optimized
over its own gauge representative. The unified ledger is a same-gauge
statement, not a collection of independently minimized inequalities.

B.44
Unified Component Residual

Definition B.127

(Single-window component residual)
.

For a window
D_{k}
and representative
\zeta_{k}
, define

\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{win}}(D_{k};\zeta_{k}):=\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{src}}^{\mathrm{prs}}(D_{k};\zeta_{k})+\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{loc}}(D_{k};\zeta_{k})+\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{gs}}^{\mathrm{win}}(D_{k};\zeta_{k}).

Definition B.128

(Edge component residual)
.

For an adjacent pair
\mathbf{D}_{k}=(D_{k},D_{k+1})
, define

\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{edge}}(\mathbf{D}_{k};\zeta_{k},\zeta_{k+1}):=\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{rep}}(\mathbf{D}_{k};\zeta_{k},\zeta_{k+1})+\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{gs}}^{\mathrm{chain}}(\mathbf{D}_{k};\zeta_{k},\zeta_{k+1}).

Definition B.129

(Total component residual)
.

The finite-chain component residual is

\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{comp}}^{[0,K]}(\mathcal{D};\boldsymbol{\zeta}):=\sum_{k=0}^{K}\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{win}}(D_{k};\zeta_{k})+\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{edge}}(\mathbf{D}_{k};\zeta_{k},\zeta_{k+1}).

Equivalently, it is the sum of pressure-source, localization, single-window
gate/slack, reproduction, and chain gate/slack residuals over the finite chain.

B.45
Unified Sharp Component Norm

Definition B.130

(Window and edge component norms)
.

The single-window component norm

|D_{k}|_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{pkg}}^{\sharp,\mathrm{comp}}

is the finite-window sharp norm that dominates the pressure-source,
localization, and single-window gate/slack coordinates proved and used in
the corresponding component branches above.
The edge component norm

|\mathbf{D}_{k}|_{\mathrm{edge}}^{\sharp,\mathrm{comp}}

is the finite-window sharp norm that dominates the reproduction and chain
gate/slack coordinates proved and used in the corresponding component branches
above.

Definition B.131

(Finite-chain component norm)
.

For a finite chain
\mathcal{D}=(D_{0},\ldots,D_{K})
, define

|\mathcal{D}|_{\mathrm{comp}}^{\sharp,[0,K]}:=\sum_{k=0}^{K}|D_{k}|_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{pkg}}^{\sharp,\mathrm{comp}}+\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}|\mathbf{D}_{k}|_{\mathrm{edge}}^{\sharp,\mathrm{comp}}.

For a shifted chain
\mathcal{D}-\boldsymbol{\zeta}
, all terms are evaluated on

(D_{0}-\zeta_{0},\ldots,D_{K}-\zeta_{K})
.

Definition B.132

(Component quotient distance)
.

Define

\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{comp}}^{\sharp,[0,K]}(\mathcal{D},\Gamma_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}^{\mathrm{comp}}):=\inf_{\boldsymbol{\zeta}\in\Gamma_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}^{\mathrm{comp}}}|\mathcal{D}-\boldsymbol{\zeta}|_{\mathrm{comp}}^{\sharp,[0,K]}.

B.46
Unified Near-Minimizer Assumption

Assumption B.133

(Unified component near-minimizer)
.

The selected representative
\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{*}(\mathcal{D})\in\Gamma_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}^{\mathrm{comp}}
satisfies

|\mathcal{D}-\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{*}|_{\mathrm{comp}}^{\sharp,[0,K]}\leq\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{comp}}^{\sharp,[0,K]}(\mathcal{D},\Gamma_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}^{\mathrm{comp}})+\delta_{\mathrm{comp}}^{[0,K]}.

The error
\delta_{\mathrm{comp}}^{[0,K]}
is the single near-minimizer error used by
the unified ledger.

Remark B.134

(Relation to component errors)
.

One may either treat
\delta_{\mathrm{comp}}^{[0,K]}
as a primitive chain
near-minimizer error, or choose it to dominate the separate errors from the
component modules:

\delta_{{\mathrm{pkg}},k},\quad\delta_{{\mathrm{loc}},k},\quad\delta_{{\mathrm{rep}},k},\quad\delta_{{\mathrm{gs}},k},\quad\delta_{{\mathrm{gs}},k}^{\mathrm{chain}}.

The important point is that these errors correspond to the same selected chain
representative.

B.47
Component Channel Estimates in the Unified Geometry

The preceding four branches prove estimates in their own sharp norms. In the
unified ledger we use component norms that dominate all branch coordinates. The
following proposition records the representative-form estimates that will be
summed in the final closure theorem.

Proposition B.135

(Representative-form component channel estimates)
.

Let
\mathcal{D}=(D_{0},\ldots,D_{K})
be a finite component chain and let

\boldsymbol{\zeta}=(\zeta_{0},\ldots,\zeta_{K})\in\Gamma_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}^{\mathrm{comp}}
be one admissible same-chain
representative. Assume that, on each window and edge, the hypotheses of the
pressure-source, localization, reproduction, and gate/slack component theorems
proved in
Sections

B.9
,
B.18
,
B.29
,
B.36
and
B.38
hold with the finite-amplitude
bound
M_{U}
. Then there are finite constants

C_{{\mathrm{prs}},k}(M_{U}),\quad C_{{\mathrm{loc}},k}(M_{U}),\quad C_{{\mathrm{rep}},k}(M_{U}),\quad C_{{\mathrm{gs}},k},\quad C_{{\mathrm{gs}},k}^{\mathrm{chain}}

such that, for
0\leq k\leq K
,

\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{src}}^{\mathrm{prs}}(D_{k};\zeta_{k})\leq C_{{\mathrm{prs}},k}(M_{U})|D_{k}-\zeta_{k}|_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{pkg}}^{\sharp,\mathrm{comp}},

\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{loc}}(D_{k};\zeta_{k})\leq C_{{\mathrm{loc}},k}(M_{U})|D_{k}-\zeta_{k}|_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{pkg}}^{\sharp,\mathrm{comp}},

\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{gs}}^{\mathrm{win}}(D_{k};\zeta_{k})\leq C_{{\mathrm{gs}},k}|D_{k}-\zeta_{k}|_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{pkg}}^{\sharp,\mathrm{comp}},

and, for
0\leq k\leq K-1
,

\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{rep}}(\mathbf{D}_{k};\zeta_{k},\zeta_{k+1})\leq C_{{\mathrm{rep}},k}(M_{U})|\mathbf{D}_{k}-(\zeta_{k},\zeta_{k+1})|_{\mathrm{edge}}^{\sharp,\mathrm{comp}},

\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{gs}}^{\mathrm{chain}}(\mathbf{D}_{k};\zeta_{k},\zeta_{k+1})\leq C_{{\mathrm{gs}},k}^{\mathrm{chain}}|\mathbf{D}_{k}-(\zeta_{k},\zeta_{k+1})|_{\mathrm{edge}}^{\sharp,\mathrm{comp}}.

Proof.

The pressure-source estimate is the representative-norm estimate obtained in

Theorem

B.28
before applying the
branch near-minimizer condition; the component window norm dominates the sharp
pressure-source norm by definition. The localization estimate follows in the
same way from the representative-norm inequality in

Theorem

B.55
. The reproduction
estimate follows from the representative-norm inequality in

Theorem

B.86
, with the component
edge norm dominating the sharp chain reproduction norm. The single-window and
chain gate/slack estimates follow from

Theorems

B.109
and
B.113
,
because the gate/slack residuals are nonnegative coordinates included in the
corresponding sharp gate/slack norms. Enlarging constants by the finite
domination constants between branch norms and component norms gives the stated
bounds.
∎

B.48
Main Componentwise Closure Theorem

Theorem B.136

(Finite-chain componentwise closure)
.

Assume the branch hypotheses needed for the component channel estimates

Section

B.47
, the same-chain convention

Section

B.43
, and the unified near-minimizer
assumption
Section

B.46
. Let

\displaystyle C_{\mathrm{comp}}^{[0,K]}(M_{U})\geq\max\bigl\{

\displaystyle C_{{\mathrm{prs}},k}(M_{U}),C_{{\mathrm{loc}},k}(M_{U}),C_{{\mathrm{gs}},k}:0\leq k\leq K,

\displaystyle C_{{\mathrm{rep}},k}(M_{U}),C_{{\mathrm{gs}},k}^{\mathrm{chain}}:0\leq k\leq K-1\bigr\}.

Then

\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{comp}}^{[0,K]}(\mathcal{D};\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{*})\leq C_{\mathrm{comp}}^{[0,K]}(M_{U})\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{comp}}^{\sharp,[0,K]}(\mathcal{D},\Gamma_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}^{\mathrm{comp}})+C_{\mathrm{comp}}^{[0,K]}(M_{U})\delta_{\mathrm{comp}}^{[0,K]}.

The constant may depend on the finite-chain length
K
, the component
constants, the fixed cutoff and reproduction maps, and any finite-amplitude
bounds used by the component estimates. No scale-uniformity in
K
or in the
window scale is asserted.

Proof.

Fix the selected representative

\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{*}=(\zeta_{0,*},\ldots,\zeta_{K,*})
. By
Section

B.47
, for each window
0\leq k\leq K
,

\displaystyle\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{win}}(D_{k};\zeta_{k,*})

\displaystyle=\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{src}}^{\mathrm{prs}}(D_{k};\zeta_{k,*})+\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{loc}}(D_{k};\zeta_{k,*})+\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{gs}}^{\mathrm{win}}(D_{k};\zeta_{k,*})

\displaystyle\leq\bigl(C_{{\mathrm{prs}},k}(M_{U})+C_{{\mathrm{loc}},k}(M_{U})+C_{{\mathrm{gs}},k}\bigr)|D_{k}-\zeta_{k,*}|_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{pkg}}^{\sharp,\mathrm{comp}}.

Since the finite number of component constants can be enlarged, the right-hand
side is bounded by

C_{\mathrm{comp}}^{[0,K]}(M_{U})|D_{k}-\zeta_{k,*}|_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{pkg}}^{\sharp,\mathrm{comp}}.

Similarly, by
Section

B.47
, for each edge

0\leq k\leq K-1
,

\displaystyle\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{edge}}(\mathbf{D}_{k};\zeta_{k,*},\zeta_{k+1,*})

\displaystyle=\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{rep}}(\mathbf{D}_{k};\zeta_{k,*},\zeta_{k+1,*})+\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{gs}}^{\mathrm{chain}}(\mathbf{D}_{k};\zeta_{k,*},\zeta_{k+1,*})

\displaystyle\leq\bigl(C_{{\mathrm{rep}},k}(M_{U})+C_{{\mathrm{gs}},k}^{\mathrm{chain}}\bigr)|\mathbf{D}_{k}-(\zeta_{k,*},\zeta_{k+1,*})|_{\mathrm{edge}}^{\sharp,\mathrm{comp}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\mathrm{comp}}^{[0,K]}(M_{U})|\mathbf{D}_{k}-(\zeta_{k,*},\zeta_{k+1,*})|_{\mathrm{edge}}^{\sharp,\mathrm{comp}}.

Summing these window and edge estimates and using the definition of the
component norm gives

\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{comp}}^{[0,K]}(\mathcal{D};\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{*})\leq C_{\mathrm{comp}}^{[0,K]}(M_{U})|\mathcal{D}-\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{*}|_{\mathrm{comp}}^{\sharp,[0,K]}.

Finally apply
Section

B.46
.
∎

Remark B.137

(Status of the theorem)
.

This theorem is finite-chain bookkeeping using the component estimates
proved in the preceding branches and the unified near-minimizer assumption.
The detector comparison and full local-to-clean transfer use this estimate as
an input.

B.49
Weighted Componentwise Closure

Assumption B.138

(Weighted component geometry)
.

Let

|\mathcal{D}|_{\mathrm{comp}}^{\sharp,\omega,[0,K]}

be a weighted component norm satisfying

|\mathcal{D}-\boldsymbol{\zeta}|_{\mathrm{comp}}^{\sharp,\omega,[0,K]}\geq\frac{C_{\mathrm{comp}}^{[0,K]}(M_{U})}{\eta_{\mathrm{comp}}}|\mathcal{D}-\boldsymbol{\zeta}|_{\mathrm{comp}}^{\sharp,[0,K]}

for every admissible representative
\boldsymbol{\zeta}
. Define the weighted quotient
distance by

\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{comp}}^{\sharp,\omega,[0,K]}(\mathcal{D},\Gamma_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}^{\mathrm{comp}}):=\inf_{\boldsymbol{\zeta}\in\Gamma_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}^{\mathrm{comp}}}|\mathcal{D}-\boldsymbol{\zeta}|_{\mathrm{comp}}^{\sharp,\omega,[0,K]}.

Assume the selected representative satisfies the weighted near-minimizer
bound

|\mathcal{D}-\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{*}|_{\mathrm{comp}}^{\sharp,\omega,[0,K]}\leq\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{comp}}^{\sharp,\omega,[0,K]}(\mathcal{D},\Gamma_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}^{\mathrm{comp}})+\delta_{\mathrm{comp}}^{\omega,[0,K]}.

Corollary B.139

(Weighted componentwise closure)
.

Under the assumptions of
Theorem

B.136
and

Section

B.49
,

\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{comp}}^{[0,K]}(\mathcal{D};\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{*})\leq\eta_{\mathrm{comp}}\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{comp}}^{\sharp,\omega,[0,K]}(\mathcal{D},\Gamma_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}^{\mathrm{comp}})+\eta_{\mathrm{comp}}\delta_{\mathrm{comp}}^{\omega,[0,K]}.

Proof.

By
Theorem

B.136
,

\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{comp}}^{[0,K]}(\mathcal{D};\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{*})\leq C_{\mathrm{comp}}^{[0,K]}(M_{U})|\mathcal{D}-\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{*}|_{\mathrm{comp}}^{\sharp,[0,K]}.

The weighted domination assumption gives

C_{\mathrm{comp}}^{[0,K]}(M_{U})|\mathcal{D}-\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{*}|_{\mathrm{comp}}^{\sharp,[0,K]}\leq\eta_{\mathrm{comp}}|\mathcal{D}-\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{*}|_{\mathrm{comp}}^{\sharp,\omega,[0,K]}.

The weighted near-minimizer bound then yields the claimed estimate.
∎

B.50
Quadratic Geometry Variant

Definition B.140

(Quadratic component geometry)
.

Let

|\mathcal{D}|_{\mathrm{comp}}^{\sharp,\mathrm{quad},[0,K]}

denote the component geometry obtained by replacing bounded-amplitude
linearized component coordinates with quadratic variants. The gate/slack
coordinates remain nonnegative additive residual coordinates.

Definition B.141

(Quadratic component quotient distance)
.

For shifted chains define

\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{comp}}^{\sharp,\mathrm{quad},[0,K]}(\mathcal{D},\Gamma_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}^{\mathrm{comp}}):=\inf_{\boldsymbol{\zeta}\in\Gamma_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}^{\mathrm{comp}}}|\mathcal{D}-\boldsymbol{\zeta}|_{\mathrm{comp}}^{\sharp,\mathrm{quad},[0,K]}.

Assumption B.142

(Quadratic component estimates)
.

There is a finite constant
C_{\mathrm{comp}}^{\mathrm{quad},[0,K]}
such that the imported
quadratic pressure-source, localization, reproduction, and gate/slack component
estimates imply

\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{comp}}^{[0,K]}(\mathcal{D};\boldsymbol{\zeta})\leq C_{\mathrm{comp}}^{\mathrm{quad},[0,K]}|\mathcal{D}-\boldsymbol{\zeta}|_{\mathrm{comp}}^{\sharp,\mathrm{quad},[0,K]}

for every admissible component representative
\boldsymbol{\zeta}
.

Assumption B.143

(Quadratic near-minimizer)
.

The selected representative satisfies

|\mathcal{D}-\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{*}|_{\mathrm{comp}}^{\sharp,\mathrm{quad},[0,K]}\leq\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{comp}}^{\sharp,\mathrm{quad},[0,K]}(\mathcal{D},\Gamma_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}^{\mathrm{comp}})+\delta_{\mathrm{comp}}^{\mathrm{quad},[0,K]}.

Theorem B.144

(Quadratic componentwise closure)
.

Assume

Sections

B.50
and
B.50
. Then

\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{comp}}^{[0,K]}(\mathcal{D};\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{*})\leq C_{\mathrm{comp}}^{\mathrm{quad},[0,K]}\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{comp}}^{\sharp,\mathrm{quad},[0,K]}(\mathcal{D},\Gamma_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}^{\mathrm{comp}})+C_{\mathrm{comp}}^{\mathrm{quad},[0,K]}\delta_{\mathrm{comp}}^{\mathrm{quad},[0,K]}.

Proof.

Apply
Section

B.50
with

\boldsymbol{\zeta}=\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{*}
, then use
Section

B.50
.
∎

Remark B.145

(Cost of the quadratic geometry)
.

This variant avoids some finite-amplitude linearization by changing the
package geometry. It is not a theorem in the original linear baseline
geometry.

B.51
Normalized Quotient Amplitude Variant

Assumption B.146

(Normalized quotient amplitude)
.

On the normalized component quotient sphere

\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{comp}}^{\sharp,[0,K]}(\mathcal{D},\Gamma_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}^{\mathrm{comp}})=1,

assume the selected representative satisfies the amplitude bounds required by
the component theorems:

|u_{k}|_{L^{3}}+|U_{k}|_{L^{3}}+|\mathcal{R}u_{k}|_{L^{3}}+|\mathcal{R}U_{k}|_{L^{3}}\leq C_{\mathrm{amp}}

for all relevant windows and edges.

Corollary B.147

(Normalized-amplitude closure)
.

Assume
Section

B.51
and assume the imported component
estimates hold with amplitude parameter

M_{U}=C_{\mathrm{amp}}.

Then the finite-chain componentwise closure holds on the normalized quotient
sphere with
C_{\mathrm{comp}}^{[0,K]}(C_{\mathrm{amp}})
in place of

C_{\mathrm{comp}}^{[0,K]}(M_{U})
.

Proof.

The normalized amplitude hypothesis supplies the amplitude parameter required
by the component channel estimates. Substitute
M_{U}=C_{\mathrm{amp}}
in

Theorem

B.136
.
∎

Remark B.148

(No global finite-amplitude removal)
.

This variant does not remove finite amplitude globally. It records a possible
quotient-normalized route in which amplitude is controlled as part of the
chosen finite-window component geometry.

B.52
Relation to the Previous Assembly Theorem

Earlier conditional assembly theorems used a residual-budget input of the form

\mathsf{Err}_{\Lambda}(D)\leq\eta_{\Lambda}\operatorname{dist}^{\sharp}(D,\Gamma)+\Delta_{\Lambda}.

The present paper targets only the componentwise part of such a residual
budget:

\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{comp}}=\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{src}}^{\mathrm{prs}}+\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{loc}}+\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{rep}}+\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{gs}}.

In the detector-comparison framework, the residual budget is decomposed as

\mathsf{Err}_{\Lambda}=\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{comp}}+\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{rem}},

where the componentwise module supplies the
\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{comp}}
estimate and
the remaining term
\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{rem}}
is controlled by the detector
intertwining ledger.

Proposition B.149

(Partial residual-budget insertion)
.

Assume the hypotheses of
Theorem

B.136
. Suppose a
larger residual budget splits as

\mathsf{Err}_{\Lambda}(\mathcal{D};\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{*})=\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{comp}}^{[0,K]}(\mathcal{D};\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{*})+\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{rem}}(\mathcal{D};\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{*}),

and suppose the remaining residual satisfies

\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{rem}}(\mathcal{D};\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{*})\leq C_{\mathrm{rem}}\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{rem}}^{\sharp}(\mathcal{D},\Gamma_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}^{\mathrm{comp}})+\Delta_{\mathrm{rem}}.

Then

\displaystyle\mathsf{Err}_{\Lambda}(\mathcal{D};\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{*})

\displaystyle\leq C_{\mathrm{comp}}^{[0,K]}(M_{U})\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{comp}}^{\sharp,[0,K]}(\mathcal{D},\Gamma_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}^{\mathrm{comp}})+C_{\mathrm{comp}}^{[0,K]}(M_{U})\delta_{\mathrm{comp}}^{[0,K]}

\displaystyle+C_{\mathrm{rem}}\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{rem}}^{\sharp}(\mathcal{D},\Gamma_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}^{\mathrm{comp}})+\Delta_{\mathrm{rem}}.

Proof.

Insert the componentwise estimate from

Theorem

B.136
into the assumed residual splitting and
then use the assumed bound for
\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{rem}}
.
∎

Remark B.150

(Interface with detector comparison)
.

This relation is the component residual estimate used in the detector
comparison. The detector inequality itself is proved in

Appendix

C
.

B.53
Interface with the detector-comparison module

The componentwise ledger developed in this appendix is used by the detector
comparison and local-to-clean transfer module. Its output is the same-chain
residual estimate

\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{comp}}^{[0,K]}(\mathcal{D};\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{*})\leq C_{\mathrm{comp}}^{[0,K]}(M_{U})\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{comp}}^{\sharp,[0,K]}(\mathcal{D},\Gamma_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}^{\mathrm{comp}})+C_{\mathrm{comp}}^{[0,K]}(M_{U})\delta_{\mathrm{comp}}^{[0,K]},

together with its weighted, quadratic, and normalized-amplitude variants. The
separate hypotheses concerning detector-intertwining, clean-source compactness,
package-realizability, and reduced pressure/tax kernel-freeness are treated in

Appendix

C
. They are logically independent of the
componentwise estimates and are not used in the proof of

Theorem

B.136
.

B.54
Summary of the componentwise proof

The componentwise proof consists of the following finite-window steps.

Step 1.

The same-chain representative convention is fixed for all component
channel estimates.

Step 2.

The pressure-source residual, localization leakage, reproduction
drift, and gate/slack budget-violation estimates are proved in their own
sharp component geometries.

Step 3.

The finite-chain componentwise closure theorem is obtained by summing
the window and edge estimates in one unified component norm.

Step 4.

The weighted componentwise closure, quadratic-geometry variant, and
normalized quotient-amplitude variant are recorded as finite-window
consequences of the same ledger.

Step 5.

The partial residual-budget insertion proposition identifies how the
closed component residual enters a larger detector-comparison budget.

Appendix C
Detector Comparison and Structural Criteria: Detailed Proofs

C.1
Imported Componentwise Ledger Closure

Assumption C.1

(Componentwise residual-ledger closure)
.

For a finite-chain sharp package
\mathcal{D}
, a same-chain representative

\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{*}\in\Gamma_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}^{\mathrm{comp}}
, and a finite-window amplitude parameter
M_{U}
, the
componentwise residual-ledger theorem supplies

\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{comp}}^{[0,K]}(\mathcal{D};\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{*})\leq C_{\mathrm{comp}}^{[0,K]}(M_{U})\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{comp}}^{\sharp,[0,K]}(\mathcal{D},\Gamma_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}^{\mathrm{comp}})+C_{\mathrm{comp}}^{[0,K]}(M_{U})\delta_{\mathrm{comp}}^{[0,K]}.

The weighted form supplies, when the weighted component geometry is used,

\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{comp}}^{[0,K]}(\mathcal{D};\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{*})\leq\eta_{\mathrm{comp}}\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{comp}}^{\sharp,\omega,[0,K]}(\mathcal{D},\Gamma_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}^{\mathrm{comp}})+\eta_{\mathrm{comp}}\delta_{\mathrm{comp}}^{\omega,[0,K]}.

Remark C.2

(Imported status)
.

This paper does not reprove the componentwise ledger theorem. It treats it as
the already closed residual ledger available to the detector-comparison branch.

C.2
Localized and Clean Detectors

Definition C.3

(Localized detector)
.

A localized detector is a nonnegative finite-window functional of the form

M_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{loc}}(D):=|O_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{loc}}D|_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{loc}}}+\sum_{a\in\mathfrak{A}_{\mathrm{loc}}}\alpha_{a}\mathsf{Tax}_{a}^{\mathrm{loc}}(D),

where
O_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{loc}}
is the localized observable, the

\mathsf{Tax}_{a}^{\mathrm{loc}}
are nonnegative localized tax channels, and the weights

\alpha_{a}\geq 0
are fixed finite-window constants.

Remark C.4

(Shifted detector notation)
.

The notation
M_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathcal{D}-\boldsymbol{\zeta})
means that the localized
detector is evaluated on the shifted package. All estimates below use one
fixed shifted representative. This convention is part of the finite-window
quotient bookkeeping, not an independent minimization procedure.

Definition C.5

(Clean detector)
.

A clean detector is a nonnegative finite-window functional on clean packages

d
:

M_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{comp}}(d):=|O_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{comp}}d|_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{comp}}}+\sum_{b\in\mathfrak{A}_{\mathrm{comp}}^{\mathrm{det}}}\beta_{b}\mathsf{Tax}_{b}^{\mathrm{comp}}(d),

where
O_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{comp}}
is the clean observable, the

\mathsf{Tax}_{b}^{\mathrm{comp}}
are clean tax channels, and
\beta_{b}\geq 0
.

Remark C.6

(Accounting role)
.

The detectors are finite-window accounting objects. This paper does not
derive their coercivity or kernel-freeness from the Navier–Stokes equations.

C.3
Local-to-Clean Chart Convention

Definition C.7

(Finite-window local-to-clean chart)
.

The local-to-clean chart is a finite-window structural map

\Theta_{\Lambda}:\mathcal{D}\longrightarrow d=\Theta_{\Lambda}\mathcal{D}

from localized package chains to clean packages. For shifted representatives,
we write

\Theta_{\Lambda}(\mathcal{D}-\boldsymbol{\zeta})

for the clean package associated with the shifted finite-chain data.

Remark C.8

(No evolution claim)
.

The chart
\Theta_{\Lambda}
is not assumed to be an exact Navier–Stokes
evolution map. It is a finite-window comparison map whose compatibility with
the detectors is a structural input.

C.4
Detector Discrepancy

Definition C.9

(Positive detector discrepancy)
.

For a shifted package
\mathcal{D}-\boldsymbol{\zeta}
, define

\mathsf{Disc}_{\mathrm{det}}^{+}(\mathcal{D};\boldsymbol{\zeta}):=\left(M_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{comp}}(\Theta_{\Lambda}(\mathcal{D}-\boldsymbol{\zeta}))-M_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathcal{D}-\boldsymbol{\zeta})\right)_{+}.

This quantity measures the part of the clean detector not yet seen by the
localized detector.

Definition C.10

(Observable/tax mismatch functional)
.

Let
\mathcal{I}_{\Lambda}:\mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{loc}}\to\mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{comp}}
be a fixed
finite-window comparison operator between observable spaces. Define an
observable-level detector mismatch by

\displaystyle\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{det}}(\mathcal{D};\boldsymbol{\zeta})

\displaystyle:=\left|O_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{comp}}\Theta_{\Lambda}(\mathcal{D}-\boldsymbol{\zeta})-\mathcal{I}_{\Lambda}O_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathcal{D}-\boldsymbol{\zeta})\right|_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{comp}}}

\displaystyle+\sum_{b\in\mathfrak{A}_{\mathrm{comp}}^{\mathrm{det}}}\mathsf{Tax}_{b}^{\mathrm{mismatch}}(\mathcal{D};\boldsymbol{\zeta}).

The mismatch taxes are nonnegative channelwise errors comparing clean tax
channels with localized tax channels.

C.5
Detector-Intertwining Assumptions

Assumption C.11

(Clean detector controlled by local detector plus mismatch)
.

There are constants
C_{\mathrm{det}}<\infty
and
\Delta_{\mathrm{det}}^{0}\geq 0
such
that, for every admissible shifted package,

M_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{comp}}(\Theta_{\Lambda}(\mathcal{D}-\boldsymbol{\zeta}))\leq M_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathcal{D}-\boldsymbol{\zeta})+C_{\mathrm{det}}\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{det}}(\mathcal{D};\boldsymbol{\zeta})+\Delta_{\mathrm{det}}^{0}.

Assumption C.12

(Detector mismatch controlled by component residual)
.

There are constants
a_{\mathrm{det}}\geq 0
and

\Delta_{\mathrm{det}}^{\mathrm{rem}}\geq 0
such that

\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{det}}(\mathcal{D};\boldsymbol{\zeta})\leq a_{\mathrm{det}}\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{comp}}^{[0,K]}(\mathcal{D};\boldsymbol{\zeta})+\Delta_{\mathrm{det}}^{\mathrm{rem}}.

Convention C.13

(Same representative)
.

The same representative
\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{*}
is used in the localized detector, the
clean detector after charting, the detector mismatch, and the component residual
ledger. No independent minimization over different gauges is allowed in this
comparison theorem.

C.6
Main Detector Comparison

Theorem C.14

(Conditional finite-window detector comparison)
.

Assume
Sections

C.1
,
C.5
and
C.5

and the same-representative convention
Section

C.5
. Set

C_{\mathrm{dc}}:=C_{\mathrm{det}}a_{\mathrm{det}},\qquad\Delta_{\mathrm{dc}}:=C_{\mathrm{det}}\Delta_{\mathrm{det}}^{\mathrm{rem}}+\Delta_{\mathrm{det}}^{0}.

Then

M_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathcal{D}-\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{*})\geq M_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{comp}}(\Theta_{\Lambda}(\mathcal{D}-\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{*}))-C_{\mathrm{dc}}\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{comp}}^{[0,K]}(\mathcal{D};\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{*})-\Delta_{\mathrm{dc}}.

Consequently, using componentwise closure,

\displaystyle M_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathcal{D}-\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{*})

\displaystyle\geq M_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{comp}}(\Theta_{\Lambda}(\mathcal{D}-\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{*}))

\displaystyle-C_{\mathrm{dc}}C_{\mathrm{comp}}^{[0,K]}(M_{U})\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{comp}}^{\sharp,[0,K]}(\mathcal{D},\Gamma_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}^{\mathrm{comp}})

\displaystyle-C_{\mathrm{dc}}C_{\mathrm{comp}}^{[0,K]}(M_{U})\delta_{\mathrm{comp}}^{[0,K]}-\Delta_{\mathrm{dc}}.

Proof.

Apply
Section

C.5
with the representative
\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{*}
. It
gives

M_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{comp}}(\Theta_{\Lambda}(\mathcal{D}-\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{*}))\leq M_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathcal{D}-\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{*})+C_{\mathrm{det}}\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{det}}(\mathcal{D};\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{*})+\Delta_{\mathrm{det}}^{0}.

Rearranging,

M_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathcal{D}-\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{*})\geq M_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{comp}}(\Theta_{\Lambda}(\mathcal{D}-\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{*}))-C_{\mathrm{det}}\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{det}}(\mathcal{D};\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{*})-\Delta_{\mathrm{det}}^{0}.

The detector residual-control assumption gives

C_{\mathrm{det}}\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{det}}(\mathcal{D};\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{*})\leq C_{\mathrm{det}}a_{\mathrm{det}}\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{comp}}^{[0,K]}(\mathcal{D};\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{*})+C_{\mathrm{det}}\Delta_{\mathrm{det}}^{\mathrm{rem}}.

With the definitions of
C_{\mathrm{dc}}
and
\Delta_{\mathrm{dc}}
, this is
the first asserted estimate. Inserting the unweighted componentwise closure
from
Section

C.1
gives

C_{\mathrm{dc}}\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{comp}}^{[0,K]}(\mathcal{D};\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{*})\leq C_{\mathrm{dc}}C_{\mathrm{comp}}^{[0,K]}(M_{U})\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{comp}}^{\sharp,[0,K]}(\mathcal{D},\Gamma_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}^{\mathrm{comp}})+C_{\mathrm{dc}}C_{\mathrm{comp}}^{[0,K]}(M_{U})\delta_{\mathrm{comp}}^{[0,K]},

which proves the displayed consequence.
∎

Remark C.15

(Theorem status)
.

This theorem is proved as a finite-window conditional statement. The new
structural content required later is the verification of

Sections

C.5
and
C.5
in a specific
detector model.

C.7
Weighted Detector Comparison

Corollary C.16

(Conditional weighted detector comparison)
.

Assume
Sections

C.1
,
C.5
and
C.5

and the same-representative convention
Section

C.5
. If the
weighted componentwise closure in
Section

C.1
is used, then

\displaystyle M_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathcal{D}-\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{*})

\displaystyle\geq M_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{comp}}(\Theta_{\Lambda}(\mathcal{D}-\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{*}))

\displaystyle-C_{\mathrm{dc}}\eta_{\mathrm{comp}}\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{comp}}^{\sharp,\omega,[0,K]}(\mathcal{D},\Gamma_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}^{\mathrm{comp}})-C_{\mathrm{dc}}\eta_{\mathrm{comp}}\delta_{\mathrm{comp}}^{\omega,[0,K]}-\Delta_{\mathrm{dc}}.

Proof.

The first part of the proof of
Theorem

C.14
gives

M_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathcal{D}-\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{*})\geq M_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{comp}}(\Theta_{\Lambda}(\mathcal{D}-\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{*}))-C_{\mathrm{dc}}\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{comp}}^{[0,K]}(\mathcal{D};\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{*})-\Delta_{\mathrm{dc}}.

Now use the weighted closure estimate in
Section

C.1
,

\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{comp}}^{[0,K]}(\mathcal{D};\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{*})\leq\eta_{\mathrm{comp}}\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{comp}}^{\sharp,\omega,[0,K]}(\mathcal{D},\Gamma_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}^{\mathrm{comp}})+\eta_{\mathrm{comp}}\delta_{\mathrm{comp}}^{\omega,[0,K]}.

Substitution gives the claimed weighted comparison.
∎

C.8
Channelwise Intertwining Ledger

Definition C.17

(Detector channel assignment)
.

A detector channel assignment is a finite map that assigns each clean observable
or clean tax discrepancy to one of the already closed component channels:

\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{src}}^{\mathrm{prs}},\qquad\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{loc}},\qquad\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{rep}},\qquad\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{gs}},

or to the remaining detector error
\Delta_{\mathrm{det}}^{\mathrm{rem}}
.

Proposition C.18

(Channelwise sufficiency criterion)
.

Let
\mathfrak{Q}_{\mathrm{det}}
be a finite set of detector mismatch channels.
Suppose there are nonnegative channel errors
E_{q}(\mathcal{D};\boldsymbol{\zeta})
, constants

a_{q}\geq 0
, and remainders
r_{q}(\mathcal{D};\boldsymbol{\zeta})\geq 0
such that

\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{det}}(\mathcal{D};\boldsymbol{\zeta})\leq\sum_{q\in\mathfrak{Q}_{\mathrm{det}}}E_{q}(\mathcal{D};\boldsymbol{\zeta}),

E_{q}(\mathcal{D};\boldsymbol{\zeta})\leq a_{q}\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{comp}}^{[0,K]}(\mathcal{D};\boldsymbol{\zeta})+r_{q}(\mathcal{D};\boldsymbol{\zeta})\qquad(q\in\mathfrak{Q}_{\mathrm{det}}),

and

\sum_{q\in\mathfrak{Q}_{\mathrm{det}}}r_{q}(\mathcal{D};\boldsymbol{\zeta})\leq\Delta_{\mathrm{det}}^{\mathrm{rem}}.

Then
Section

C.5
holds with

a_{\mathrm{det}}:=\sum_{q\in\mathfrak{Q}_{\mathrm{det}}}a_{q}.

Proof.

Summing the channelwise bounds gives

\displaystyle\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{det}}(\mathcal{D};\boldsymbol{\zeta})

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{q\in\mathfrak{Q}_{\mathrm{det}}}\left(a_{q}\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{comp}}^{[0,K]}(\mathcal{D};\boldsymbol{\zeta})+r_{q}(\mathcal{D};\boldsymbol{\zeta})\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\sum_{q\in\mathfrak{Q}_{\mathrm{det}}}a_{q}\right)\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{comp}}^{[0,K]}(\mathcal{D};\boldsymbol{\zeta})+\sum_{q\in\mathfrak{Q}_{\mathrm{det}}}r_{q}(\mathcal{D};\boldsymbol{\zeta})

\displaystyle\leq a_{\mathrm{det}}\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{comp}}^{[0,K]}(\mathcal{D};\boldsymbol{\zeta})+\Delta_{\mathrm{det}}^{\mathrm{rem}}.

This is exactly
Section

C.5
.
∎

Remark C.19

(Unassigned detector channels)
.

The proposition identifies the only bookkeeping obstruction in this branch:
every detector discrepancy channel must either be assigned to the closed
component residual or placed explicitly in

\Delta_{\mathrm{det}}^{\mathrm{rem}}
. This paper does not verify those assignments
for a concrete detector model.

C.9
Conditional Finite-Window Local-to-Clean Transfer

This section assembles the detector-comparison theorem with imported clean-side
and chart-visibility inputs. The assembly remains same-representative: the
representative
\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{*}
used in the detector comparison is also the
representative used in the charted clean package and in the component residual
ledger.

Assumption C.20

(Clean finite-window anti-phantom gap)
.

There exists
\mu_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{comp}}>0
such that every clean package
d

in the finite-window clean class satisfies

M_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{comp}}(d)\geq\mu_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{comp}}\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{cl}}(d,\Gamma_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{cl}}),

where
\Gamma_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{cl}}
is the clean gauge or clean null class.

Assumption C.21

(Chart visibility in the older baseline geometry)
.

There are constants
\lambda_{G}>0
and
\delta_{G}\geq 0
such that the same
shifted representative satisfies

\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{cl}}(\Theta_{\Lambda}(\mathcal{D}-\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{*}),\Gamma_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{cl}})\geq\lambda_{G}\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},0}(\mathcal{D},\Gamma_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}^{\mathrm{int}})-\delta_{G}.

Assumption C.22

(Weighted component-to-baseline comparison)
.

There are a constant
C_{\mathrm{comp}/0}\geq 0
and a nonnegative finite-window
defect
B_{\mathrm{comp}}(\mathcal{D})
such that

\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{comp}}^{\sharp,\omega,[0,K]}(\mathcal{D},\Gamma_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}^{\mathrm{comp}})\leq C_{\mathrm{comp}/0}\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},0}(\mathcal{D},\Gamma_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}^{\mathrm{int}})+B_{\mathrm{comp}}(\mathcal{D}).

Theorem C.23

(Conditional finite-window local-to-clean transfer)
.

Assume the weighted detector comparison of
Section

C.7
, the
clean gap
Section

C.9
, the chart visibility estimate

Section

C.9
, and the component-to-baseline comparison

Section

C.9
. Define

c_{\Lambda}:=\mu_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{comp}}\lambda_{G}-C_{\mathrm{dc}}\eta_{\mathrm{comp}}C_{\mathrm{comp}/0}

and

\mathcal{E}_{\Lambda}(\mathcal{D}):=\mu_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{comp}}\delta_{G}+C_{\mathrm{dc}}\eta_{\mathrm{comp}}B_{\mathrm{comp}}(\mathcal{D})+C_{\mathrm{dc}}\eta_{\mathrm{comp}}\delta_{\mathrm{comp}}^{\omega,[0,K]}+\Delta_{\mathrm{dc}}.

Then

M_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathcal{D}-\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{*})\geq c_{\Lambda}\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},0}(\mathcal{D},\Gamma_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}^{\mathrm{int}})-\mathcal{E}_{\Lambda}(\mathcal{D}).

Proof.

By the weighted detector comparison,

\displaystyle M_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathcal{D}-\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{*})

\displaystyle\geq M_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{comp}}(\Theta_{\Lambda}(\mathcal{D}-\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{*}))

\displaystyle-C_{\mathrm{dc}}\eta_{\mathrm{comp}}\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{comp}}^{\sharp,\omega,[0,K]}(\mathcal{D},\Gamma_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}^{\mathrm{comp}})-C_{\mathrm{dc}}\eta_{\mathrm{comp}}\delta_{\mathrm{comp}}^{\omega,[0,K]}-\Delta_{\mathrm{dc}}.

The clean anti-phantom gap and chart visibility give

\displaystyle M_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{comp}}(\Theta_{\Lambda}(\mathcal{D}-\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{*}))

\displaystyle\geq\mu_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{comp}}\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{cl}}(\Theta_{\Lambda}(\mathcal{D}-\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{*}),\Gamma_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{cl}})

\displaystyle\geq\mu_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{comp}}\lambda_{G}\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},0}(\mathcal{D},\Gamma_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}^{\mathrm{int}})-\mu_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{comp}}\delta_{G}.

Since
C_{\mathrm{dc}}\eta_{\mathrm{comp}}\geq 0
, the component-to-baseline comparison
implies

\displaystyle-C_{\mathrm{dc}}\eta_{\mathrm{comp}}\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{comp}}^{\sharp,\omega,[0,K]}(\mathcal{D},\Gamma_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}^{\mathrm{comp}})

\displaystyle\geq-C_{\mathrm{dc}}\eta_{\mathrm{comp}}C_{\mathrm{comp}/0}\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},0}(\mathcal{D},\Gamma_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}^{\mathrm{int}})

\displaystyle-C_{\mathrm{dc}}\eta_{\mathrm{comp}}B_{\mathrm{comp}}(\mathcal{D}).

Substituting the last two displays into the weighted detector comparison and
collecting the coefficient of

\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},0}(\mathcal{D},\Gamma_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}^{\mathrm{int}})
gives exactly

M_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathcal{D}-\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{*})\geq\left(\mu_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{comp}}\lambda_{G}-C_{\mathrm{dc}}\eta_{\mathrm{comp}}C_{\mathrm{comp}/0}\right)\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},0}(\mathcal{D},\Gamma_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}^{\mathrm{int}})-\mathcal{E}_{\Lambda}(\mathcal{D}).

This is the claimed estimate.
∎

Corollary C.24

(Detection threshold)
.

Assume the hypotheses of
Theorem

C.23
and suppose

c_{\Lambda}>0
. If

\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},0}(\mathcal{D},\Gamma_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}^{\mathrm{int}})>\frac{\mathcal{E}_{\Lambda}(\mathcal{D})}{c_{\Lambda}},

then

M_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathcal{D}-\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{*})>0.

Proof.

The strict inequality gives

c_{\Lambda}\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},0}(\mathcal{D},\Gamma_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}^{\mathrm{int}})-\mathcal{E}_{\Lambda}(\mathcal{D})>0.

The conclusion follows from
Theorem

C.23
.
∎

Remark C.25

(Scope of the transfer theorem)
.

The theorem is a finite-window assembly result. It imports the clean gap,
chart visibility, component-to-baseline comparison, and detector comparison. It
does not prove compactness of Navier–Stokes-generated clean sources,
pressure/tax kernel-freeness, scale-uniformity, regularity, singularity
exclusion, or a Clay-problem conclusion.

C.10
Package-Realizability for Local Navier–Stokes Data

This section verifies that the finite-window package coordinates used above can
be constructed from local Navier–Stokes data. The result is a coordinate
realizability theorem only. It does not prove compactness, detector
kernel-freeness, baseline visibility, component-to-baseline comparison,
scale-uniformity, or regularity.

C.10.1
Local data and package spaces

Let
I=(-1,0)
and
Q_{1}=B_{1}\times I
. Fix cutoffs

\eta\in C_{c}^{\infty}(B_{1}),\qquad\eta\equiv 1\ \text{on }B_{3/4},

and

\chi\in C_{c}^{\infty}(B_{3/4}),\qquad\chi\equiv 1\ \text{on }B_{1/2}.

Set

A_{\chi}:=\operatorname{supp}\nabla\chi\cup\operatorname{supp}\Delta\chi.

The pressure-source and pressure observation spaces are

X_{\mathrm{src}}:=L^{3/2}(I;L^{3/2}(B_{1}))^{3\times 3},\qquad Y_{\mathrm{prs}}:=L^{3/2}(I;L^{3/2}(B_{1/2})),

and the pressure-natural harmonic observation space is

Y_{\mathrm{harm}}^{(3/2)}:=L^{3/2}(I;L^{3/2}(B_{3/4})).

Assumption C.26

(Pressure-admissible local Navier–Stokes data)
.

The local data
(u,p)
satisfy

u\in L^{3}(Q_{1})^{3},\qquad\nabla u\in L^{2}(I;L^{2}(B_{1}))^{3\times 3},\qquad p\in L^{3/2}(Q_{1}),

\nabla\cdot u=0,

and

\partial_{t}u-\Delta u+\nabla p+\nabla\cdot(u\otimes u)=0

in distributions on
Q_{1}
. The pressure is admissible in the sense that

-\Delta p=\partial_{i}\partial_{j}(u_{i}u_{j})

in distributions on the fixed local window, modulo time-dependent constants.
When the package includes the essential local energy coordinate

\operatorname*{ess\,sup}_{t\in I}\int_{B_{1}}|u(t)|^{2}\,dx
, we also assume the
usual local-energy part of suitable weak data,

u\in L^{\infty}(I;L^{2}(B_{1}))^{3}.

Definition C.27

(Canonical package coordinates)
.

For data satisfying
Section

C.10.1
, define

u_{D}:=u,\qquad p_{D}:=p,

F^{\mathrm{act}}_{D,ij}:=\eta u_{i}u_{j},\qquad p_{D}^{\mathrm{act}}:=R_{i}R_{j}(F^{\mathrm{act}}_{D,ij}),

where
F_{D}^{\mathrm{act}}
is extended by zero outside
B_{1}
before applying
the Riesz transforms. On
B_{3/4}
, define

p_{\mathrm{harm},D}:=p_{D}-p_{D}^{\mathrm{act}}.

The canonical source model is

U_{D}:=u,\qquad R_{D}:=0,\qquad E_{F,D}:=0,\qquad F^{\mathrm{mod}}_{D}:=F_{D}^{\mathrm{act}}.

Lemma C.28

(Pressure-source realizability)
.

Under
Section

C.10.1
,

F_{D}^{\mathrm{act}}\in X_{\mathrm{src}},\qquad p_{D}^{\mathrm{act}}\in Y_{\mathrm{prs}},\qquad p_{\mathrm{harm},D}\in Y_{\mathrm{harm}}^{(3/2)}.

Moreover,

-\Delta p_{\mathrm{harm},D}=0

in distributions on
B_{3/4}
for almost every time, equivalently on

B_{3/4}\times I
in the time-dependent distributional sense.

Proof.

Since
u\in L^{3}(Q_{1})^{3}
, Holder’s inequality gives

u_{i}u_{j}\in L^{3/2}(Q_{1})\qquad(1\leq i,j\leq 3).

Multiplication by the bounded cutoff
\eta
therefore gives

F_{D}^{\mathrm{act}}\in X_{\mathrm{src}}.

The fixed-window Calderon–Zygmund estimate for the zero extension yields

\|R_{i}R_{j}(F^{\mathrm{act}}_{D,ij})\|_{L^{3/2}(I;L^{3/2}(\mathbb{R}^{3}))}\leq C\|F_{D}^{\mathrm{act}}\|_{X_{\mathrm{src}}},

and hence
p_{D}^{\mathrm{act}}\in Y_{\mathrm{prs}}
. The same estimate also
gives
p_{D}^{\mathrm{act}}\in L^{3/2}(I;L^{3/2}(B_{3/4}))
. Since

p\in L^{3/2}(Q_{1})
, it follows that

p_{\mathrm{harm},D}=p-p_{D}^{\mathrm{act}}\in L^{3/2}(I;L^{3/2}(B_{3/4}))=Y_{\mathrm{harm}}^{(3/2)}.

With the sign convention
-\Delta R_{i}R_{j}F_{ij}=\partial_{i}\partial_{j}F_{ij}
,

-\Delta p_{D}^{\mathrm{act}}=\partial_{i}\partial_{j}(\eta u_{i}u_{j})

on
B_{1}
. Because
\eta\equiv 1
on
B_{3/4}
, this equals

\partial_{i}\partial_{j}(u_{i}u_{j})
on
B_{3/4}
. Pressure admissibility gives

-\Delta p=\partial_{i}\partial_{j}(u_{i}u_{j})

on the same region, modulo time-dependent constants. Their difference is
therefore harmonic in the spatial variable on
B_{3/4}
.
∎

C.10.2
Localization, energy, and gate coordinates

Definition C.29

(Localization leakage and finite-window observables)
.

Define the localization leakage coordinates

\mathsf{Leak}_{\nabla u}(D):=\|\nabla u\|_{L^{2}(I;L^{2}(A_{\chi}))},

\mathsf{Leak}_{u}(D):=\|u\|_{L^{3}(I;L^{3}(A_{\chi}))},

and

\mathsf{Leak}_{p}(D):=\|p\|_{L^{3/2}(I;L^{3/2}(A_{\chi}))}.

A canonical flux coordinate may be taken as any finite list of quantities
controlled by the displayed
L^{3}
,
L^{3/2}
, and
L^{2}
norms, for
example

\Pi_{D}:=\left(\int_{Q_{1}}\chi^{2}|u|^{3}\,dx\,dt,\int_{Q_{1}}\chi|p|^{3/2}\,dx\,dt\right).

When the local-energy part of
Section

C.10.1
is assumed, define

\Phi_{D}:=\left(\operatorname*{ess\,sup}_{t\in I}\int_{B_{1}}|u(x,t)|^{2}\,dx,\int_{Q_{1}}|\nabla u|^{2}\,dx\,dt\right).

The trace coordinate
T_{D}
is either omitted, set to
0
, or chosen as a
good-time datum
u(t_{D})\in L^{2}(B_{1})^{3}
at a time
t_{D}\in I
for which the
local energy is finite.

Definition C.30

(Gate and slack convention)
.

Let
\mathfrak{A}
be a finite set of gate/slack channels. For each

a\in\mathfrak{A}
, let
B_{a}(D)\geq 0
be a finite used-budget functional of
the finite coordinates above, and let
\tau_{a}(D)<\infty
be the selected
threshold. Define

s_{a}(D):=(\tau_{a}(D)-B_{a}(D))_{+},\qquad\mathsf{Gate}_{a}(D):=(B_{a}(D)-\tau_{a}(D))_{+},

and

\mathsf{Slack}_{a}(D):=|B_{a}(D)+s_{a}(D)-\tau_{a}(D)|.

Lemma C.31

(Finiteness of localization and gate coordinates)
.

Under
Section

C.10.1
, the leakage coordinates

\mathsf{Leak}_{\nabla u}(D),\qquad\mathsf{Leak}_{u}(D),\qquad\mathsf{Leak}_{p}(D)

are finite. The canonical
\Pi_{D}
is finite. If the local-energy part of

Section

C.10.1
is included, then
\Phi_{D}
and any good-time
trace
T_{D}
are finite. For every finite gate/slack rule in

Section

C.10.2
,
s_{a}(D)
,
\mathsf{Gate}_{a}(D)
, and

\mathsf{Slack}_{a}(D)
are finite.

Proof.

The shell
A_{\chi}
is contained in
B_{1}
. The assumptions

\nabla u\in L^{2}(I;L^{2}(B_{1})),\qquad u\in L^{3}(Q_{1}),\qquad p\in L^{3/2}(Q_{1})

therefore immediately imply finiteness of the three leakage norms. The
canonical
\Pi_{D}
is finite by the same
L^{3}
and
L^{3/2}
bounds and the
boundedness of
\chi
. If
u\in L^{\infty}(I;L^{2}(B_{1}))^{3}
, then the first
coordinate of
\Phi_{D}
is finite, and the second is finite by the assumed

L^{2}
bound on
\nabla u
. For almost every time
t
,
u(t)\in L^{2}(B_{1})^{3}
, so a selected good-time trace is finite. Finally, the gate/slack
coordinates are obtained from finite nonnegative numbers by addition,
subtraction, absolute value, and positive part.
∎

C.10.3
Projected source convention

Proposition C.32

(Projected clean source coordinates)
.

Let
P_{N}^{u}:L^{3}(Q_{1})^{3}\to L^{3}(Q_{1})^{3}
be a bounded finite-window velocity
projection or coordinate selector. Set

U_{D}:=P_{N}^{u}u,\qquad R_{D,ij}:=u_{i}u_{j}-U_{D,i}U_{D,j},\qquad E_{F,D}:=0,

and

F^{\mathrm{mod}}_{D,ij}:=\eta(U_{D,i}U_{D,j}+R_{D,ij}).

Then

U_{D}\in L^{3}(Q_{1})^{3},\qquad R_{D}\in X_{\mathrm{src}},\qquad F_{D}^{\mathrm{mod}}=F_{D}^{\mathrm{act}}.

Proof.

Boundedness of
P_{N}^{u}
gives
U_{D}\in L^{3}(Q_{1})^{3}
. Holder’s inequality gives

u_{i}u_{j}\in L^{3/2}(Q_{1}),\qquad U_{D,i}U_{D,j}\in L^{3/2}(Q_{1}),

so
R_{D}\in X_{\mathrm{src}}
. The definition of
R_{D}
gives

U_{D,i}U_{D,j}+R_{D,ij}=u_{i}u_{j},

and hence

F_{D,ij}^{\mathrm{mod}}=\eta u_{i}u_{j}=F_{D,ij}^{\mathrm{act}}.

∎

C.10.4
Finite-chain reproduction coordinates

Proposition C.33

(Well-defined reproduction drifts)
.

Let
(u_{k},p_{k})_{k=0}^{K}
be a finite chain of data satisfying

Section

C.10.1
, and let
D_{k}=D_{\Lambda}(u_{k},p_{k})
be the
corresponding packages. Suppose the finite-window reproduction maps

\mathcal{R}^{u},\quad\mathcal{R}^{\mathrm{src}},\quad\mathcal{R}^{\mathrm{prs}},\quad\mathcal{R}^{\mathrm{harm}},\quad\mathcal{R}^{U},\quad\mathcal{R}^{R},\quad\mathcal{R}^{E}

map each coordinate space into the corresponding next-window coordinate space.
Then the reproduction drifts

u_{k+1}-\mathcal{R}^{u}u_{k},

F^{\mathrm{act}}_{k+1}-\mathcal{R}^{\mathrm{src}}F^{\mathrm{act}}_{k},

p^{\mathrm{act}}_{k+1}-\mathcal{R}^{\mathrm{prs}}p^{\mathrm{act}}_{k},

and

p_{\mathrm{harm},k+1}-\mathcal{R}^{\mathrm{harm}}p_{\mathrm{harm},k}

are well-defined finite-window objects in their assigned coordinate spaces.

Proof.

Each
D_{k}
has its source, active pressure, and harmonic pressure coordinates
in the spaces identified in
Section

C.10.1
. The
assumed mapping property of the reproduction operators places

\mathcal{R}^{u}u_{k}
,
\mathcal{R}^{\mathrm{src}}F^{\mathrm{act}}_{k}
,

\mathcal{R}^{\mathrm{prs}}p^{\mathrm{act}}_{k}
, and

\mathcal{R}^{\mathrm{harm}}p_{\mathrm{harm},k}
in the same spaces as the
corresponding
(k+1)
-coordinates. Since these spaces are vector spaces, the
displayed differences are well-defined. No scale-uniform reproduction estimate
is asserted.
∎

Theorem C.34

(Finite-window package-realizability)
.

Let
(u,p)
satisfy
Section

C.10.1
, and fix the canonical
coordinate convention of
Section

C.10.1
together with any
finite gate/slack and observable rules of

Sections

C.10.2
and
C.10.2
. Then

D_{\Lambda}(u,p)

is a well-defined sharp localized finite-window package in the coordinate
sense. In particular,

F_{D}^{\mathrm{act}}\in X_{\mathrm{src}},\qquad p_{D}^{\mathrm{act}}\in Y_{\mathrm{prs}},\qquad p_{\mathrm{harm},D}\in Y_{\mathrm{harm}}^{(3/2)},

\mathsf{Leak}_{\nabla u}(D),\quad\mathsf{Leak}_{u}(D),\quad\mathsf{Leak}_{p}(D)<\infty,

and all finite-window gate/slack and ledger coordinates are well-defined. For
finite chains, the reproduction drift coordinates are well-defined under the
mapping assumption of
Section

C.10.4
.

If, in addition, the selected model coordinates satisfy the bounded-amplitude
or quadratic-geometry hypotheses required earlier, then
D_{\Lambda}(u,p)

belongs to the corresponding package class.

Proof.

The pressure-source, active-pressure, and harmonic-pressure assertions are

Section

C.10.1
. The localization leakage,
observable, trace, and gate/slack assertions are

Section

C.10.2
. The canonical source model is
well-defined by
Section

C.10.1
; the projected variant is
available under the additional projection hypothesis in

Section

C.10.3
. The finite-chain reproduction
coordinates are well-defined by
Section

C.10.4
. The
last statement is conditional by definition: once the selected coordinates
satisfy the additional bounded-amplitude or quadratic-geometry hypotheses used
in the earlier package-level theorems, the realized package lies in the
corresponding admissible package class.
∎

Remark C.35

(Status of package-realizability)
.

This theorem connects local Navier–Stokes data to the coordinate layer of the
finite-window package framework. It does not show that the resulting family is
compact, that the pressure/tax detector is kernel-free, that baseline visibility
or component-to-baseline comparison holds automatically, or that any estimate is
scale-uniform.

C.11
Clean-Source Compactness and Effective Projection

The transfer estimates used in the finite-window framework may contain a clean
pressure projection tail. This section records finite-window hypotheses under
which that tail converges uniformly on a selected Navier–Stokes-generated
package class. The point is deliberately modest: compactness or effective
projection is a structural input. It is not a consequence of boundedness alone,
and it is not proved here for arbitrary suitable weak solutions.

C.11.1
Selected clean sources and pressure images

Fix an NS-generated package class

\mathcal{A}_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{NS}}:=\{D_{\Lambda}(u,p):(u,p)\in\mathcal{S}_{\Lambda}\},

where
\mathcal{S}_{\Lambda}
is a selected family of local data satisfying the
realizability assumptions of
Section

C.10.1
. Fix also a
same-representative selection

D\longmapsto\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{0}(D).

The clean source associated with
D-\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{0}(D)
is denoted

F^{\mathrm{cl}}_{D-\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{0}(D)}\in X_{\mathrm{src}}.

Depending on the source convention, this may be the canonical active source

F^{\mathrm{cl}}_{D-\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{0}(D)}=F^{\mathrm{act}}_{D-\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{0}(D)}=\eta u_{D-\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{0}(D)}\otimes u_{D-\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{0}(D)},

the model source

F^{\mathrm{cl}}_{D-\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{0}(D)}=\eta\bigl(U_{D-\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{0}(D)}\otimes U_{D-\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{0}(D)}+R_{D-\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{0}(D)}\bigr),

or the residual clean source

F^{\mathrm{cl}}_{D-\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{0}(D)}=\eta\bigl(U_{D-\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{0}(D)}\otimes U_{D-\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{0}(D)}+R_{D-\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{0}(D)}\bigr)+E_{F,D-\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{0}(D)}.

Define the selected source family

\mathcal{F}_{\Lambda,0}:=\{F^{\mathrm{cl}}_{D-\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{0}(D)}:D\in\mathcal{A}_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{NS}}\}\subset X_{\mathrm{src}}.

Let

\mathcal{R}_{\mathrm{prs}}:X_{\mathrm{src}}\to Y_{\mathrm{prs}},\qquad\mathcal{R}_{\mathrm{prs}}(F):=R_{i}R_{j}(F_{ij})|_{B_{1/2}},

where
F
is extended by zero before applying the Riesz transforms. The
fixed-window Calderon–Zygmund estimate gives

\|\mathcal{R}_{\mathrm{prs}}(F)\|_{Y_{\mathrm{prs}}}\leq C_{\mathrm{CZ}}\|F\|_{X_{\mathrm{src}}}.

The selected clean pressure image is

\mathcal{G}_{\Lambda,0}:=\mathcal{R}_{\mathrm{prs}}(\mathcal{F}_{\Lambda,0})\subset Y_{\mathrm{prs}}.

Let
P_{N}^{\mathrm{cl}}:Y_{\mathrm{prs}}\to Y_{\mathrm{prs}}
be finite-rank clean
pressure projections satisfying

P_{N}^{\mathrm{cl}}g\to g\qquad\text{for every }g\in Y_{\mathrm{prs}},

and

C_{P}:=\sup_{N}\|P_{N}^{\mathrm{cl}}\|_{Y_{\mathrm{prs}}\to Y_{\mathrm{prs}}}<\infty.

Define the uniform clean projection tail by

\Delta_{\mathrm{proj},N}^{\mathrm{unif}}(\mathcal{A}_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{NS}}):=\sup_{F\in\mathcal{F}_{\Lambda,0}}\|(I-P_{N}^{\mathrm{cl}})\mathcal{R}_{\mathrm{prs}}(F)\|_{Y_{\mathrm{prs}}}.

Remark C.36

(Boundedness alone is not compactness)
.

If
Y
is infinite-dimensional and
P_{N}:Y\to Y
are finite-rank operators
with
P_{N}g\to g
strongly for each fixed
g
, one cannot infer

\sup_{\|g\|_{Y}\leq 1}\|(I-P_{N})g\|_{Y}\to 0.

Otherwise
P_{N}\to I
in operator norm, so the identity would be a norm limit
of compact operators and hence compact, impossible on an infinite-dimensional
Banach space. Thus bounded finite amplitude is not a substitute for compactness
or an effective projection estimate.

C.11.2
Compactness implies uniform projection tails

Theorem C.37

(Compact pressure image gives uniform projection-tail convergence)
.

Assume

\mathcal{G}_{\Lambda,0}\Subset Y_{\mathrm{prs}}.

Then

\Delta_{\mathrm{proj},N}^{\mathrm{unif}}(\mathcal{A}_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{NS}})\to 0.

Proof.

Let
K=\overline{\mathcal{G}_{\Lambda,0}}
, compact in
Y_{\mathrm{prs}}
.
Fix
\varepsilon>0
. Choose points
g_{1},\ldots,g_{J}\in K
such that

K\subset\bigcup_{j=1}^{J}B_{Y_{\mathrm{prs}}}\left(g_{j},\frac{\varepsilon}{3(1+C_{P})}\right).

For each fixed
j
, strong convergence gives

\|(I-P_{N}^{\mathrm{cl}})g_{j}\|_{Y_{\mathrm{prs}}}\to 0.

After increasing
N
, this quantity is at most
\varepsilon/3
for all

1\leq j\leq J
. If
g\in K
, choose
j
with

\|g-g_{j}\|_{Y_{\mathrm{prs}}}<\varepsilon/[3(1+C_{P})]
. Then

\displaystyle\|(I-P_{N}^{\mathrm{cl}})g\|_{Y_{\mathrm{prs}}}

\displaystyle\leq\|(I-P_{N}^{\mathrm{cl}})(g-g_{j})\|_{Y_{\mathrm{prs}}}+\|(I-P_{N}^{\mathrm{cl}})g_{j}\|_{Y_{\mathrm{prs}}}

\displaystyle\leq(1+C_{P})\|g-g_{j}\|_{Y_{\mathrm{prs}}}+\frac{\varepsilon}{3}<\varepsilon.

Taking the supremum over
g\in\mathcal{G}_{\Lambda,0}\subset K
proves the
claim.
∎

Theorem C.38

(Source-level compactness criterion)
.

If

\mathcal{F}_{\Lambda,0}\Subset X_{\mathrm{src}},

then

\mathcal{G}_{\Lambda,0}\Subset Y_{\mathrm{prs}},

and hence

\Delta_{\mathrm{proj},N}^{\mathrm{unif}}(\mathcal{A}_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{NS}})\to 0.

Proof.

The map
\mathcal{R}_{\mathrm{prs}}:X_{\mathrm{src}}\to Y_{\mathrm{prs}}
is bounded
linear by the fixed Calderon–Zygmund estimate, hence continuous. The continuous
image of a compact set is compact, so

\mathcal{R}_{\mathrm{prs}}(\overline{\mathcal{F}_{\Lambda,0}})
is compact in

Y_{\mathrm{prs}}
and contains
\mathcal{G}_{\Lambda,0}
. The uniform
projection-tail convergence then follows from

Theorem

C.37
.
∎

C.11.3
Concrete compactness criteria

Theorem C.39

(Finite-dimensional source model)
.

Assume there is a finite-dimensional subspace
S_{J}\subset X_{\mathrm{src}}
such that

F^{\mathrm{cl}}_{D-\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{0}(D)}\in S_{J}\qquad(D\in\mathcal{A}_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{NS}}),

and the corresponding coefficient set is bounded in
S_{J}
. Then

\mathcal{F}_{\Lambda,0}\Subset X_{\mathrm{src}},

and consequently

\Delta_{\mathrm{proj},N}^{\mathrm{unif}}(\mathcal{A}_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{NS}})\to 0.

Proof.

On a finite-dimensional normed space, bounded sets are precompact. Since all
norms on
S_{J}
are equivalent and
S_{J}
is continuously embedded in

X_{\mathrm{src}}
, the bounded coefficient set has compact closure in
X_{\mathrm{src}}
.
Thus
\mathcal{F}_{\Lambda,0}\Subset X_{\mathrm{src}}
, and the conclusion follows from

Theorem

C.38
.
∎

Remark C.40
.

This theorem applies to reduced finite-window models, Galerkin source families,
numerical package classes, or explicitly truncated clean-source models. It is
not a statement that arbitrary Navier–Stokes sources are finite-dimensional.

Theorem C.41

(Strong clean-coordinate compactness)
.

Assume the clean source has the form

F^{\mathrm{cl}}_{D-\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{0}(D)}=\eta\bigl(U_{D-\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{0}(D)}\otimes U_{D-\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{0}(D)}+R_{D-\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{0}(D)}\bigr)+E_{F,D-\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{0}(D)}.

Define

\mathcal{U}_{\Lambda,0}:=\{U_{D-\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{0}(D)}:D\in\mathcal{A}_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{NS}}\}\subset L^{3}(Q_{1})^{3},

\mathcal{C}_{\Lambda,0}^{R}:=\{R_{D-\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{0}(D)}:D\in\mathcal{A}_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{NS}}\}\subset L^{3/2}(Q_{1})^{3\times 3},

and

\mathcal{E}_{\Lambda,0}^{F}:=\{E_{F,D-\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{0}(D)}:D\in\mathcal{A}_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{NS}}\}\subset X_{\mathrm{src}}.

If

\mathcal{U}_{\Lambda,0}\Subset L^{3}(Q_{1})^{3},\qquad\mathcal{C}_{\Lambda,0}^{R}\Subset L^{3/2}(Q_{1})^{3\times 3},\qquad\mathcal{E}_{\Lambda,0}^{F}\Subset X_{\mathrm{src}},

then

\mathcal{F}_{\Lambda,0}\Subset X_{\mathrm{src}}.

Proof.

It is enough to prove continuity of

(U,R,E)\longmapsto\eta(U\otimes U+R)+E

from
L^{3}(Q_{1})^{3}\times L^{3/2}(Q_{1})^{3\times 3}\times X_{\mathrm{src}}
into

X_{\mathrm{src}}
. Suppose
U_{n}\to U
in
L^{3}
,
R_{n}\to R
in
L^{3/2}
,
and
E_{n}\to E
in
X_{\mathrm{src}}
. Then

\displaystyle\|U_{n}\otimes U_{n}-U\otimes U\|_{L^{3/2}}

\displaystyle\leq\|(U_{n}-U)\otimes U_{n}\|_{L^{3/2}}+\|U\otimes(U_{n}-U)\|_{L^{3/2}}

\displaystyle\leq\|U_{n}-U\|_{L^{3}}\bigl(\|U_{n}\|_{L^{3}}+\|U\|_{L^{3}}\bigr).

Since
U_{n}\to U
, the
L^{3}
-norms of
U_{n}
are bounded, and the right-hand
side tends to zero. Multiplication by the smooth cutoff
\eta
is bounded on

L^{3/2}
. Hence the clean sources converge in
X_{\mathrm{src}}
. The product of
three compact sets is compact, and the continuous image of that product is
compact.
∎

Corollary C.42
.

Under the hypotheses of
Theorem

C.41
,

\Delta_{\mathrm{proj},N}^{\mathrm{unif}}(\mathcal{A}_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{NS}})\to 0.

Proof.

Combine
Theorem

C.41
with

Theorem

C.38
.
∎

Theorem C.43

(Sobolev clean-source compactness)
.

Assume that for some
s>0
,

\sup_{F\in\mathcal{F}_{\Lambda,0}}\|F\|_{W^{s,3/2}(Q_{1})^{3\times 3}}<\infty.

Then

\mathcal{F}_{\Lambda,0}\Subset X_{\mathrm{src}},

and consequently

\Delta_{\mathrm{proj},N}^{\mathrm{unif}}(\mathcal{A}_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{NS}})\to 0.

Proof.

Since
Q_{1}
is bounded, the Rellich–Kondrachov theorem gives the compact
embedding

W^{s,3/2}(Q_{1})\Subset L^{3/2}(Q_{1})

for
s>0
. Applying this componentwise to tensor-valued sources proves

\mathcal{F}_{\Lambda,0}\Subset X_{\mathrm{src}}
. The projection-tail conclusion is

Theorem

C.38
.
∎

Remark C.44
.

This is only a sufficient criterion. No Sobolev or fractional differentiability
bound for arbitrary suitable weak solutions is proved here.

Theorem C.45

(Kolmogorov–Riesz source compactness)
.

Assume
\mathcal{F}_{\Lambda,0}\subset L^{3/2}(Q_{1})^{3\times 3}
is uniformly
bounded and, after zero extension, satisfies uniform translation continuity:

\lim_{|h|\to 0}\sup_{F\in\mathcal{F}_{\Lambda,0}}\|F(\cdot+h)-F(\cdot)\|_{L^{3/2}(\mathbb{R}^{4})^{3\times 3}}=0.

Then

\mathcal{F}_{\Lambda,0}\Subset X_{\mathrm{src}}.

Consequently,

\mathcal{G}_{\Lambda,0}\Subset Y_{\mathrm{prs}},\qquad\Delta_{\mathrm{proj},N}^{\mathrm{unif}}(\mathcal{A}_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{NS}})\to 0.

Proof.

After zero extension, the family is uniformly bounded in

L^{3/2}(\mathbb{R}^{4})^{3\times 3}
. Its supports lie in the fixed bounded set

\overline{Q_{1}}
, so tightness is automatic. The assumed uniform translation
continuity is the remaining Kolmogorov–Riesz hypothesis. Therefore the family
is precompact in
L^{3/2}(\mathbb{R}^{4})^{3\times 3}
, and restriction to
Q_{1}
gives
precompactness in
X_{\mathrm{src}}
. The pressure-image and projection-tail
conclusions follow from
Theorem

C.38
.
∎

Theorem C.46

(Conditional NS-generated compactness under source translation compactness)
.

Let
\mathcal{A}_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{NS}}
be generated by a selected local data family

\mathcal{S}_{\Lambda}
. Assume the selected clean source family

\mathcal{F}_{\Lambda,0}
is compatible with the same representative selection,
is uniformly bounded in
L^{3/2}(Q_{1})^{3\times 3}
, and satisfies the uniform
translation continuity hypothesis of

Theorem

C.45
. Then

\mathcal{F}_{\Lambda,0}\Subset X_{\mathrm{src}},\qquad\mathcal{G}_{\Lambda,0}\Subset Y_{\mathrm{prs}},

and

\Delta_{\mathrm{proj},N}^{\mathrm{unif}}(\mathcal{A}_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{NS}})\to 0.

Proof.

This is
Theorem

C.45
applied to the
NS-generated selected clean source family.
∎

Remark C.47

(Status of the NS-facing criterion)
.

Theorem

C.46
identifies a concrete structural
input: uniform
L^{3/2}
translation compactness of the selected clean sources.
The theorem does not prove that arbitrary suitable weak solutions satisfy this
input.

C.11.4
Effective projection without compactness

Proposition C.48

(Effective projection replacement)
.

Assume there is a sequence
\varepsilon_{N}\downarrow 0
such that

\Delta_{\mathrm{proj},N}^{\mathrm{unif}}(\mathcal{A}_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{NS}})\leq\varepsilon_{N}.

Then any finite-window transfer estimate whose error contains the projection
term

\alpha_{\mathrm{proj}}\Delta_{\mathrm{proj},N}^{\mathrm{unif}}(\mathcal{A}_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{NS}})

remains valid with this contribution replaced by

\alpha_{\mathrm{proj}}\varepsilon_{N}
. In particular, the projection
contribution tends to zero as
N\to\infty
.

Proof.

The assumed estimate gives

\alpha_{\mathrm{proj}}\Delta_{\mathrm{proj},N}^{\mathrm{unif}}(\mathcal{A}_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{NS}})\leq\alpha_{\mathrm{proj}}\varepsilon_{N}.

This is a direct substitution into the transfer error budget.
∎

Corollary C.49

(Insertion into finite-window transfer errors)
.

Suppose a finite-window transfer estimate has an error budget of the form

\mathcal{E}_{\Lambda}^{(N)}(D)=\mathcal{E}_{\Lambda}^{0}(D)+\alpha_{\mathrm{proj}}\Delta_{\mathrm{proj},N}^{\mathrm{unif}}(\mathcal{A}_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{NS}}),

where
\mathcal{E}_{\Lambda}^{0}
is independent of
N
. If any one of the
compactness criteria in this section applies, or if the effective projection
bound of
Section

C.11.4
holds, then for every

\varepsilon>0
there is
N_{\varepsilon}
such that for

N\geq N_{\varepsilon}
,

\mathcal{E}_{\Lambda}^{(N)}(D)\leq\mathcal{E}_{\Lambda}^{0}(D)+\varepsilon.

Proof.

Under the compactness criteria, the projection tail tends to zero by the
preceding theorems. Under the effective projection assumption, it tends to zero
by
Section

C.11.4
. Choose
N_{\varepsilon}
so
that

\alpha_{\mathrm{proj}}\Delta_{\mathrm{proj},N}^{\mathrm{unif}}(\mathcal{A}_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{NS}})\leq\varepsilon

for all
N\geq N_{\varepsilon}
, and substitute into the displayed error budget.
∎

Remark C.50

(Status)
.

This section gives compactness and effective-projection criteria for selected
clean sources. It does not prove that all suitable weak solutions generate
compact clean-source families. It also does not prove pressure/tax
kernel-freeness, baseline visibility, component-to-baseline comparison from
Navier–Stokes, scale-uniformity, regularity, or singularity exclusion.

C.12
Pressure/Tax Kernel-Freeness in Reduced Packages

The previous sections close residual and projection-tail inputs under explicit
finite-window hypotheses. The present section addresses a different structural
question: the common zero set of the active observation and tax channels. The
goal is not residual absorption. It is zero-set rigidity: a package with no
observable defect and no active tax should be an admissible gauge direction.
The results below are fixed finite-window and reduced-model criteria only.

C.12.1
Normalized pressure/tax detector

Definition C.51

(Normalized pressure/tax detector)
.

Let
\mathcal{A}_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{tax}}
be a finite-window tax-admissible
package class. A normalized pressure/tax detector is a nonnegative functional

\displaystyle M_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{tax}}(D)

\displaystyle:=\|O^{0}_{\Lambda}D\|_{\mathcal{O}}+\beta_{\mathrm{prs}}\mathsf{Tax}_{\mathrm{prs}}(D)+\beta_{\mathrm{loc}}\mathsf{Tax}_{\mathrm{loc}}(D)

\displaystyle+\beta_{\mathrm{rep}}\mathsf{Tax}_{\mathrm{rep}}(D)+\beta_{\mathrm{gs}}\mathsf{Tax}_{\mathrm{gs}}(D)+\beta_{\mathrm{det}}\mathsf{Tax}_{\mathrm{det}}(D),

where all tax channels are nonnegative and all active weights satisfy

\beta_{\mathrm{prs}},\beta_{\mathrm{loc}},\beta_{\mathrm{rep}},\beta_{\mathrm{gs}},\beta_{\mathrm{det}}>0.

The term
O^{0}_{\Lambda}D
is the older-baseline observable. The five tax
channels measure pressure-source cost, localization leakage, reproduction
drift, gate/slack violation, and detector-comparison mismatch.

Remark C.52

(Active weights)
.

If one of the displayed weights is zero, its channel is not part of the
zero-set detector unless a separate zero-set containment hypothesis is imposed.
The positivity assumption ensures that

M_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{tax}}(D)=0

forces the observable and every active tax channel to vanish.

Definition C.53

(Pressure/tax kernel)
.

The pressure/tax kernel is

\mathcal{K}_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{tax}}:=\{D\in\mathcal{A}_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{tax}}:M_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{tax}}(D)=0\}.

Equivalently, under the positivity convention in
Section

C.12.1
,

D\in\mathcal{K}_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{tax}}
if and only if

O^{0}_{\Lambda}D=0,

\mathsf{Tax}_{\mathrm{prs}}(D)=\mathsf{Tax}_{\mathrm{loc}}(D)=\mathsf{Tax}_{\mathrm{rep}}(D)=\mathsf{Tax}_{\mathrm{gs}}(D)=\mathsf{Tax}_{\mathrm{det}}(D)=0.

The kernel-free condition is

\mathcal{K}_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{tax}}\subset\Gamma_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}^{\mathrm{int}}.

Lemma C.54

(No coercivity without kernel-freeness)
.

Assume there is
H\in\mathcal{A}_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{tax}}
such that

H\notin\Gamma_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}^{\mathrm{int}},\qquad\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},0}(H,\Gamma_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}^{\mathrm{int}})>0,

and all active observation and tax channels vanish on the ray

\{\lambda H:\lambda\geq 0\}
. Assume also that this ray is contained in

\mathcal{A}_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{tax}}
and the baseline quotient distance is
homogeneous on it:

\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},0}(\lambda H,\Gamma_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}^{\mathrm{int}})=\lambda\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},0}(H,\Gamma_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}^{\mathrm{int}})\qquad(\lambda\geq 0).

Then no estimate of the form

M_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{tax}}(D)\geq\mu_{\Lambda}\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},0}(D,\Gamma_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}^{\mathrm{int}})

can hold on this class with
\mu_{\Lambda}>0
.

Proof.

For every
\lambda>0
, the channel-vanishing assumption gives

M_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{tax}}(\lambda H)=0.

The alleged coercive estimate would give

0\geq\mu_{\Lambda}\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},0}(\lambda H,\Gamma_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}^{\mathrm{int}})=\mu_{\Lambda}\lambda\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},0}(H,\Gamma_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}^{\mathrm{int}})>0,

a contradiction.
∎

C.12.2
Reduced finite-dimensional criterion

Definition C.55

(Reduced finite-window package model)
.

A reduced finite-window package model consists of a finite-dimensional coordinate
space
X_{N}
, a coordinate map

x:\mathcal{A}_{\Lambda,N}^{\mathrm{red}}\to X_{N},

a gauge subspace
G_{N}\subset X_{N}
representing

\Gamma_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}^{\mathrm{int}}
, and a finite-dimensional combined
detector/tax map

T_{\Lambda}:X_{N}\to Y_{N},

where

T_{\Lambda}x=\left(O^{0}_{\Lambda}x,T_{\mathrm{prs}}x,T_{\mathrm{loc}}x,T_{\mathrm{rep}}x,T_{\mathrm{gs}}x,T_{\mathrm{det}}x\right).

The model is compatible with the tax kernel if

M_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{tax}}(D)=0\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad T_{\Lambda}x(D)=0.

Theorem C.56

(Finite-dimensional matrix kernel criterion)
.

Let
\mathcal{A}_{\Lambda,N}^{\mathrm{red}}
be a reduced finite-dimensional
package class in the sense of
Section

C.12.2
. Assume

\ker T_{\Lambda}\subset G_{N}.

Then

\mathcal{K}_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{tax}}\cap\mathcal{A}_{\Lambda,N}^{\mathrm{red}}\subset\Gamma_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}^{\mathrm{int}}.

If additionally
G_{N}\subset\ker T_{\Lambda}
, then the reduced kernel-free
condition is equivalent to

\ker T_{\Lambda}=G_{N}.

Proof.

Let
D\in\mathcal{K}_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{tax}}\cap\mathcal{A}_{\Lambda,N}^{\mathrm{red}}
. Compatibility gives

T_{\Lambda}x(D)=0,

so
x(D)\in\ker T_{\Lambda}
. By hypothesis,
x(D)\in G_{N}
, which means
that
D
is an admissible gauge direction in the reduced model. This proves
kernel-freeness. If
G_{N}\subset\ker T_{\Lambda}
, the inclusion

\ker T_{\Lambda}\subset G_{N}
is exactly equivalent to equality.
∎

C.12.3
Zero-set rigidity

Definition C.57

(Channel zero sets)
.

Define

Z_{\mathrm{obs}}:=\{D:O^{0}_{\Lambda}D=0\},

Z_{\mathrm{prs}}:=\{D:\mathsf{Tax}_{\mathrm{prs}}(D)=0\},\qquad Z_{\mathrm{loc}}:=\{D:\mathsf{Tax}_{\mathrm{loc}}(D)=0\},

Z_{\mathrm{rep}}:=\{D:\mathsf{Tax}_{\mathrm{rep}}(D)=0\},

Z_{\mathrm{gs}}:=\{D:\mathsf{Tax}_{\mathrm{gs}}(D)=0\},

Z_{\mathrm{det}}:=\{D:\mathsf{Tax}_{\mathrm{det}}(D)=0\}.

Theorem C.58

(Zero-set rigidity criterion)
.

If

Z_{\mathrm{obs}}\cap Z_{\mathrm{prs}}\cap Z_{\mathrm{loc}}\cap Z_{\mathrm{rep}}\cap Z_{\mathrm{gs}}\cap Z_{\mathrm{det}}\subset\Gamma_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}^{\mathrm{int}},

then

\mathcal{K}_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{tax}}\subset\Gamma_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}^{\mathrm{int}}.

Proof.

If
D\in\mathcal{K}_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{tax}}
, then by

Section

C.12.1
the observable and all active tax channels vanish. Hence
