D\in Z_{\mathrm{obs}}\cap Z_{\mathrm{prs}}\cap Z_{\mathrm{loc}}\cap Z_{\mathrm{rep}}\cap Z_{\mathrm{gs}}\cap Z_{\mathrm{det}}.

The assumed zero-set intersection containment gives

D\in\Gamma_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}^{\mathrm{int}}
.
∎

C.12.4
Channelwise zero-set implications

Proposition C.59

(Pressure-source zero-set implication)
.

Assume there is
c_{\mathrm{prs}}>0
such that

\mathsf{Tax}_{\mathrm{prs}}(D)\geq c_{\mathrm{prs}}\left(\|C(D)\|+\|E_{F}(D)\|_{X_{\mathrm{src}}}+\|F^{\mathrm{act}}_{D}-F^{\mathrm{mod}}_{D}\|_{X_{\mathrm{src}}}+\|p_{D}^{\mathrm{act}}-p_{D}^{\mathrm{mod}}\|_{Y_{\mathrm{prs}}}\right).

If
\mathsf{Tax}_{\mathrm{prs}}(D)=0
, then

C(D)=0,\qquad E_{F}(D)=0,\qquad F^{\mathrm{act}}_{D}=F^{\mathrm{mod}}_{D},\qquad p_{D}^{\mathrm{act}}=p_{D}^{\mathrm{mod}}.

Proof.

The right-hand side is a sum of nonnegative terms multiplied by a positive
constant. If the left-hand side is zero, the sum is zero, and every term in the
sum must vanish.
∎

Proposition C.60

(Localization zero-set implication)
.

Assume there is
c_{\mathrm{loc}}>0
such that

\mathsf{Tax}_{\mathrm{loc}}(D)\geq c_{\mathrm{loc}}\left(\mathsf{Leak}_{\nabla u}(D)+\mathsf{Leak}_{u}(D)+\mathsf{Leak}_{p}(D)\right).

If
\mathsf{Tax}_{\mathrm{loc}}(D)=0
, then

\mathsf{Leak}_{\nabla u}(D)=\mathsf{Leak}_{u}(D)=\mathsf{Leak}_{p}(D)=0.

Proof.

The displayed lower bound is a positive multiple of a sum of nonnegative
leakage coordinates. Vanishing of the tax forces each leakage coordinate to
vanish.
∎

Proposition C.61

(Reproduction zero-set implication)
.

Assume that along a finite chain the reproduction tax dominates all active
coordinate drifts:

\mathsf{Tax}_{\mathrm{rep}}(\mathcal{D})\geq c_{\mathrm{rep}}\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\|D_{k+1}-\mathcal{R}_{k\to k+1}D_{k}\|_{\mathrm{act}}

for some
c_{\mathrm{rep}}>0
. If
\mathsf{Tax}_{\mathrm{rep}}(\mathcal{D})=0
, then

D_{k+1}=\mathcal{R}_{k\to k+1}D_{k}\qquad(0\leq k<K)

in all active coordinates.

Proof.

The proof is the same nonnegative-sum argument. If the tax vanishes, every
active drift norm in the finite sum vanishes.
∎

Proposition C.62

(Gate/slack zero-set implication)
.

Assume the gate/slack tax dominates

\sum_{a\in\mathfrak{A}}\left((B_{a}(D)-\tau_{a}(D))_{+}+|B_{a}(D)+s_{a}(D)-\tau_{a}(D)|\right)

with a positive constant. If
\mathsf{Tax}_{\mathrm{gs}}(D)=0
, then for every

a\in\mathfrak{A}
,

B_{a}(D)\leq\tau_{a}(D),\qquad B_{a}(D)+s_{a}(D)=\tau_{a}(D).

Proof.

Vanishing of the tax forces both nonnegative terms for each gate channel to
vanish. Thus
(B_{a}-\tau_{a})_{+}=0
, which is
B_{a}\leq\tau_{a}
, and

|B_{a}+s_{a}-\tau_{a}|=0
, which is the displayed identity.
∎

Proposition C.63

(Detector zero-set implication)
.

Assume the detector-comparison tax dominates the detector mismatch:

\mathsf{Tax}_{\mathrm{det}}(D)\geq c_{\mathrm{det}}^{0}\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{det}}(D;\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{0})

for some
c_{\mathrm{det}}^{0}>0
. If
\mathsf{Tax}_{\mathrm{det}}(D)=0
and

O^{0}_{\Lambda}D=0
, then

\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{det}}(D;\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{0})=0.

In any detector model where
O^{0}_{\Lambda}D=0
and

\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{det}}(D;\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{0})=0
imply that the clean and localized detector
coordinates have no active defect, the clean and localized detectors see no
active defect.

Proof.

The displayed domination gives
\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{det}}(D;\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{0})=0
when

\mathsf{Tax}_{\mathrm{det}}(D)=0
. The final statement is exactly the additional detector
model implication stated in the proposition.
∎

Theorem C.64

(Component zero-set rigidity implies kernel-freeness)
.

Assume all active detector/tax weights are positive. Assume the zero-tax
conditions imply the channel constraints in

Sections

C.12.4
,
C.12.4
,
C.12.4
,
C.12.4
and
C.12.4
.
Finally assume the combined constraints

O^{0}_{\Lambda}D=0,\qquad C(D)=0,\qquad E_{F}(D)=0,\qquad F_{D}^{\mathrm{act}}=F_{D}^{\mathrm{mod}},

\mathsf{Leak}_{\nabla u}(D)=\mathsf{Leak}_{u}(D)=\mathsf{Leak}_{p}(D)=0,

D_{k+1}=\mathcal{R}_{k\to k+1}D_{k},\qquad B_{a}(D)\leq\tau_{a}(D),\qquad B_{a}(D)+s_{a}(D)=\tau_{a}(D),

and

\mathsf{Err}_{\mathrm{det}}(D;\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{0})=0

jointly imply

D\in\Gamma_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}^{\mathrm{int}}.

Then

\mathcal{K}_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{tax}}\subset\Gamma_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}^{\mathrm{int}}.

Proof.

Let
D\in\mathcal{K}_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{tax}}
. Since all active weights are
positive,
O^{0}_{\Lambda}D=0
and every tax channel vanishes. The channelwise
zero-set implications give the displayed pressure, localization, reproduction,
gate/slack, and detector constraints. The combined zero-set rigidity assumption
then gives
D\in\Gamma_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}^{\mathrm{int}}
.
∎

C.12.5
Compact quotient coercivity

Theorem C.65

(Conditional compact quotient pressure/tax gap)
.

Assume:

(i)

the tax class is stable under quotient normalization: if

r=\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},0}(D,\Gamma_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}^{\mathrm{int}})>0
,
then a normalized representative
D/r
belongs to the class and has
distance
1
;

(ii)

the unit quotient sphere

S_{\Lambda,0}:=\{D\in\mathcal{A}_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{tax}}:\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},0}(D,\Gamma_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}^{\mathrm{int}})=1\}

is compact modulo gauge;

(iii)

M_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{tax}}
is lower semicontinuous on this
quotient;

(iv)

M_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{tax}}
is positively homogeneous under the
quotient normalization;

(v)

kernel-freeness holds:

\mathcal{K}_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{tax}}\subset\Gamma_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}^{\mathrm{int}}.

Then

\mu_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{tax}}:=\inf_{D\in S_{\Lambda,0}}M_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{tax}}(D)>0,

and for every
D\in\mathcal{A}_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{tax}}
,

M_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{tax}}(D)\geq\mu_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{tax}}\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},0}(D,\Gamma_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}^{\mathrm{int}}).

Proof.

If the infimum were zero, compactness of
S_{\Lambda,0}
modulo gauge and
lower semicontinuity would give
D_{*}\in S_{\Lambda,0}
with

M_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{tax}}(D_{*})=0.

Thus
D_{*}\in\mathcal{K}_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{tax}}
. Kernel-freeness gives

D_{*}\in\Gamma_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}^{\mathrm{int}},

which contradicts

\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},0}(D_{*},\Gamma_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}^{\mathrm{int}})=1.

Hence
\mu_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{tax}}>0
. If
D
has quotient distance

r=0
, the asserted inequality is immediate. If
r>0
, normalize to

\widehat{D}=D/r\in S_{\Lambda,0}
. Homogeneity gives

M_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{tax}}(D)=rM_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{tax}}(\widehat{D})\geq r\mu_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{tax}},

which is the desired estimate.
∎

Theorem C.66

(Additive-error tax coercivity)
.

Assume an ideal detector
\widetilde{M}_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{tax}}
satisfies

\widetilde{M}_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{tax}}(D)\geq\widetilde{\mu}_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{tax}}\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},0}(D,\Gamma_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}^{\mathrm{int}})

and the realized detector satisfies

M_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{tax}}(D)+\Delta_{\mathrm{model}}\geq\widetilde{M}_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{tax}}(D).

Then

M_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{tax}}(D)\geq\widetilde{\mu}_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{tax}}\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},0}(D,\Gamma_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}^{\mathrm{int}})-\Delta_{\mathrm{model}}.

Proof.

Rearrange the model comparison and insert the ideal coercivity estimate:

M_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{tax}}(D)\geq\widetilde{M}_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{tax}}(D)-\Delta_{\mathrm{model}}\geq\widetilde{\mu}_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{tax}}\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},0}(D,\Gamma_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}^{\mathrm{int}})-\Delta_{\mathrm{model}}.

∎

Corollary C.67

(Conditional tax-route detection)
.

Assume the additive-error tax coercivity estimate of

Theorem

C.66
. If the localized detector dominates the tax
detector in the sense that

M_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{loc}}(D)+\Delta_{\mathrm{det/tax}}\geq a_{\Lambda}M_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{tax}}(D)

with
a_{\Lambda}>0
, then

M_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{loc}}(D)\geq a_{\Lambda}\widetilde{\mu}_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{tax}}\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{loc},\mathrm{int},0}(D,\Gamma_{\Lambda,\mathrm{adm}}^{\mathrm{int}})-a_{\Lambda}\Delta_{\mathrm{model}}-\Delta_{\mathrm{det/tax}}.

Proof.

From the detector domination,

M_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{loc}}(D)\geq a_{\Lambda}M_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{tax}}(D)-\Delta_{\mathrm{det/tax}}.

Insert
Theorem

C.66
and distribute the factor

a_{\Lambda}
.
∎

Remark C.68

(Status)
.

This section proves fixed finite-window and reduced-model criteria for
pressure/tax kernel-freeness and the resulting compact quotient gap. It does
not prove kernel-freeness for all suitable weak solutions, scale-uniform
pressure/tax coercivity, regularity, singularity exclusion, or a Clay-problem
conclusion.

C.13
Scope of the finite-window theorem

The results assembled above close the fixed-window package-realizability,
clean-source compactness, effective projection, reduced pressure/tax
kernel-freeness, and compact quotient-coercivity steps needed by the conditional
local-to-clean transfer theorem. The conclusion remains a finite-window
detection statement. The following issues are structural inputs or directions
outside the conclusion proved here.

(i)

A concrete detector model must verify the observable and tax-channel
assumptions
Sections

C.5
and
C.5
.

(ii)

The clean gap, chart visibility inequality, and
component-to-baseline comparison are imported hypotheses for the transfer
theorem; they are not derived from the package-realizability theorem.

(iii)

The compactness and effective-projection criteria are sufficient
finite-window criteria. The paper does not assert that arbitrary suitable
weak solutions automatically generate compact clean-source families.

(iv)

The reduced pressure/tax criteria give usable finite-window
kernel-free tests. The paper does not assert global pressure/tax
kernel-freeness for all localized Navier–Stokes packages.

(v)

No scale-uniform propagation, infinite-chain iteration, regularity
theorem, singularity-exclusion theorem, or Clay-problem conclusion follows
from the finite-window result alone.

C.14
Summary of auxiliary proof modules

The detailed proof above establishes the following auxiliary modules used in the finite-window assembly.

Step 1.

The finite-window detector comparison theorem is proved from the two
detector-intertwining assumptions.

Step 2.

The weighted detector-comparison corollary and the channelwise
sufficiency criterion for detector mismatch control are proved.

Step 3.

Unassigned detector channels are isolated in

\Delta_{\mathrm{det}}^{\mathrm{rem}}
.

Step 4.

The conditional finite-window local-to-clean transfer theorem is
proved by combining detector comparison with the clean gap, chart
visibility, and weighted component comparison.

Step 5.

The finite-window detection threshold corollary is proved.

Step 6.

The package-realizability theorem shows that local
pressure-admissible Navier–Stokes data generate the package coordinates.

Step 7.

Clean pressure-image compactness is proved to imply uniform projection
tail convergence.

Step 8.

Source compactness, finite-dimensional source models, strong
clean-coordinate compactness, Sobolev compactness, and Kolmogorov–Riesz
translation compactness are proved to be sufficient compactness criteria.

Step 9.

Effective projection bounds are shown to replace compactness in the
finite-window transfer error.

Step 10.

Reduced finite-window pressure/tax kernel-freeness criteria are proved
through matrix and zero-set rigidity conditions.

Step 11.

Compact quotient pressure/tax coercivity is proved under
kernel-freeness, quotient compactness, lower semicontinuity, and
homogeneity.
